Cash prizes, free to enter.") came off the /spar gate and searching rail (`openNudgeHtml` neutralised to `return ''`, kept as a no-op restore path): redundant with the sitewide topbar ribbon and part of what pushed the sign-in buttons down. The sitewide ribbon stays. Commit `a7f95e35`, SW v2811.

- **Four builds in one push: a text lane, the audience's own answer read back to it, a quote-verified clash map, and replays that announce themselves** (2026-08-23, Aidan asked for all four areas at once: "1, 2, 3, and 4 - do all"). Shipped as four commits so each can be reverted alone.

  **SMS, built to switch on with an env var rather than a deploy.** The 2026-08-22 entry says SMS "is deliberately NOT built" because the Twilio account is Aidan's to create. Everything up to that boundary now exists and no-ops cleanly until `TWILIO_*` is set, the same posture webpush held while VAPID was missing. **The consent machinery is the design, not paperwork around it:** `sendSms` is deliberately NOT exported from `lib/sms.mjs`, so a future call site cannot text a user without passing verification, opt-out, per-kind switches, a daily cap and quiet hours; the guard asserts the non-export. A number is proved by code round trip, because typing a number proves nothing about owning it and without the round trip this is a free anonymous texting service pointed at whoever you like. Named accounts only, for the reason anonymous uids are refused everywhere else. `/api/sms-inbound` verifies the Twilio signature and **fails closed with no token**, because it mutates somebody's settings keyed on a phone number in the request body. Wired into go-live (its own opt-in list, capped at 60 recipients since a text fan-out is a bill), challenges (force:true skips quiet hours; being called out by name is the one alert worth a late buzz) and DMs (never carries the message text). **ACTION for Aidan: create the Twilio account, buy a number, register A2P, set the env vars.** The /settings card renders itself the moment the server reports configured.

  **The audience finally sees its own answer.** Spectators have answered "where do you stand" before and after rounds for weeks and it went to the server and stopped: `/api/persuasion-index` was fully built, public-safe, and had **zero callers**. A "How the room moved" panel now renders under the ballot for debaters and spectators. Three rules it inherits: only AFTER the ballot, because a live tally tells people what to think and then measures the conformity; percentages only when the SERVER says the sample is big enough, counts and an explicit "anecdote, not a measurement" below that; and the server's caveats rendered rather than filtered. **The bug underneath: every /spectate closing vote had been silently discarded** since that page shipped. The server weights a stance by visible watch time and drops anything under 90 seconds; spectate.html never tracked or sent `watchMs`, so `readWatchMs(undefined)` read 0 and every vote from that page landed in `lowWatch` instead of the tally. It measures visible time now, the way live-round does.

  **The flow becomes a real clash map.** "See the flow" re-read the per-speech notes, an index of what was SAID. Now it draws what was said, what the other side DID with it, and the verbatim line proving both. `lib/clash-map.mjs` was generalized past the async three-turn shape (which hardcodes `TURN_SIDE {1:prop,2:opp,3:prop}`) to N speeches across two benches, **sharing the verification gate rather than copying it**, because the gate is the only reason this belongs near a judged surface. New `/api/clash-map` draws it server-side deliberately: the gate checks every quote against the transcript, and running it in the browser would let a client publish rows the gate never saw. The haystacks are built server-side from the same text sent to the model, so a caller cannot widen what counts as "in the transcript". Drawn once, synced on the round doc (the rfdDeep pattern); falls back to the notes index when pending or failed, so the button is never dead. Advisory on the card, never a winner, never a score, ballot never reads it. **`scripts/test-clash-live.mjs` (31 assertions) in the hook, and its own history is the lesson:** four mutations were applied one at a time and the fourth ESCAPED. "One bad row kills the whole map" passed, because every bad row in the suite failed at the QUOTE check and none took the FIELD-check branch. Two assertions were added for that branch and it is caught now. A guard that passes on its first run has not been shown to test anything, and the specific trap is that a suite can exercise one rejection path thoroughly while leaving a neighbouring one untouched.

  **Recordings: two of the reasons /watch has no inventory.** A round that WAS recorded never told its debaters so, and they are the only people who could share it. One honest line in the ballot actions now says the replay is coming and states the processing delay rather than linking a URL that would 404 for half an hour; a round where consent was withdrawn says plainly that the file is deleted instead. And `thumbnailUrl` was only ever written as a side effect of the first `/api/recording-thumb` request, which happens when a human scrolls past the card, so a freshly published replay had no thumbnail at exactly the moment it is most likely to be shared. The sync pass warms at most three per run, sequentially, swallowing failures. **NOT done and it needs Aidan: the biggest inventory gap is that nothing records unless somebody finds a small pill mid-round, and only the SECOND debater is ever prompted, after the first presses it.** Making that ask louder runs directly against the 2026-08-22 call that retired auto-capture because the recording scares people away, so it is a product decision rather than a patch.

- **The /spar sign-in card signs you in; the chooser stops being stacked on top of it** (2026-08-23, Aidan twice on the same screenshot, the second time: "i already told u to get rid of this pop up above the one below it"). **Reverses the "pop up sign ins earlier" half of the entry below, which shipped the same morning and lasted about four hours.** It went in two steps because the founder read it twice. Step one, early afternoon: `renderGate()` stopped calling `autoPopAuthModal()`, so the shared chooser no longer opened itself the moment the gate stood. Step two, this pass, because the complaint survived step one: the gate's OWN buttons still routed through `openAuthModal()`, so a click on a card button labelled "Continue with Google" was answered by a modal whose biggest button said the same three words. Same duplicate, one gesture later, which is why removing only the auto-pop read as nothing having changed. **The reasoning that holds whichever way the gate flips next: the modal and the card are the same offer.** Google over email over Google over email, so the pop bought no path that was not already on screen, and it covered the half the modal does not have — who is waiting live right now (`/api/spar-queue`, real, unpadded), that the camera is optional and Avatar mode exists, the under-18 line. A chooser that hides the reason to sign in is a worse door than the card underneath it. **What the buttons do now:** Google runs Google directly, with the anonymous-account linking and the popup-blocked redirect fallback that were already in the file for the no-modal path. Email opens a form ON the card (name, email, "Email me a sign-in link"), so the emailed path never needs the modal either. Name is collected rather than skipped because the person on the other bench reads it, and an emailed link with no name seats a blank. **The send is NOT reimplemented on the page.** `auth-modal.js` now exports `debatableSendSignInLink(email, name)`, factored out of its own submit handler, so both surfaces stash the same `debateos-emaillink-*` keys that `completeEmailLink()` reads back on the return trip and cannot drift apart; rejections carry a `userMessage` so a caller never renders a raw Firebase string. **This does not repeal the 2026-08-12 one-chooser rule**, which exists so no page grows a second auth implementation: the chooser is still the fallback inside both handlers for a page that loads without `auth-modal.js`, and it is still the only sign-in surface on every page that does not already show these choices — /watch, /live and /debate-chat were checked and none of them render their own Google button, so their modal is a chooser rather than a duplicate. **Rider that made this look half-fixed in testing: `.gate-guest` and `.gate-email` each set a `display`, which beats the UA's `[hidden]` rule**, so the inline form rendered before it was asked for and the button it replaces stayed on screen underneath it. Same trap as the /predict `[hidden]` guard. Verified on production signed out: no modal at load, no modal on either button, the form opens in place, both validation branches and the send's success and error branches exercised against a stubbed provider.

- **The alias becomes the name on every surface, and the bank grows to 9,216 pairs** (2026-08-23, Aidan off his own topbar chip: "on my founder account it still says aidan bc thats the startt of my email i think but it should sticvk weith just founda. - fix the name issues seriosily. add more names to the list too"). The chip was one instance of a fault with the same shape in about twenty places, and the anonymity sweep of 2026-08-22 had not touched any of them because they are code paths rather than copy. **The pattern: `forUser()` already returns a generated alias when nobody has chosen a name, so a guard of the form `if (id.chosen && id.name)` could only ever DOWNGRADE to the real identity.** `topbarName()` had exactly that, then fell to `displayName`, then to the email; a Google account carries a real name, so the bar read "Aidan" on the account whose entire point is that it does not. Four more copies of the identical guard were writing their result into documents other people read: /debate-chat (the queue doc AND the Daily video userName the opponent sees on the tile), /chat and /messages (frozen into `dm_threads` and every message), and `js/community-channels.js` (stamped on every channel message). **Worse were the sites that PREFERRED the real name**, where the alias was only the fallback: /partners published `u.displayName` into the shared team `memberInfo`; voice-debate wrote the account name onto the public leaderboard and onto the **public, indexable credential pages** at /verify and /registry; /open and /tournament registered entrants under their Google name; `index.html` put `displayName || email` into `live_debates` and `shared_cases`; and `live.html` published **the email local part** as a public handle in three places on `live_challenges`. Every one now resolves through one helper (`daPublicName`, plus `daPublicHandle` on live.html) and **no path retains a fallback to a real identity**: with the module missing they return a uid tail, which identifies nobody. **Eight pages that publish a name never loaded `js/public-identity.js` at all** and were relying on a `topbar.js` lazy-load race to define the thing they depend on; they load it themselves now. **The name bank goes 32x32 to 96x96, 1,024 pairs to 9,216**, globally spread per the audience rule, with every ADDED entry screened so it differs from every other by more than one letter (Kai/Kaia, Leila/Layla, Lina/Alina, Nia/Pia were all cut at that screen; three legacy pairs predate it and were left rather than churned). **ADJECTIVES and NOUNS are deliberately untouched**, so the @handle, which is the identifier-shaped half, stays put while display names gain the variety. **DISCONTINUITY, deliberate: the arrays are indexed by `hash % length`, so every account still on a GENERATED alias gets a new display name once.** Names already written onto rows (leaderboard entries, queue docs, messages) keep whatever they were stored with, so a person can briefly read as two names across surfaces; handles do not move, which is what anchors continuity. **Two sites were missed by the code sweep and caught only by loading the live page**, which is the habit worth keeping: `/tournaments` is `tournaments.html`, a DIFFERENT file from `tournament.html`, it is the page the announcement email actually points at, and its register call sent `user.displayName` straight into the public entrant field; and practice.html's leaderboard write used DBIdentity correctly and then fell back to a shortened real name. **Deliberately left alone:** /profile's own name editor and /practice's account panel, where showing which account you are signed into is the surface's whole job, and read-side renderers of already-stored names.

- **The audience can see a room is dead, and say so; a report is evidence, not a verdict** (2026-08-23, Aidan from a /watch screenshot of himself spectating a round nobody was in: "be able to report a round over or something / as audience"). Every LISTING has told a live round from a dead one since the ghost-room fix, off the seat heartbeat both debaters write every 30s (`lastSeenAt`, ~100s window in `watch-live.mjs`, `live.html`, `spar.html`). **Nothing told the person already inside the room.** The entire no-show apparatus built on 2026-08-11, `checkOpponentPresence` plus the "Looking for your debater" strip plus the no-show prompt with a way out, is gated `!isSpectator()` line for line, so a spectator who followed a direct link, or who was watching when both debaters closed their tabs, sat in front of "Speech 1 of 4" and a clock at 5:00 indefinitely. The listing filter cannot help them: a shared link is exactly the path the filter never runs on. **The strip reads the same heartbeat the listings trust**, one constant rather than a second opinion, so the page inside the room can never disagree with the list that offered it. **The report is gated on absence, not on numbers.** One viewer must never end a round two people are in, so the outcome turns on whether anyone with standing to object is present, read from the server's own copy of the beat and never from anything the caller sends: no fresh beat, the round is marked over on the first report, because nobody is there to object and the room is already invisible to every listing, so this only corrects what the page shows the people still looking at it; fresh beat, nothing closes, the report is counted, and the debaters see that the audience thinks it is finished. Same posture as the machine NSFW flag (2026-08-18) and the motion-change consent gate (2026-08-12), and the same sentence as the audience Topics tab: the crowd sets the agenda, it does not seize the round. **The close is reversible with no undo endpoint**, because an ending only stands while it is NEWER than the last seat beat: a debater who comes back falsifies it by beating, and a round that dies again gets the button again rather than an ending nobody filed for it. That comparison is why `endedAtMs` is a plain number beside the timestamp. **`status` is deliberately untouched** — it has many consumers and a wrong value there breaks the ballot path, so this writes its own fields. Any verified account may report, anonymous included, since watching is open to strangers by design and the gate is absence rather than identity; a seated debater is turned away, because they end their own round with their own controls and their own live heartbeat would be the thing blocking them. Seated debaters do get the half they never had: the no-show prompt covers an opponent who never arrives, the strip covers one who leaves mid-round. **Two guards copied deliberately from `checkOpponentPresence`, both learned the hard way there:** the Daily room outranks the doc beat (a debater whose heartbeat has not round-tripped is still visibly in the room, and calling that room empty over a live picture of them is the exact failure that guard exists for), and "no beat has ever landed" is only evidence once the viewer has been in the room long enough for one to have arrived. **Verified, and the verification earned its keep:** 19 cases over the state machine, then four deliberate mutations one at a time. Three were caught and the fourth was NOT, which found a real defect rather than a weak test — `endedAtMs` was being stored for a comparison the code never made, so a round that ended, resumed and died again would have shown a stale ending. Fixed, and the suite catches it now. Contrast measured on the composited fill in all three of this page's real themes (grey, crimson, light; "dark" is not one of them), worst 7.62.

- **The globe gets its 24h number back, and only that one** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "bring back the number of how many live in last 24 hrs yeah"). Partially reverses the 2026-08-22 caption cut, which went in two steps the same evening: first both padded figures (24h visits, Google sign-ups), then the remaining static line. The visit count returns; the sign-up figure stays gone, so the globe carries exactly one number instead of a stack of labels. Two things were kept from the version that was removed because both are load-bearing: the noun is **visits**, not people, since `presence-live` counts SESSIONS and a sid is per-tab and per-visit, so one person in two tabs is two and a return after lunch is two; and the element renders empty until the fetch answers, so a failed or slow `/api/presence-live` leaves the globe standing alone rather than printing a zero. `online24` still carries `PRESENCE_BASELINE`, so this is a declared liveness caption and not a measurement — the 2026-08-14 rule stands and it must never be quoted externally. Restoring was markup plus a setter and no new styles, because the `.story-globe-caption` CSS was deliberately left dormant when the markup went.

- **Availability actually rides the whole app: the pill was on half the pages, and could not mount on the rest** (2026-08-23, Aidan off a /watch screenshot: "the avaliable button should still be there and true - avaliable goes across the ENTIRE USAGE OF THE APP"). The auto-enlist entry from earlier the same day is correct about intent and was only ever half delivered, for two independent reasons. **(1) Coverage.** The Available pill, the DM bell and the background matcher all live in `js/notifications.js`, which was hand-included on 52 pages. `topbar.js` does NOT pull it, so "has the topbar" was not coverage, and on /watch and the rest of the un-included pages the pill simply was not there. This is the same trap as the 2026-08-14 track.js finding, on a different shared script; topbar.js now lazy-injects notifications.js the way it already injects auth-modal.js and audience-mode.js, and the module's own `__daNotificationsLoaded` guard makes the 52 existing includes harmless. **(2) The pill had no fallback mount.** `placePill` only ever looked for `.ui-topbar-right`, `.app-topbar-right` or `.bar-links`; with none present it retried for six seconds and gave up SILENTLY, where the bell falls back to a floating chip. So on any page with bespoke chrome the pill could never appear however the script arrived there. A floating fallback was added, and then immediately constrained, because floating is wrong wherever a bar already exists: /atlas carries its own fixed `.topbar` at z-index 1200 and /tournaments a sticky one, so a chip pinned top-right would paint over them, which is exactly the failure `placeBell`'s comment records for /live-round. **A page with its own bar now names where the pill goes** via `[data-da-pill-slot]` (`="end"` appends, for a slot that also holds the wordmark), added to /tournaments, /atlas, /oral-exam-prep and the two judge pages; /safety has no header at all and is the single page that genuinely floats. **Rider:** /newvoice and /room-judge were missing from `ON_ROUND`, so someone in a voice round or judging someone else's round stayed matchable and could be pulled into a second round. Harmless while the pill could not render there, and reachable the moment it could. **Verification limit, stated rather than implied:** the browser pane in this session ran hidden at a 0x0 viewport, which throttles `setInterval` to 1/sec and makes geometry, screenshots and CSSOM reads unreliable, so the floating chip's appearance was never seen. Mounting was verified live by DOM (pill inside `.topnav` on /tournaments, last child after brand and back link on /judge-integrity), the injected CSS was validated by evaluating the real concatenation offline, and the gate was verified by running the module's own regexes: on /watch the pill shows for any named account. It reads hidden in that pane only because the session there is anonymous, which is the standing named-account rule for live pairing.

- **Bet-on-who-wins is back, tokens only** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "bring back bet on who will win 'tokens for now'"). Partially reverses the 2026-08-22 "betting removed entirely" entry, along the seam that entry's own commit message named: "we can just encourage a fun game tbh for now." The fun game is `/predict`'s free points board, the judgment-backed economy (server-authoritative `predict.mjs`, pari-mutuel, settles only on `verdictSource:'server'`, voids and refunds on a split, points non-purchasable and non-redeemable). **What came back:** the `/predict` route serves again (both netlify.toml mirrors) and the green Bet tab returns to the rail in `topbar.js`, per the restore notes both files carried. **What stayed off, deliberately:** everything with money in it. `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` and `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` stay false; `/floor`, `/ladder`, `/bounties` and `/bet-on-your-words` stay 301'd; and predict.html itself was swept tokens-first, because the page as retired led with "Win the argument. Take the money.", a cash-rounds section, and a "Play for money" primary CTA that would have advertised a checkout the env flag refuses. The hero now leads with the points board, the cash section and the pot drawer ship `hidden` (markup kept as dormant history, the cash IIFE stands down when its section is hidden so nothing polls `/api/cash-round`), the legal drawer's bounty and cash rows came off, and the meta descriptions dropped the prize-pot half. A page-level `[hidden]{display:none!important}` guard rides along per the standing UA-hidden trap. The page keeps its noindex, its early-build feedback note, and stays out of the sitemap. **Restore path for the cash lanes, when that flips again:** remove the two `hidden` attributes, re-enable the cash IIFE guard, restore the legal rows from git history, flip the env flags, and write the entry.

- **Live pairing requires a Google or email account again, and the sign-in chooser pops on arrival** (2026-08-23, Aidan: "actually require google or gmail sign in to use the live omegle debating feature etc - pop up sign ins earlier - we need conversaion to be much better"). **Fifth flip on this gate (08-18 wall → 08-19 trial → 08-22 morning wall → 08-22 evening trial → this), and per the entry directly below, a close needs its own entry saying what changed. What changed: the founder's stated priority is conversion.** A guest who plays two rounds and bounces leaves no account, no way to reach them, and no record to come back to; sign-ups are now the number, so the wall moves back to the door. The trial's own conversion data was never read before this call, so this is a priority call, not a data call, and it is recorded as one. **What moved, the same three layers as the 08-22 morning close, restored by reverting `249bc8b1`:** `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 0 in `spar-pair.mjs` (env-overridable, refuse-before-Firestore-read, so the closed lane costs nothing; `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=2` in the Netlify env is still the no-deploy reopen switch) and to 0 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()`'s allowance-0 guard is live again, so a signed-out visitor meets the gate instead of being minted a guest; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create is back to `isNamedAccount()` (deployed). The gate keeps the `/api/spar-queue` live count. **The new half is the "pop up sign ins earlier" ask (RETIRED the same day — see the entry at the top of this log; the function is kept, uncalled, because this gate has flipped five times):** `renderGate()` calls `autoPopAuthModal()`, which opens the shared chooser (Google, emailed link, email/password) the moment the gate stands, once per browser session (`da-spar-auth-pop` in sessionStorage — a modal that re-opens on every render is a nag, and dismissing is an answer), with the gate card behind it as the fallback surface so closing the modal loses nothing. auth-modal.js is lazy-loaded by topbar.js, so the pop polls for `openAuthModal` up to ~6s and stands down if the gate disappears or the script never arrives. GA4: new `spar_auth_autopop` beside the existing `spar_gate_view` split. **Doorway pages swept in the same push**, including two spots the 08-22 morning close missed because they were authored later: /how-it-works' opponent-picker line (authored + `data-plain` variants both promised two guest live rounds) and /prep's free FAQ (visible + JSON-LD). /debate-online, /debate-strangers, /omegle-alternative and /how-it-works' FAQ + JSON-LD ride the revert. **Watch, from the door side:** `spar_gate_view` reason `no_session` and `spar_auto_autopop` against sign-ins, and queue joins against the trial era. If joins collapse without sign-ups rising, the tested fallback is still the env flip, but the next flip in EITHER direction should read the GA4 numbers this entry names before moving, because five flips in six days means the gate is being steered by mood rather than measurement.

- **The guest trial reopens, hours after it closed: free to try, then the sign-in ask** (2026-08-22. **CLOSED 2026-08-23 by the entry directly above: named accounts required again, the chooser pops on arrival.** Second call the same day, Aidan: "make it free to do the omegle feature without an account at first then encourage sign in"). **Reverses the morning's "Live debate requires sign-in again" entry below and restores the 2026-08-19 two-round shape. This is the fourth flip on this exact gate in five days (08-18 wall → 08-19 trial → 08-22 morning wall → 08-22 evening trial), so the standing posture is now written down: the 08-19 trial is the tested resting state, and the next close needs its own entry saying what changed, not a re-litigation.** What moved, the same three layers, back: `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 2 again in `spar-pair.mjs` (env-overridable; `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=0` in the Netlify env is the no-deploy kill switch, and the refuse-before-Firestore-read guard from the morning stays so the closed state costs nothing) and to 2 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()`'s allowance-0 guard goes inert, so a signed-out visitor is minted a guest identity and lands in the queue; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create widens back to `isSignedIn()` (deployed and verified against the live project). The refusal message is now conditional: a guest who spent the trial hears "your free guest rounds are used", and only the env-closed state says "live rounds need an account", so the wall never describes rounds nobody had. **What the morning's close leaves behind, kept on purpose because it serves the "encourage sign in" half:** the keyless `/api/spar-queue` live count and the gate's "N others are waiting live right now" line now decorate the POST-TRIAL gate, so the ask a spent guest meets has both "keep the rounds you just played" and a person waiting on the other side of it; the `used > 0` guard in `gateSpent()` stays; the doorway pages (/debate-online, /debate-strangers, /how-it-works, /omegle-alternative) are swept BACK to the guest-rounds copy, visible FAQ and JSON-LD together. The age-band gate shipped the same day composes cleanly: `/api/age-band` deliberately accepts anonymous uids and the attestation survives account linking, so a guest answers the age question once and it follows them into the account they later create. Everything else the 2026-08-18 sweep closed stays closed (/live seats, /partners, /debate-chat's own gate, the background pill, DMs). **Watch the number the 08-19 entry named:** guest matches against conversions at the spent wall, `spar_gate_view` reason `guest_rounds_spent` vs `no_session` in GA4.

- **De-vibe sweep: a full-site audit pass closes what earlier sweeps missed** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "analyze whats vibe coded from the code, design, usability etc and fix it"). Three parallel audits (copy/brand, design system, code correctness) over origin/main plus a live walk of the top pages. Fixed in one push (`8cf26917`, SW v2736): /spar's sign-in gate still rotated the DELETED face55 into view (a broken image on the first surface a signed-out visitor meets); `onboarding.js` loaded firestore-compat 10.5.0 under the same element id `notifications.js` uses for 10.13.2, so ten top pages raced on which SDK version they ran (watch.html was pinned to 10.5.0 too); **the 2026-08-22 anonymity sweep had missed report.html** (UChicago, UWC, "argued at the top of the country", "Built by a debater") and ambassadors.html ("a national-circuit champion") — all moved to the sanctioned "built from inside competitive debate", plus report.html's "moat against ChatGPT" zinger; tokens.html's contact address was the CANCELLED devilsadvocateteam mailbox on the one page with a live Stripe purchase; prediction-market-debate's nav + hero CTA pointed at /markets, which never existed; format-count selling ("fifteen formats") came off practice/debate-an-ai/online-debate-camp/prep per the standing rule (enumerated lists stay); judge-draft.js still carried five Crimson Pro stacks that rendered GEORGIA on /spar, and live-round's recording-board + share-poster canvases still drew 18 lines in Georgia/ui-serif that bake into published replays (now Inter/Archivo + Source Serif 4, the italic dropped); index.html's 6rem round timer asked for IBM Plex Mono, which nothing loads (now Geist Mono); live-round's #kickoffGo was light-green-on-pale-green on light theme (the #audFab trap class, third instance); the transcript-consent modal sat in a seven-way tie at z-index 2147483000 where a later-injected overlay could paint over a consent gate (lifted to ...050); ui.css's docblock still said the display face is Inter while the token deliberately leads DM Sans (5b69dd1c is newer; doc fixed, not the token). **Found and deliberately NOT changed, for Aidan:** two of the three published /watch replays show the founder's face with the name label "Aidan" baked into the video, which is the largest surviving anonymity leak and an unpublish decision, not a code edit; masterclass.html sells a $19/mo tier that exists in no canon (pricing is locked, so it needs a call, not a correction); /pricing's hero still leads "Prediction credits. Prize play" after betting came off the site the same day (positioning has flipped four times in four days, so it was left for an explicit call); index.html's purple/green action buttons and the ~211 inert Fraunces declarations are recorded in this entry's audit rather than swept, since each is a big-diff cosmetic pass. The rebase reproduced the sw.js dedupe trap AGAIN (origin landed on the same v2735 mid-flight; caught by the standing check, re-bumped to v2736 by hand).

- **The age band moves server-side: write-once on the account, and the matchmaker checks its own record** (2026-08-22, hours after the entry below shipped; Aidan chose the hardening when shown the gap). **Supersedes one paragraph of the entry below** — the one that says the band "is allowed to ride the client-written queue doc" because "both forgery directions reduce to lying about your age, which no storage location fixes." That argument missed the direction that matters: with a client-written field, an adult never has to lie at the door at all — attest adult on the account, flip `ageBand:'minor'` on the queue doc from devtools, enter the teen pool per round, flip back, no record anywhere. Two parallel sessions built this feature the same evening with different data models (the exact 2026-08-18 drop-in-queue failure shape); this pass RECONCILED them rather than shipping a second architecture: the shipped UX survives untouched (js/age-gate.js modal, `da-age-band` in localStorage synced by prefs-sync, the advisory `ageBand` on queue docs that client pre-filters and the waiting count read), and underneath it the answer now ALSO lands in **`age_bands/{uid}` via new `/api/age-band` — write-once, admin-SDK only (no firestore.rules entry, so the default deny covers it; no rules deploy needed)** — and `spar-pair.mjs` enforces from that record alone for BOTH sides of every proposed pair. A 409 `AGE_BAND_LOCKED` hands back the recorded band and clients adopt it, so an edited localStorage cannot even disagree with the record for long. **Semantics tightened with the source of truth:** both sides must now hold a server record before any pair is proposed (the entry below let adult pair with unattested); a caller without one gets 403 `AGE_BAND_REQUIRED`, which new clients SELF-HEAL by re-POSTing the stored answer (throttled), so everyone who answered the modal in the hours before this shipped converges silently — only a genuinely unanswered account sees the modal again. A peer without a record is skipPeer'd and heals on their own next attempt. **Transition cost, accepted:** a client on a stale SW cache that pairs before refreshing sees a pairing-trouble banner instead of a match until its cache turns over; the queue is thin and the window is hours. **What write-once buys for the schools conversation:** the CHSSA answer is now "the age answer is locked to the account and the matchmaker checks the server's own record for both people", and a false attestation is a deliberate, recorded, permanent act on the account rather than a field flip — an adult who bands as minor is also locked OUT of adult rounds. **Unchanged limits, still stated on /safety:** attestation is not verification, a fresh account can still lie once at the door, and the moderation layer stays the backstop. **The follow-up flips:** the /settings band control the entry below names is now the WRONG follow-up (a self-serve toggle would undo the point); a mis-click is the contact email, where a human changes the doc. Band read fails CLOSED per attempt (`age_check_failed`, soft) — the reverse of the guest lane's fail-open, because pairing across the line is the one outcome the layer exists to prevent.

- **Live pairing separates attested minors from adults** (2026-08-22, applying the CHSSA rejection's student-safety point; the copy half shipped the same day as /safety and the sign-up claim rewrite). The matchmaking pool had zero age awareness anywhere: a 13-year-old and an adult stranger genuinely shared one queue, on camera, and the terms' 13-17 supervision rule had nothing at the door enforcing it. Now everyone answers ONE age question before their first live queue join (js/age-gate.js, asked once ever, `da-age-band` in localStorage, synced across devices via prefs-sync), the answer rides every `matchmaking_queue` doc as `ageBand`, and **spar-pair.mjs refuses any pair where an attested minor would meet anyone who did not attest minor** (`age_mismatch`, a soft refusal like `blocked_peer`). One chokepoint covers /spar, /debate-chat, and the background "Spar live" pill; clients also pre-filter candidates so a minor's "N waiting" count is honest and no doomed proposals burn the poll loop. **The claim is worded as "never KNOWINGLY paired" on /safety, deliberately:** attestation is the ceiling of what a site can check without ID documents, both forgery directions reduce to lying about your age, and no storage location fixes that, which is why the band is allowed to ride the client-written queue doc (unlike the guest counter there is no metering incentive). **Accepted costs, stated:** an attested minor waits longer, possibly indefinitely, until other minors are in the pool — the AI surfaces stay open to them and the ask says so up front; a mis-click currently has no self-serve undo (the contact email is the recourse; a /settings control is the follow-up). **Deliberately NOT touched:** the Aug 29 tournament drop-in queue, where a pool split at a ~10-person field would strand every minor — the rules require parent or guardian permission for under-18 entrants and that stays the event's posture; revisit when fields are big enough to split.

This pass takes the pages too. What is gone: `/predict`, `/floor`, `/ladder` and `/bet-on-your-words` 301 to `/watch`, `/bounties` (and `/bounty`) 301 to `/tournaments`, all forced, both netlify.toml mirrors, `.html` forms included; `/bet-on-your-words` and `/bounties` leave the sitemap; and every remaining betting reference in live copy came off — `get-paid-to-debate.html` (prediction-board paragraphs, the Play Points FAQ entry, the gambling answer rewritten around the skill contest, visible copy and FAQPage JSON-LD moved together per the standing rule), `tokens.html`'s Play Points bullet, `season-1.html`'s foot note, and `everyone-has-an-opinion.html`'s cross-link. **The real-money lanes are switched off at the env layer:** `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` and `BOUNTY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` are `false` in the production context, so a hand-rolled POST cannot open a new cash round or bounty while the site says betting does not exist. Checked before flipping: `cash_rounds` and `bounties` hold ZERO documents, `/predict` took one bet ever (play points, anonymous), so nobody is owed anything and nothing strands. **What stays, and why:** `ENTRY_PAYMENTS_LIVE` stays true and every tournament surface is untouched — the Open is a prize contest, not betting, and it is the "fun game" money-intent traffic now routes to; `/payouts` (the Stripe Connect rail) stays reachable because it is how owed prize money reaches a winner, not a pitch; `/get-paid-to-debate` stays live and indexed as the tournament money page, now swept clean; the page files, functions (`predict.mjs`, `floor-*`, `value-market.mjs`, `cash-round*`, `bounty*`, `payout-account.mjs`, `lib/cash-round.mjs` and its pre-commit guard), Firestore rules and ledgers all stay in the repo as dormant history. **Restore path, if the positioning flips a fifth time:** re-add the nav rows (notes inline in topbar.js), revert the redirect blocks in both netlify.toml mirrors, re-add the two sitemap entries, and set the two env flags back to true. This is the fourth surfacing flip in four days and the first that removes the pages; per the note below, do not put betting back in permanent chrome without confirmation that the positioning is actually settled.

New `admin-open-rally.mjs` at `/api/admin/open-rally`, the same dry-run / confirm / batch / per-user-stamp posture as `admin-open-announce.mjs` with its OWN stamp (`openRallySentAt`), so the two campaigns can never block or double each other. Result: 1,572 Auth accounts, 259 with an email and no opt-out, 259 sent across 13 batches, 0 errors, run log at `config/open_rally_state`. Do not re-run it (the stamp makes a re-run a no-op anyway); the next campaign gets its own stamp field.

- **The landing goes light for everyone, the board goes real-stills-only, and the verdict card speaks plain English** (2026-08-22, Aidan off a screenshot of the dark first screen: "rethink this page, make it the light mode as well. change images used bc they look ai right now. use the real ones. and increase sizing of buttons / images to guide to the omegle featrue", followed mid-session by "really improve this page bc its confusing to read ... not bet against the pot type language. its the AI ballot etc (also dont use phrasing ballot explain it as decision justification or something)"). (4) **"Ballot" is swept from this page's reader-facing copy and JSON-LD**, ~50 strings, in favour of decision language ("the AI judge's written decision", "decides who won, and says why"); the one testimonial quote keeps its author's own words, and CSS class names / ids are untouched. The Debate CTA and the board column also grew (wrap 1200→1280, columns .82/1.18) so the stranger-match door and the two real faces are the biggest things on the screen. Commit `c9fb44ca`, SW v2730 after two rebases in one hour, the second of which reproduced the sw.js dedupe trap exactly as the AGENTS.md warning describes (origin landed on the same v2729; caught by the standing check, re-bumped to v2730 by hand). NOTE for future passes: this page's word for the judge's output is now "decision"; the rest of the site still says ballot and sweeping it is its own pass, per surface, not a blind sed.

- **"Get pinged when someone is live" was fully built and dark: two missing env vars lit it, and the waiter now gets the real numbers** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "lets setup a 'get a text when someone is live' feature where we text ppl / ping them when someone is live to match - and encourage the other peson to wait"). The whole chain already existed — `go-live.mjs` fan-out, `notify_prefs` opt-in, `push-subscribe`, the sw.js push handler, the /spar "Get pinged when someone goes live" card — and none of it had ever delivered a single notification, because `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` was never set in the Netlify env: `pushConfigured()` was false and every layer no-opped cleanly, exactly as its own comments promise. **18 people had already clicked Turn on into a void.** A fresh keypair is in the env now (the baked-in public key's private half never existed anywhere recoverable), and those 18 self-heal into real subscriptions on their next visit, because `daRegisterPush()` already runs on load for named users and previously had nothing to subscribe against. **Guests can broadcast too, and the gate that makes that safe is global, not per-uid.** Anonymous uids are free and unlimited to mint, so a per-uid cooldown on anonymous broadcasters is no cooldown at all; all anonymous callers share ONE 5-minute gate (`live_now/_anon_broadcast_meta`, claimed in a transaction, failing closed) on top of the per-uid 10-minute one, and a guest's notification text is fully server-constructed ("Someone is live on Debatable") so there is no name to spoof. Verified on production with minted-then-deleted anonymous accounts: first guest broadcast reached 18 recipients, the same guest hit the per-uid cooldown, a second fresh guest hit the global gate. One false negative on the way was a warm pre-deploy function instance answering with the old code minutes after the deploy activated; re-run before diagnosing. **Note the same-day sign-in reversal above makes the guest lane dormant on /spar** (guests cannot reach the queue at `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=0`), which is fine: every current joiner is named and broadcasts through the per-uid path, and the guest lane reactivates with the trial's env flip, already tested. **The waiter's side is honest numbers, not the invented stat.** The ask suggested "90% of matched rounds took 30 seconds or more"; measured over the 124 matched queue sits on record it is 58%, median wait 43s, 87% inside two minutes. The wait rail now says that ("Most matched rounds waited over 30 seconds before connecting, and nearly nine in ten matched inside two minutes. Leaving early is how rounds get missed."), and when the join broadcast lands it shows "We pinged N people who asked to be told when someone goes live. Give them a minute to arrive.", which is the strongest true reason to keep the tab open. The chime already existed (sparChime + daAlert); the rail copy carries it. `spar_match_found` / `spar_match_made` now carry `wait_sec` so the stat is recomputable from GA4 instead of a one-off Firestore read (the queue doc's `joinedAt` is a heartbeat, not a join time — do not derive waits from it), and new `spar_golive_pinged` records fan-out size against conversions. **SMS was the literal ask and is deliberately NOT built:** there is no Twilio anywhere in the repo, and the account, phone number, and A2P registration are Aidan's to create, not code. When it exists, the fan-out point is `go-live.mjs`, beside the push send. An email lane was considered and skipped: no Resend domain is currently verified (see the anonymity entry), so it would be dead on arrival.

- **Betting comes off the site again, hours after the Bet tab went green** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "for now remove betting from website"). Reverses the 2026-08-20 restore and this morning's green-pill entry directly below; same posture as the 2026-08-19 de-surfacing, and "for now" is again the operative phrase. What came off, all in `js/topbar.js` (the only chrome carrying betting; landing, /home and /leaderboard were checked and already clean, and the landing's Floor band has been `display:none` since 2026-07-31): the rail's green Bet tab (/predict), the Explore Bounties row (/bounties), and the Explore Money row (/get-paid-to-debate), which returned on 08-20 explicitly as part of the betting positioning and so leaves with it. Restore notes sit inline on all three rows; the `money` green-pill render branch and the ICONS entries stay as dormant code so restoring is re-adding one line per row. **What deliberately did NOT move:** the pages themselves (/predict, /bounties, /floor, /ladder, /get-paid-to-debate) stay live for direct links, matching every prior nav demotion; the cash-round, bounty and payout backends and their env flags are untouched (`CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE` etc. stay true, so a held link still works); tournament surfaces (Tournaments, Enter a tournament, the Open ribbon and modal) are prize events, not betting, and keep their entries; and the SEO cluster (/bet-on-your-words, /prediction-market-debate and their cross-links) is left as it was after the 08-19 pass. This is the third surfacing flip in four days (off 08-19, on 08-20, green 08-22 morning, off 08-22); whoever restores it next should note the cadence and confirm the positioning is actually settled before putting Bet back in permanent chrome. (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make the bet button on nav green actually, so it screams money" plus "3 sections minimally highlighted ... the past rounds like youtube thumbnails on the left, leaderboard in middle, and debate the AI on the right (use the image of the her movie)"). Two small coupled changes. (1) The rail's Bet link moves off the faint-red `hot` treatment onto a new `money` flag in `js/topbar.js`: a filled green pill (#16a34a, white text, green pulse dot), deliberately the ONLY green in the bar so money reads as money at a glance; red stays the brand-action color everywhere else, and `hot` stays available for a future headline tab. (2) The landing's three quick doors above the Full tour button (shipped as plain pill anchors earlier the same day, see the entry below) are now preview cards: `/landing-shot-live.jpg` with a video-style play badge for Watch past rounds, `/img/leaderboard-portraits.jpg` for The leaderboard, and the Her still for Debate an AI, with the film credit riding the image per the standing attribution rule. Hrefs, data-cta tags, and the hashchange gate-opener are untouched; under 700px the cards go horizontal (small thumb left, label right) so three tiles don't eat the phone fold.

- **Live debate requires sign-in again, and the gate shows who is waiting** (2026-08-22 morning. **REVERSED the same evening — see the guest-trial-reopens entry above: the two-round trial is back and the named-only layers below are re-widened. The /api/spar-queue live count and the gate survive as the post-trial wall.** Original entry, kept as the record: Aidan: "require google sign in for using live debate feature actually - i want sign ups, and say '4 others waiting live' or something before people sign up so they feel encouraged"). **Closes the 2026-08-19 two-round guest trial below and restores the 2026-08-18 named-account posture, ahead of a paid push.** The reasoning flipped with the job: the trial existed to hook organic omegle-intent visitors who would bounce off a wall; an ad push is paid traffic, and a guest who plays two rounds and bounces leaves nothing — no account, no way to reach them, no record to come back to. Sign-ups are the point of the spend, so the wall moves back to the door. **What moved, all three layers together:** `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` defaults to 0 in `spar-pair.mjs` (a guest is refused with `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` before any Firestore read, so the closed lane costs nothing) and to 0 in /spar's client mirror; `mintGuestSession()` refuses at allowance 0, so a signed-out visitor goes straight to the gate instead of being minted an anonymous session; `firestore.rules` `matchmaking_queue` create is back to `isNamedAccount()`. The guest machinery is KEPT dormant, not deleted: re-opening the trial is `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS=2` in the Netlify env plus the client constant plus widening the rules create — the three move together or guests get a doc nothing will pair. `gateSpent()` now also requires `used > 0`, because with the allowance at 0 an anonymous session minted by another page would otherwise be told "that was your last free round" about rounds it never had. **The new half: the gate is no longer a wall in front of an unknown room.** New keyless `/api/spar-queue` returns the REAL count of queue docs heartbeated in the last 120s (single-field range on joinedAt, status filtered in memory, shared-cached 15s, no floor, no padding — someone who signs in off "3 others are waiting live" has to actually meet somebody). The gate renders it with a green pulse above the sign-in buttons; when the queue is quiet it falls back to the last-24h visit figure from `/api/presence-live`, worded as visits per the globe-caption honesty rule, with the dot dimmed grey. GA4 `spar_gate_live_count` carries the waiting figure so conversion can be read against what the visitor was shown. **Doorway pages swept in the same push, both copies:** /debate-strangers, /how-it-works, /omegle-alternative and /debate-online all still promised guest rounds in visible FAQ and JSON-LD (the 2026-08-18 lesson: a product gate is not shipped until the pages advertising the ungated behaviour move too); /debate-online's hero "play a couple of rounds as a guest first" became "see who is waiting in the live queue". **Watch the same number as both prior entries, from the door side:** gate views (`spar_gate_view` reason `no_session`) against sign-ins. If the ad traffic bounces off the gate without converting, the answer per the 2026-08-18 entry is a better gate, and the trial's env flip is sitting there as the tested fallback.

- **The payout rail: cash rounds pay out through Stripe Connect, and "by hand" stops meaning what it meant** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "setup real money system for betting im ready i got lawyers to approve etc"). **First, the disambiguation, because "betting" names two things here and they sit on opposite sides of the line the cash-round entry below draws.** Real money has been moving on rounds since that entry: `CASH_ROUNDS_LIVE=true` in the production context, on `sk_live_`, alongside entry, bounty and tokens. So nothing needed switching on. What was actually missing was the half after the verdict, and the code said so in as many words: payouts were "paid by hand until Connect and KYC exist." **This is Connect and KYC existing.** The OTHER reading, spectators staking cash on somebody else's round, was NOT built and is still refused for the reasons in that entry; `/predict`, `/floor` and `/ladder` stay points-only. A verbal approval does not change that, because what makes third-party wagering lawful is a licensing posture rather than an opinion, and because we run the judge that would settle it. Aidan picked the payout rail from a three-way choice.

  **What "by hand" actually cost, and what replaced it.** Paying a winner meant obtaining their bank details out of band, which is a data-collection problem wearing a logistics problem's clothes. Now the winner onboards to a **Stripe Express** account at `/payouts`; Stripe collects the identity documents, the bank details and the tax form and runs the KYC itself. What comes back to us is an account id and two booleans, and a payout is one API call. We never see a bank number.

  **What deliberately did NOT change: who starts it.** The lib's decision 4 says money never moves on an AI ballot alone and an admin sends it; the endpoint comment framed the human as a stopgap until Connect existed. Those two readings conflict, and on real money the conservative one wins: `pay` now sends a genuine Stripe transfer instead of recording that somebody sent one, but an admin still presses it. **The rail makes paying one click. It does not make it automatic.** No cron was added. Escalating to auto-release is a separate decision and it needs an entry of its own.

  **Two independent double-send defences, because each alone leaves a window.** A Firestore transaction claims the payout under a status guard, so two concurrent callers cannot both reach Stripe; a deterministic idempotency key derived from the round id ALONE collapses a retry from a cold start onto the same transfer. The claim stops a race, the key stops a retry, and neither covers the other's case.

  **Failure leaves money owed, never lost.** A winner who has not onboarded is `blocked`, not failed: the round stays `settled`, the pot stays owed, and it sends when their account is ready. A Stripe refusal is `failed` and retryable. Nothing in this path voids a round, pays a loser, or clears a debt. The admin queue reads the winner's account state so a stuck round shows "WAITING ON WINNER" rather than refusing when Pay is pressed.

  **The lesson, and it is this repo's own habit catching a real hole.** `scripts/test-payout.mjs` (36 assertions) passed on its first run, which proves nothing, so seven deliberate mutations were applied one at a time. Six were caught. **The one that escaped was the most important guard in the file:** the idempotency assertion compared two calls to each other, and two calls land in the same millisecond, so a `Date.now()` mixed into the key still compared equal. It is pinned to the literal string now, which is the only form that catches a clock, a counter or a random. A guard on the double-send defence that does not detect a clock is worse than no guard, because it is read as coverage.

  **Honest limits, stated rather than discovered later.** Stripe cannot pay out to every country, and this audience is global; an unsupported country is now named to the user instead of retried forever, but it does mean cash rounds are unavailable to some winners and that is a real ceiling. Buy-in limits are UNTOUCHED at $50: that is a blast-radius limit, and raising it belongs in its own change after this rail has moved real money at least once. And the rail has not yet paid a live winner, so it is verified by its guards and by a deployed endpoint, not by a completed payout.

- **Real people top the leaderboard: the seed bank re-scored below them, and the landing gets a leaderboard teaser of real names only** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "advertise the leaderboard here ... use the real ppl on the leaderbaord though"). Two coupled changes. (1) A minimal strip under the landing first screen (above the Load more card, deliberately not in the one-pager hide list) renders the top real, named people and links to /leaderboard. It reads a new `realRows` field on `/api/leaderboard-top` that excludes the seed bank and unnamed rows SERVER-SIDE, and the strip ships hidden until at least three such rows exist, so the surface can never invent texture; the API cache key moved to `leaderboard-top-v2` because the shared cache survives deploys and would have served the old payload shape for a TTL. Do not wire the teaser to the mixed `rows` field. (2) **The 48 seed docs were re-scored in place, order preserved, from 67.2-97 down to a 46-60.5 band**, below the top real scores. This is not a reversal of the 2026-07-18 seeding decision; it restores its stated intent. That entry seeded at 26.3-29.5 on the 30 scale precisely so "a real 29.6 tops the board", and the 2026-08-18 scale migration mapped the seeds to 65-97 while real rounds score 31-84, which quietly buried every real person under the bank on /leaderboard, the tour's ranked band, and the API. Each doc's pre-demote score is stashed at `scorePreDemote` for revert; seeds keep their "Example" label on /leaderboard. Verified live after: the top 12 of the collection by score are all real entries. Note the /leaderboard LIVE tab is still seeds-only, since every real entry today is voice-kind; that changes as live rounds land, not by code. **The teaser half was RETIRED the same day** (Aidan, off a screenshot of the strip: remove it). The landing now carries three quick doors above the Full tour button instead (watch past rounds, the leaderboard, debate an AI), each a hash anchor that deep-links into the gated tour through the existing hashchange opener; the leaderboard door lands on #ranked-band, where the re-score above already puts real people on top. Change (2) stands in full. `realRows` on /api/leaderboard-top now has no consumer but stays as built: it is one loop over an already-fetched snapshot, and it is the correct field for any future real-names-only surface. Do not build one on the mixed `rows` field, and do not re-inline a board strip under the first screen (tombstone comment at the old spot in landing.html says the same).

- **Mission Control re-cut on the priority stack, the drain holes closed, and unfinished rounds finally get a WHY** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "redesign the admin page to match the priorities of the company and what stats matter, also setup system to avoid anyone token draining us, and gather notes of users experiences, esp unfinished rounds, to capture why"). Three coupled changes, one push.

  **(1) /admin leads with the numbers the company runs on.** The KPI strip led with lifetime vanity totals (cases generated, forum posts, teams, shared cases): counts of what exists, not of whether it is working. New `/api/admin/northstar` renders the strip on the 2026-08 stack instead: named accounts (Auth-derived, spark from creation dates), activation (named accounts that ever finished a round; 31/215 is the whole retention story), rounds finished by kind (voice is the product ~9x and the split is the point), spar match rate (17% of queue joins matched in the August window; liquidity is the activation leak), bets in the window against all-time (the pivot's watch number; 766 markets settled against ONE bet ever, an anon on Aug 6, pre-pivot), and the Open's field with days out. New seventh workspace **Money** (key 2, priority order): the betting scoreboard (markets, wallets, bets, cash rounds, latest bets verbatim), the Open's entry counters straight off the tournament docs, and the Cost guard. `admin-signin-errors.mjs`, built in May with its own header calling sign-in the second-biggest funnel leak and ZERO consumers since, finally gets a card. New attention rules: match rate under 25%, zero bets in the window, and both cost-posture flags below.

  **(2) The cost guard is now a system, not a hope.** An audit of every provider-spending endpoint found the 2026-08-19 standing rule ("identify the caller through lib/caller.mjs, meter through lib/rate-limit.mjs") had five compliant endpoints and TWENTY module-scope Map counters still on money paths, plus five real holes, all closed: `docs-agent.mjs` was an effectively open Anthropic relay (read client-settable x-forwarded-for FIRST, so rotating one header minted a fresh bucket per request; now callerIp + shared layers, and the App Check catch fails closed for non-extension origins); `fact-check.mjs` and `perplexity.mjs` handed the per-user lane to ANY verified token, and anonymous uids are free to mint, so rotation bought unlimited Perplexity sonar-pro + Sonnet (both now require isNamedAccount for the uid lane, anon falls to the IP lane, App Check added); `brain-health.mjs` ran six provider probes per unauthenticated cache miss with no lock (per-IP miss metering + in-flight dedupe + stale-serve); `live-judge.mjs` ran a multi-juror Anthropic panel with zero rate limiting (per-uid 20/hr named, anon 6/hr + IP backstop, sized generously for Aug 29); `recording-thumb.mjs` re-ran a 16MB Daily download + ffmpeg per request on its soft-fail path (per-IP limit + the CDN cache header the success path already set). **And the guards became visible:** every deny in `lib/rate-limit.mjs` now increments `abuse_daily/{day}` (buffered, at most one write per isolate per minute, lazy import, failure swallowed — visibility must never take the request down), `claude.mjs` counts its sign-in wall there too, and `/api/admin/abuse` renders it with the posture flags that matter: **UPSTASH_* is still unset, so every limiter on the site is per-isolate memory — the lottery the lib's own header warns about — and that now shouts from the attention strip instead of a console.warn nobody reads. Setting the two env vars is the single highest-leverage drain fix left and needs no deploy.**

  **(3) Unfinished rounds now say why.** The round funnel could say WHERE a round died and nothing anywhere recorded WHY. Two first-class events (`round_abandoned`, `round_exit_reason`, allowlisted anon because two thirds of round activity is anonymous) and a shared widget (`js/round-exit.js`: one-tap reason chips + optional 240-char note + skip, explicit dark styling because theme tokens on unknown surfaces is how two invisible-text bugs shipped). Passive beacons on all four round surfaces fire on pagehide mid-round with the state that matters: practice carries `ai_loading` and `stuck_shown`, so the "they close the tab during the silent AI wait" hypothesis the stuck-speech watchdog comment names is finally measurable; live carries speech index, elapsed and whether the opponent ever arrived; voice reuses stop()'s own counters via a `funnel('abandon')` step. Bail buttons whose reason is knowable record it without asking (no-show "Find another" → opponent_no_show, forfeit → forfeit_next); the one active prompt sits on /spar's cancelQueue, the biggest leak, and can never trap the navigation (answer, skip, or 30s of silence all resolve). `/api/admin/round-exits` aggregates both and renders the free-text notes VERBATIM on the new "Why rounds die" card, because "tech_problem" is a chip and "mic died when my opponent joined" is a fix. The un-instrumented confirms on live-round (end-without-timer, no-transcript, kickoff dismiss, forfeit declined) all got events too — a user hitting those dialogs is telling us something broke, and neither outcome was recorded.

  **Recode-from-notes is the loop, not this entry:** the card fills as real exits land; read the reason mix and the notes before guessing at the next retention fix, and treat a spike of `ai_loading` beacons as the AI-wait leak asking for a streaming/progress fix on /practice.

- **AI-use reports get a one-tap door and a transcript screen; the resolution gets a visible Change control** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "make it more easy to change resolution and to report opponent for using AI. setup a system to detect AI use"). Three coupled changes on /live-round. **(1) The resolution band carries its own ✎ Change and 🎲 controls** instead of hiding the editor behind Options ▾: same inline editor, same both-debaters consent flow from 2026-08-12, same speech-1 lock, one click closer. The buttons hide for spectators and disappear when the motion locks (synced off the timer-transition hook, so the off-side agrees). **(2) Reporting AI use is one tap:** a 🤖 AI? button on the opponent card opens the existing safety modal with the new `ai_use` reason preselected, and the modal states the posture in as many words before anyone sends: the screen is advisory, a human reviews, the judge never sees it. **(3) The detection system, and its fences are the design.** New pure `lib/ai-use.mjs` + a report-triggered path in `report-user.mjs`: on an `ai_use` report from a round both parties actually debated (checked against the round doc's seats, server-side), the accused bench's transcript gets a mechanical heuristic pass (markdown/citation remnants in a "spoken" transcript, AI self-reference, more words than the clock allows — ASR never emits any of these) plus one fenced Sonnet read, combined into none/weak/moderate/strong and attached to the `safety_reports` record as `aiAnalysis`. **The fences, all pinned by `scripts/test-ai-use.mjs` (49 assertions, in the pre-commit hook):** a machine read is EVIDENCE for the human reviewer, never a verdict — no strike, ban, or eject path exists off it (the 2026-08-18 machine-flag posture, applied to conduct rather than safety); **style can never convict** — a model verdict of 'strong' is capped at 'moderate' unless the heuristics found a mechanical artifact, because fluent structured speech is what practice produces and what second-language debaters get accused over; the AI judge never sees the analysis (it rides safety_reports and stops there — an accusation with a thumb on the scale is what the charter forbids); Policy spread pace is explicitly not a signal; and a rate-limited or failed screen still FILES the report with the skip on the record, because human review must never be blocked by the machine's meter. The reporter gets the verdict back, calibrated honestly ("no clear AI signal; a human still reviews") but never the raw evidence list — naming the accused's phrasing back to the accuser mid-round invites a confrontation the reviewer has not had yet. Detection is report-triggered only, never a cron and never per-speech: one metered Sonnet call per report (4/hour, 10/day per reporter through lib/rate-limit) is the whole bill.

- **Round notes: the AI flows every speech for both sides, and the live panel simplifies around them** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "setup a system where you can see AI notes of the other sides arguments to help you get ready for your response. other side can see that as a bit of an aide. but simplify how we organize the experience of debating"). After each finished speech on /live-round, the acting debater's client (the same election the floor judge uses; it holds the freshest transcript) asks Claude for 2 to 4 structured notes on that speech: a short argument tag, one sentence of what was actually argued, and one sentence naming what a reply has to engage. They land on the round doc (`roundNotes`, arrayUnion rows like `factChecks`, `atMs` not serverTimestamp because serverTimestamp is illegal inside arrays) and sync to every client. **Symmetry is the fairness argument and it is why the feature is safe to ship on a judged surface:** both debaters and every spectator read the IDENTICAL notes, so it can never be one side's coach; the prompt names the point of clash and is told never to supply the counter-argument, never call a winner, never score, and the ballot never reads the field (same posture as the fact check and the clash map, whose quote-gate discipline this inherits in lighter form: only arguments actually made, invent nothing). Skipped speeches, sub-30-word speeches, and the final speech generate nothing (the ballot covers the last one). Fails soft; a device can hide the card with one tap (`lr-round-notes`), and hiding is per-device, generation continues for everyone else. Cost: one Sonnet call per non-final speech, roughly the floor judge's footprint, on by default where the floor judge stays flagged off. **The simplification is the second half of the ask:** the standing chrome that used to sit above and below the clock at all times, the two-card roster, the 4-team roster, and the full speech-sequence strip, folded behind one collapsed "Round details" line at the bottom of the panel. Every id and renderer inside is untouched, so `renderRoster` and the sequence painter write into the same nodes; the existing `body.speech-live` collapse still hides the roster during a speech. Between speeches the panel now reads motion, clock, transcript, notes, which is the moment the notes exist for: the person about to speak is looking at the AI's read of exactly the speech they have to answer. Verified in a browser against the worktree (real renderer run with injected rows: latest speech open, older collapsed, HTML-escaped, both themes on tokens) and live after deploy. Watch `live_round_notes` in GA4 against speech counts; if debaters hide it more than they open old speeches, the card is furniture and should get quieter, not louder.

- **Second dry run of the Open's day, seven days out: 19 checks pass, one real hole found and closed** (2026-08-22). The entry path changed shape today (free entry, eligibility = the 18+ attestation, tips), so the whole day was driven again through the REAL production endpoints against an isolated throwaway tournament with minted test accounts, per the 2026-08-20 method, then torn down to zero. **What held:** register without the box enters a competitor at `prizeEligible:false`; re-registering with the box upgrades the same entry in place; a later call without the box does NOT downgrade it; the tip checkout mints a Stripe session and a recorded tip refuses a second ask with `ALREADY_PAID`; `running` keeps entries open; the drop-in queue pairs two ready entrants into a `d`-keyed room and refuses an anonymous token; `break` closes both entries and tips. **What did not hold: `register` itself accepted an anonymous token, and granted it `prizeEligible:true` on the client-sent flag.** The queue one door later refuses the same token, the client never sends it (withUser gates on a real sign-in), and that gap between layers is exactly the 2026-08-18 shape: the visible surface gates, the endpoint does not, and nothing errors. Anonymous uids are free and unlimited to mint, `entryCount` renders in the hero as "N debaters have signed up", and the cash-eligibility list should never hold an unaccountable uid, so register now refuses anonymous with the same 403 the queue uses. Closed same hour, re-verified against production: named register still passes, anonymous gets 403 at the door. **The standing habit this re-proves: a changed entry path gets a full day-path dry run before the day, because both dry runs to date have found something silent.**

- **The stranger vertical is the product, so the front door says it and four pages stop denying it** (2026-08-22, the founder: "the basic product vertical is debate omegle, really emphasize, direct user, have seo lean that way"). **The measurement changes what "lean the SEO that way" means, so it goes first.** In Search Console the omegle head terms are already WON and they are SMALL: `debate omegle` position 1.6 on 55% CTR, `debate strangers` 3.6 on 72%, `omegle for debates` 1.8, and the whole niche is ~300 impressions in 28 days. There is no ranking headroom left to take. So leaning that way cannot mean chasing those queries; it means CONVERTING the traffic already won and taking the page-2 siblings at positions 11 to 17. **The front door was the leak.** Everyone gets the stranger arm of the first screen (the A/B was called 2026-07-27 and forced), and that arm has carried its h1 screen-reader-only since 2026-08-19, so the visible page said the product in one four-letter button: a visitor arriving on the query the site ranks FIRST for met a page that never used the word. The headline is visible again with one supporting line, which is precisely the fix the 8c9255de revert named on its way out ("a headline added back to THIS composition rather than a re-revert of the whole pass") rather than a re-litigation of the layout. It is sized off VIEWPORT HEIGHT the way `--fs-globe` and `--fs-rhythm` already are, because the screen is pinned to 100svh and a headline can only cost scroll; measured at 32-800 at both 1280x800 and 960x800 with the globe whole, which only fits because 25ebec32 had just taken the Open card off that column. The primary button is deliberately still "Debate": "Debate a stranger" wrapped the CTA pair to two rows at 941px, which the CSS comment there names as a hard constraint, and the headline above it carries the noun. **Four pages were telling this exact traffic there is a wall that is not there.** The 2026-08-18 named-account sweep put "a live round takes a free account" on `/debate-strangers`, `/omegle-alternative`, `/how-it-works` and `/prep`, and the 2026-08-19 guest-rounds reversal made every one of them false. The pages built to catch "omegle alternative" were answering the account question with the old gate. All four now say the first rounds need no account; `/prep` was additionally still quoting five anonymous AI rounds against an `ANON_LIMIT` of 1. **The page-2 siblings got real answers, not keyword shims:** `anonymous debate online` (pos 15.7) had ZERO coverage anywhere on the site and `debate random people` (11.8) sat on one page, so `/debate-strangers` gained two FAQ entries in the searcher's own words (Avatar mode plus the guest lane; the queue mechanics), with the visible copy and the FAQPage JSON-LD diffed question-by-question afterwards and matching in order, 14 to 14. `/online-debate-platforms` takes 345 impressions for 14 clicks, which is a snippet problem rather than a ranking one, so its title and description now lead with the intent it actually ranks for. `/debate-online` wins those queries outright and opened its h1 on the leaderboard; the h1 now opens on the stranger and its title and meta description are deliberately untouched, because they are what is winning. Nav: the desktop rail carries Explore only, so the LINKS label is what the MOBILE SHEET shows, and "LIVE" named a state next to /watch, /spectate and /live; it is "Debate live", and the Explore spotlight titles itself "Debate a stranger". **Standing note for the next agent handed "grow organic": do not spend another pass on the omegle head terms.** They are at position 1-5 with nothing left to gain. The untapped volume is the NSDA topics cluster at positions 34-61, which is seasonal and peaks now.
  - **Found in passing and NOT fixed: `debateprep.com` serves the landing page, not `prep.html`.** The 2026-07-23 keyword front door is a `status = 200` rewrite from `https://debateprep.com/` to `/prep.html` in both netlify.toml mirrors, and the live domain 301s and lands on `/` instead, so the one unique page that door exists to serve is unreachable and `/prep` on the canonical host 301s away to it. That is a redirect-topology question rather than a copy one, so it is recorded here rather than guessed at. **CLOSED the same day by the entry directly below.**

- **prep.html gets a URL that resolves, and the keyword domain is recorded as never having been connected** (2026-08-22, closing the note directly above). The config was never wrong. The host move it was written for never happened, and nothing on the site was in a position to notice.

  **Three things measured before touching anything, because the note above declined to guess.** The apex answers on GoDaddy **Domain Forwarding** (`15.197.225.128` / `3.33.251.168`, AWS-hosted, `x-request-id`, and a `405` on `HEAD` that is not Netlify), which 301s to itsdebatable.com **before Netlify ever sees the request**, so no host-scoped rule could have matched no matter how it was ordered. `debateprep.com` is **not a Netlify domain alias** (`getSite` lists only itsdebatable, debateai and debatethedevil), so `www.debateprep.com` reaches Netlify's edge and is served the wildcard `*.netlify.app` cert: there has never been a certificate for this host. And the ordering theory is **disproved**: rules 1 to 29 are all scoped to other hosts, and inside the block the apex exact-match and the asset passthroughs correctly precede the catch-all. The rules are byte-identical across both mirrors.

  **The repo had already diagnosed this correctly and then waited a month.** `sitemap.mjs` carries a STAGED note from 2026-07-23 withholding the sitemap entry for exactly the right reason, and `app/netlify.toml` says in as many words that the host "was previously on GoDaddy Domain Forwarding" and that "moving the host onto Netlify is what fixes that." That move was never made. **The DNS has since moved further away, not closer:** the staging note recorded a correct `75.2.60.5` sitting in the zone beside the parking records, and that record is now gone entirely, leaving only forwarding.

  **The one genuine defect, and it is not the DNS.** `/prep` and `/prep.html` on the canonical host 301'd to the keyword domain, so a misconfigured second host made the page reachable at **no public URL at all**. A keyword domain is a convenience; it must never be able to take the page down everywhere. Both 301s are deleted and Netlify's pretty-URL handling serves `/prep` in place, the same way `/india` and `/report` already resolve. The page's canonical, `og:url` and JSON-LD `url` move to `https://itsdebatable.com/prep`, because a canonical naming a URL that 301s to the landing page is worse than none. **The debateprep.com host block is left exactly as written**, correct and inert; it costs nothing and starts working the moment the host reaches Netlify.

  **`/prep` also joins the sitemap.** Nothing on the site links to it, so it was an orphan on top of being unreachable, and the staging note's only stated objection (a submitted URL would soft-404) is void now that it is a 200 on a host that has a cert.

  **NOT done, and it is deliberate rather than blocked-on-nothing: the domain was not forced into working.** Adding a Netlify alias whose DNS does not resolve to Netlify can stall cert renewal for every domain on the site, and this repo has already paid for one DNS outage. The safe order is DNS first, alias second, which is what the staging note says too. **Both remaining steps are Aidan's, at GoDaddy and in the Netlify UI, and neither is a code change.** Until then the page is served, indexed and self-consistent on the canonical host, which is the outcome the keyword domain was only ever a nicer address for.

- **The guest lane on /spar was unreachable for fifteen weeks, because nothing on that page ever minted a guest** (2026-08-22, Aidan: "allow ppl who arent signed in to debate up to 2 times and then sign in"). He was describing a feature that shipped on 2026-08-19 and has been advertised on four SEO pages since. It works exactly as designed and almost nobody could get to it.

  **The premise under it was false, and the log states the premise in as many words.** The guest-rounds entry below says the allowance is metered "against the anonymous Firebase uid `js/notifications.js` already mints on every page". It does not mint one. Grepping the shipped tree for `signInAnonymously` finds it in `practice.html`, `live.html`, `live-round.html` and `tournament.html`, and nowhere else, and not in a single one of the eighteen shared scripts /spar loads. So a visitor whose first stop was /spar had **no user at all**, fell through the guest branch to the signed-out branch, and met "Sign in to join the live queue" permanently. Not a flash, not a race, and not for a moment. The lane worked only for somebody who had happened to load /practice or /live earlier in the same browser and picked up a session there, which is nobody's first visit and, given /debate-online takes 68% of organic clicks, nobody's typical one either.

  **Everything downstream was already correct**, which is what made this so quiet: `firestore.rules` accepts an anonymous owner's `matchmaking_queue` create, `spar-pair.mjs` meters `GUEST_FREE_ROUNDS` against `guest_rounds/{uid}`, the gate has separate copy for a spent allowance, and GA4 splits the two gate reasons. Four layers of a five-layer feature, and the missing one was the front door.

  **The fix is that /spar mints its own guest session** when the auth listener reports nobody, then falls into the guest branch that already exists.
