Fixed, and the class is now guarded: the suite parses the paradigm keys out of the data file and asserts both that the dropdown offers every one and that no guide card deep-links to a paradigm the picker cannot select. Worth stating as a habit rather than a bugfix: a data file and a hand-written control that are supposed to agree will drift, and reading the diff will not tell you, because both sides look right on their own.
  - **Guarded, and the guards were shown to fire.** 194 assertions, up from 145, covering the persuasion fence, the weighing position, the panel pin's completeness, effort validity, dispatch and persona coverage per pinned family, and issue-consensus maths. Per the standing habit, the suite passing on the first run proves nothing: sixteen deliberate mutations were applied one at a time (strip the delivery disclaimer, promote persuasion up the ladder, delete the fence, pin a provider with no dispatch entry, pin a bogus effort, collapse the panel to one family, report a lone juror as agreeing with itself, count stopwords as agreement, drop the deciding-issue rule, break contiguity) and every one was caught. One mutation initially "passed" and that was the harness's fault, not the guard's: the anchor string existed in both rubric versions and the edit landed on the retired one.
  - **A third silent gap, found by running the finished code rather than reading it: `degraded` was measured in configured keys, not in votes.** The flag was `available.length < wanted.length`, which only catches a juror whose API key is unset. A juror whose provider returns 429 or times out counted as available, cast no vote, and the ballot still reported `degraded: false`. So the dark Google seat produced full-strength-looking ballots, and `/api/judge/reliability` was undercounting degraded panels using exactly the rounds that were degraded. Now `tally.votesCast < wanted.length`. This one matters more than it reads: silent degradation is the specific thing the 2026-07-30 charter forbids, and the layer had been failing its own promise in the one direction nobody would notice.
  - **And the same mistake, one layer down, which broke every commit in the repo for a few hours.** The renderers were fixed to take the four original axes as a floor; the PARSERS were left strictly all-or-nothing. So a four-axis ballot produced no scorecard at all where it used to produce four, and `scripts/test-judge-run.mjs` (a guard a parallel session had just added around the shared runner) started failing on `origin/main`, which blocks the pre-commit hook for everyone. Fixed by giving `parseDims` and the panel's `medianDims` the same floor the renderers already had: a REQUIRED axis missing drops the card, an OPTIONAL one missing does not. **The lesson is not "run the other test", it is that a fix applied at one layer of a pipeline is not applied.** Half the change was correct and the incorrect half was the half nobody had a reason to look at. Guarded now: the suite asserts a five-axis ballot keeps five, a four-axis ballot still renders four, and a missing required axis still drops the card.
  - **Renderers were the quiet risk.** Both scorecard renderers validated dimensions all-or-nothing across a fixed axis list, which was right while the list was fixed and becomes a bug the instant it grows: adding an axis under that rule would have silently deleted the scorecard from every previously judged round. They now require the four original axes as a floor and render anything beyond them.
  - **`/judge` runs `claude-opus-5`, not Fable, and that is a drain decision rather than a quality one.** The page is public and anonymous; roughly double the per-ballot price buys a difference no debater would notice on a single transcript. The recorded rounds that actually move standing and settle credits are judged by the pinned panel, where the Anthropic seat *is* `claude-fable-5`. Switching `/judge` is one word in `js/judge-options.js` if that trade ever flips.

- **The AI judge stops being a single point of failure: published rubric, three-family panel, human appeal, fee neutrality, permanent audit** (2026-07-30). The judge was the one component where our own thesis conceded a hole: AI-judge resolution is circular. That was survivable while it was only an epistemic problem. It stops being survivable the moment credits, standing, or money settle off a verdict, because "an AI judge the operator owns, tunes, and can silently update" is house control of the outcome, which is the argument that sank Papaya and the argument a plaintiff's lawyer makes in the first paragraph. Five coupled changes, all state in Firestore.
  - **(1) The rubric is published before the round, versioned, and HASHED.** New `lib/judge-charter.mjs` is a pure module holding a season calendar and the plain-language criteria the adjudication core implements: the ten tests, the six-step weighing order, the four scorecard axes, the deadlock ladder, and the five things the judge may not do (no model repair, no invented arguments, no rewarding a flatly false unanswered claim, no deciding on what the judge would have argued, no debater instruction that names a winner). `rubricHash()` is a sha256 over a KEY-SORTED canonical serialization, so the digest follows content rather than object literal order: a reformat does not read as a rubric change and a real change cannot hide behind one. Every judgment now stamps `seasonId`, `rubricVersion`, and `rubricHash` **as of when it was judged**, not as of today, so a July ballot stays attributable to July's criteria forever. `RUBRIC_VERSION` is gone as a constant; that was the thing that made the rubric a moving target. The calendar is append-only and contiguous, with the pre-2026-07-30 era recorded honestly as `published:false` rather than retroactively reclassified under the new rubric.
  - **(2) The judge is a panel of three model families, not one call.** The season pins `claude-sonnet-5`, `gpt-4o`, and `gemini-2.0-flash` (the ids this repo's own brain proxies pin). Three calls to ONE model would measure sampling temperature and produce a flattering agreement number that means nothing, because the correlated error is the model itself; three families measure whether the round was decidable. Majority carries, medians (not means) set the points so one outlier juror cannot drag a score, and the load-bearing rule is that **an even split is NOT tie-broken**: any tie-break rule is a thumb on the scale and it would be our thumb, so a split records `resolution:'unresolved'`, no winner, the ladder does not move, and any market voids at face value. Refunding is the only side-neutral way to close a round nobody can decide. Panel calls run concurrently so wall clock is the slowest juror, not the sum, which keeps a three-family panel inside the sweep's existing budget. **Degradation is disclosed, never silent:** a missing provider key or a failed juror runs the panel short, and if quorum cannot be reached the ballot falls back to a single judge STAMPED `degraded`, surfaced by the public charter endpoint. `JUDGE_REQUIRE_PANEL=1` refuses instead of degrading; the default keeps rounds moving, because a missing key should not freeze every ballot on the site.
  - **(3) Inter-rater reliability is published, warts included.** `/api/judge/reliability` reports **Fleiss' kappa** on the binary winner rather than a raw agreement percentage, because raw agreement is inflated by any panel that leans one way: a judge that always said Prop would score 100% and measure nothing. Kappa nets that out. Published alongside it: the unanimity rate, the unresolved rate (a system that quietly resolved its split cases would show a better number and be worth less), the median juror spread in speaker points, and a count of any round where jurors did NOT receive identical prompts, which would make their agreement a measurement of the prompt. **Nothing is reported as a result under 30 rounds** (`reportable:false`); a kappa over four rounds is noise and dressing noise as documented reliability is the exact overclaim this system exists to avoid.
  - **(4) A human appeal layer, and no model may touch it.** Either debater can appeal within 72 hours on six stated grounds. Filing sets `disputeState:'open'`, which `verdictFrom` has always treated as a refusal and which nothing had ever set, so settlement genuinely freezes. A reviewer is disqualified by having debated the ROUND, not by role, so an admin who was in it cannot rule. Outcomes are upheld / overturned / void, a reason of 20+ characters is mandatory and public, and the original ballot is never deleted: it moves to `original` and the correction lives beside it, which is the only version that survives being subpoenaed. An overturn does not invent new speaker points, because a human reviewer flipping a call is not claiming to have re-scored four axes. **An appeal that cannot reach paid-out credits is decoration**, so this needed real reversal machinery: new `reverseMarket` in `lib/settle.mjs` writes negative compensating ledger entries namespaced by revision, un-settles positions so the corrected winner can be paid from the same intact pool, and backs out Sharp Score inputs; new `reverseRoundRating` in `lib/rating-apply.mjs` reverses the rating delta and the win/loss counts. Both state their limits on the record rather than faking a clean rewind: Glicko rd and volatility are path-dependent and are NOT reconstructed, and a streak is reset rather than guessed. Balances are allowed to go negative on a clawback, deliberately, because clamping at zero would make the ledger's arithmetic false and credits are non-redeemable so an underwater balance costs nothing real. `scripts/test-judge-integrity.mjs` asserts the appeal bench contains no provider call and no cron: routing an appeal back to a bigger model is the same circularity with a larger bill.
  - **(5) Fee neutrality made structural, and money gated on provenance.** Settlement is pari-mutuel with no rake term anywhere, so the operator earns the same whichever side wins. That is now enforced rather than promised: the test reads `credits.mjs` and `settle.mjs` and fails the build on any rake, fee, commission, vig, or house account. Verified adversarially by injecting a rake and confirming the guard fires. Coupled fix: **`MONEY_VERDICT_SOURCES` restricts settlement to `verdictSource:'server'`.** `judgment.mjs` has recorded provenance honestly since it was written and nothing acted on it; live-round ballots are written by a PARTICIPANT'S OWN BROWSER, and a verdict authored by one of the two interested parties settling an economy is worse than the house writing it. Stated consequence: **live human rounds do not settle credits until their ballot moves server-side.** The ladder and the public record are unaffected. No live impact today, since nothing creates `markets` documents yet, which made this the right moment to close it.
  - **Everything is logged, in the cloud, immutably.** New `judge_audit/{judgmentId}`: season, rubric version and hash, every juror's provider and model id, whether each model was the season pin or a **disclosed override**, every raw vote including the dissents, the panel outcome, a hash of the exact prompt each juror received, timings, and failures. Public read, server write, never updated in place; corrections append to a `revisions` subcollection, the same posture as the credit ledger. Full prompt text is deliberately NOT stored: it is ~60KB of identical adjudication core per juror per round, and the rubric hash plus the prompt hash plus the transcript already reproduce the input, so a copy on every row would be a large cost for no evidentiary gain.
  - **Surfaces:** public `/judge-integrity` renders the live charter and reliability figures from the two open endpoints (so the page cannot drift from what the judge is actually pinned to), linked from `/judge` and `/judge-paradigms` and in the sitemap. `/api/judge/charter` and `/api/judge/reliability` are open and keyless so anyone can fetch the charter, hash it, and confirm the criteria that judged a round. Round pages now publish panel agreement next to the verdict and render dissenting jurors with their reasoning intact rather than blended into the majority RFD. **Bug caught while wiring this:** `rounds.html` read the winner as `winner==='prop' ? prop : opp`, so a null winner would have announced the Opposition as victor, which is the precise opposite of what a split panel means; guarded, along with the sweep's ballot email.
  - **Guarded by a hook, not just a test.** `scripts/test-judge-integrity.mjs` (145 assertions) joins the price guard in `scripts/hooks/pre-commit` and hard-blocks a commit that breaks any of these promises, because each one is published to users and quotable in a dispute. Two of its guards were found VACUOUS by deliberately weakening the code they cover: the 1-1 split passes on quorum rather than on the majority rule, so the real 2-2 case (clears quorum, still must not resolve) was untested and is now covered. Worth repeating as a habit: a test suite that passes on the first run has not yet been shown to test anything.

- **The default format is now a spec'd lay format, judged out of 30** (2026-07-28). Quick Clash was the format nobody had written down: 3/3/2/2 with 2 minutes of prep, four rules of one sentence each, and a ballot scored on the APDA 23-30 speaker scale, which is a circuit instrument being pointed at people who have never seen a round. Aidan's one-page lay spec replaces it. **Structure:** constructive then rebuttal, one each a side. The sheet says 5/5/5/5 with 5 minutes prep; shipped as **5/5 constructives, 3/3 rebuttals, 5 minutes prep**, taking the sheet's own flagged risk ("maybe a shorter rebuttal?") rather than a 25-minute default. The four `rules` strings were rewritten to carry the shape: the second constructive does two jobs (answer their case, then build its own), rebuttals bring no new arguments, and every argument is claim then warrant then impact. **Scoring:** a 30-point lay ballot, 15 for the overall argument, 10 for responses to the opponent's case, 5 for structure and comprehension. Highest total wins, so **a weaker case can take the round on responses plus structure**, and the judge is told to open the RFD by saying so when that is what happened. `speakerPoints` stays on the 23-30 scale and stays out of the decision, because the leaderboard series is comparable across formats and a 22/30 rubric total posted into it would read as an incoherent round. The rubric rides a `rubric` key on the ballot JSON and renders as a scorecard under the verdict (and in the text export); if a brain omits it, the ballot renders exactly as before. **The plumbing this needed already existed and was dead:** `singleJudgeBallot` has carried a `{{formatJudgingCriteria}}` placeholder since it moved server-side, and no caller ever filled it, so the literal token was being sent to the model on every ballot. All three call sites in `practice.html` now pass `fmt.judging`, which makes per-format judging criteria a one-key-per-format edit for any future format. **Same rubric on the human surfaces:** live-round's `quick` (the "General" default) got the speech times and the 30-point JUDGE_RULES, and /judge's `quick` got the rubric in prose. **Cost note, deliberate:** AI speech length scales off `speech.time` at ~170 wpm, so the default format's AI turns grew roughly 65% in tokens. That is real drain on the format 56% of rounds use, and it is the trade for a format that is actually specified. **Left alone on purpose:** voice-debate's `quickclash` mode is a separate 2-minute voice drill with its own wiring, so "Quick Clash" now means two different lengths depending on surface; aligning it is its own pass. The /live narration script still says rounds take ten minutes for Quick Clash, and narration does not regenerate when page copy changes.
- **2v2 partner matching, a real tournament engine, and spectator admission** (2026-07-28). Three coupled builds that turn "we could host a tournament" into infrastructure. Closes the open item left by the 2026-07-27 tournament-readiness pass, which ended: *a real bracket/schedule data model once an actual tournament is scheduled (none exists yet)*. **(1) 2v2 partner matching.** A 2v2 needs a partner, an opposing team, and four people awake at once. `/partners` + `partner-match.mjs` own the first: a duo forms either by invite code or through an open `partner_pool` with a mutual-accept handshake, and it NEVER auto-completes the way a spar match does, because a partner is a bigger commitment than an opponent. `duo-pair.mjs` owns the second, and the load-bearing decision there is that the queue doc is keyed by **team, not user**: per-user docs would make a 2v2 a four-way atomic write where any half-commit strands a real person in a room their partner never entered, while team-keyed it is the same two-document transaction spar-pair already runs in production. Either partner can queue the team and the other does not need to be at their screen, because requiring four simultaneous humans instead of two plus two means it never fires at this traffic level. Every cross-user write is admin-SDK server-side, the lesson spar-pair was rebuilt on. **(2) live-round seats two per bench.** A 2v2 stays a two-BENCH round (format spec, transcript tags and ballot all reason about gov vs opp), so partner uids ride the existing benches rather than a new four-side model. Without them `mySide()` returns null for both partners and classifies them as SPECTATORS, locking them out of their own round. New `seatForSpeech()` gates control to one partner at a time; ungated, both hold live controls for every speech on their side and the round desyncs the first time they disagree about whose turn it is. Verified per format: APDA correctly gives seat 1 the PM and the PMR. **BP/Worlds in a four-person room is a COMPRESSION, not real 4-team BP, and is labelled as such.** **(3) tournament engine.** `lib/tournament.mjs` is pure and deterministic: seeded, so a draw can be reproduced when a coach asks why they hit the same opponent twice. It pairs **globally over the ranked field, not per win-bracket** — per-bracket pairing looks right and fails late, because two teams share a record precisely because they just debated, so small brackets fill with prior opponents and the only move left is the rematch (measured: 4 rematches in a 12-team field over 5 rounds, side skew reaching 3). Side pressure sits inside the matching plus a 2-opt repair, since choosing the partner is what keeps sides even; assigning sides to a fixed pairing can only pick the lesser of two evils. 1045 assertions in `scripts/test-tournament-pairing.mjs`. Odd fields are allowed side skew 2 on purpose: the bye breaks side parity and flattening it would cost an unearned pull-up, which is the worse trade. **(4) spectator admission.** Watching was open; anyone with a link dropped into the audience and debaters learned from the count ticking up. Now **public rounds require admission** (discoverable by strangers) while **unlisted rounds admit link-holders automatically** (the link is the invitation). Reuses the `joinRequests` surface with `kind:'watch'`, minus the 20s auto-decline, which would bounce nearly every spectator since debaters are mid-speech. Honest framing: an admission gate, not access control, because the round doc stays publicly readable for indexable completed rounds. **(5) partner quiz + match emails.** The pool is asynchronous in a way the spar queue is not: a spar match resolves in a minute while you watch a spinner, but finding a partner takes hours and nobody sits on a page that long, so without email the pool only fires when two people happen to be on the same screen at the same moment (the same liquidity problem Open Spar Night was built for). Emails go out on exactly two moments, proposal received and team formed, with addresses resolved server-side from Firebase Auth so the proposer never learns the other person's address, sent AFTER the transaction commits so nobody is mailed about a partnership that rolled back. New `partner` stream with its own `partnerOptOut`, deliberately not suppressed by the digest flag (an alert that a named person wants to team with you is closer to a reply than to a mailing list). A five-question quiz (level, goal, speaking position, availability, intensity) ranks the pool by fit with reasons shown, because an unranked pool's only signal is who refreshed most recently. Scoring rule: **complementary beats identical on speaking position, identical beats complementary on everything else.** Calibrating it caught two self-contradictions before ship, both worth remembering as a pattern: a pair who both wanted to speak first scored "Strong match" directly above the words "You both want to speak first," and a format mismatch was the biggest single deduction while never appearing in the reason line. **Costs held flat by design:** a round's pairings ride inline on the round doc so a draw is one read regardless of audience, and responses are shared-cached, per the 2026-07-28 load audit. **Deliberately NOT built:** results are entered by the director. The AI judge already writes a ballot, so auto-ingest is the obvious next step, but a tab that silently accepts machine results has no answer when one is contested; that wants a human confirmation step and a real tournament to calibrate against first.

- **Tournament load: voice metered per user, spectator counting made linear** (2026-07-28). A readiness audit ahead of running real tournaments found two things that break at venue scale, both invisible at today's traffic. **(1) The voice rate limit locked out whole buildings.** `realtime-session.mjs` ran `checkRateLimit('rt_' + ip)` at 6/hour and 10/day BEFORE the auth and plan gate, with no exemption for signed-in or paying callers. A school runs one NAT'd public IP, so the 7th voice round started in that building in an hour got a 429 no matter who started it or what they had paid: a paying user blocked by a stranger's practice round two tables over. Now the caller's token is verified first (JWKS-cached, no Firestore reads) and **named** signed-in callers meter per UID at 30/hour and 100/day, while anonymous callers stay per IP at the old 6/10. **"Named" is a same-day correction to this change, caught by probing the live endpoint after deploy.** The first version handed the per-UID lane to any valid token, and this app uses Firebase ANONYMOUS auth for guests (`isNamedAccount()` in firestore.rules gates on `sign_in_provider != 'anonymous'`). Anonymous accounts are free and unlimited to mint, so that replaced a hard 6/hour/IP ceiling with NO ceiling: rotate a throwaway anonymous account and the IP layers never apply again. Per-account damage was bounded at 2 by `FREE_VOICE_LIFETIME_LIMIT`, but the accounts themselves were not bounded, and `/api/realtime-session` mints real billable OpenAI Realtime sessions (confirmed by curling it unauthenticated: App Check is not enforcing because the site key was never filled in, so the IP layers are the ONLY gate on that endpoint). The lane now requires a named provider and fails closed onto the IP lane when the claim is missing. Tournament debaters sign in with Google or email, so the venue fix is unaffected. Live-verified after the fix: anonymous callers get exactly 6 before the 7th returns 429. Real entitlement was always enforced further down (`FREE_VOICE_LIFETIME_LIMIT`, plan lookup, token balance), so the per-UID layers only need to stop one runaway account and can be generous. The token is verified once and reused by the usage gate, not twice. **The limiter also only pretended to work:** it was a module-scope `Map`, so each Netlify isolate kept its own counter and a cold start wiped it. Under light traffic that reads as a working limit; under load it is a lottery. Moved to a new `lib/rate-limit.mjs` (Upstash-backed, in-memory fallback, reusing the env vars `claude.mjs` already documents). **ACTION: `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` are NOT set on Netlify**, so the shared-counter half is dormant and both this and `claude.mjs` still fall back to per-isolate memory. The per-UID keying fix works regardless. Same pass fixed a spoofable key: the IP now prefers Netlify's `x-nf-client-connection-ip` over the client-supplied `x-forwarded-for`, whose whole raw string was previously the rate-limit key, so one caller could mint unlimited distinct keys by varying a header. **(2) Spectator counting was quadratic.** Every viewer on `/live-round` ran an unbounded `onSnapshot` over the `watchers` subcollection while every spectator wrote a heartbeat every 30s, so the reads behind the "N watching" pill grew as N SQUARED: roughly 5K reads/min at 50 spectators, 80K at 200, 500K at 500 (about $18/hour for a counter), and every browser in the room reprocessed all N documents on every beat, which is what actually made big rooms janky. The obvious fix is Firestore's `count()` aggregation, and it does not exist here: **aggregation is modular-SDK only, and `Query.count` is undefined on the compat builds this page loads. Verified in-browser against 10.5.0 and 11.10.0; do not "restore" it without re-checking that.** Instead the count folds into the presence beat that seated debaters already write to the round doc every 30s, and every client in the room already listens to that doc, so distributing the number is free. Cost is now about 4N reads/min total regardless of audience size (two debaters reading N watcher docs per beat) and a spectator pays nothing: about 2K reads/min at 500 spectators against the old 500K. The freshness filter runs server-side on `ts`, so stale heartbeats never cross the wire; `ts` is a plain field so the automatic single-field index covers it, verified with a live query (no composite index to deploy). Capped at 1000 with a "1000+" display so one enormous room cannot quietly become the expensive case again. Deliberate trade-off: with nobody seated (spectator-only replay viewing) the number holds its last value instead of dropping to zero, because a frozen count beats a wrong one. **(3) A ballot had exactly one chance to succeed.** `generateBallot()` in `live-round.html` was a single fetch with no retry, and its failure card offered a "Try again" button wired to `location.reload()`. A tournament ends many rounds inside the same minute, so that endpoint takes a burst of 8000-token Sonnet calls at the moment it matters most; `claude.mjs` passes the Anthropic response straight through, so one upstream 429 or 529 ended the round with no ballot and a button that reloaded the page back into the same overloaded window. Now bounded to 4 attempts with backoff at roughly 2s, 5s, and 12s, each JITTERED, because N clients retrying on an identical schedule is just a second thundering herd. An explicit `Retry-After` is honored (capped at 30s). Retryable: 408, 425, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 529, plus network failures and PARSE failures, since a truncated or malformed generation is exactly the transient case a retry fixes and the attempt budget bounds the token cost. Auth and plan errors (401/402/403/400) fail fast because they never self-heal. Worst case is about 20s of retrying before the failure card appears, and that card now retries in place instead of reloading, keeps the round view, and says plainly that the transcript is saved. New `live_round_ballot_failed` GA4 event carries status and attempt count so a real tournament leaves evidence. **Not a code fix but load-bearing operationally: anonymous callers share `claude.mjs`'s per-IP anon caps (5/min, 15/hour, 30/day) across a whole venue, and signed-in users bypass them entirely. Everyone signs in before an event, or the building splits 30 requests a day.** Audited alongside and found clean: the deployed Firestore ruleset was byte-identical to `origin/main` (checked against the Rules API, not assumed), coach emails are correctly segregated into admin-only `atlas_contacts`, `public_profiles` carries no contact fields by design, and Daily rooms cap at 8 participants but spectators never join the video, so that is a debater-plus-judge ceiling and not an audience limit.

- **Landing chapters keep content-visibility after the gate opens: landing_cv_v1 A/B** (2026-07-27). A renderer-memory pass found the landing's `content-visibility:auto` block (shipped 2026-05-27, extended since) covers all 13 below-fold sections but was being neutralized exactly when it matters: the 2026-07-25 "See more" gate shipped with a `content-visibility:visible !important` rule that force-renders every chapter the moment the gate opens, so an engaged visitor paid full layout+raster for ~7k lines at once. New `landing_cv_v1` experiment (sticky 50/50, `da-lpcv-ab`, `?lpcv=cv|control` QA force, registered on the shared `__abAssignments`/experiments.js rail, gate-toggle tagged `data-ab-test` and `landing_more_toggle` now carries the arm): the 'cv' arm scopes that override to `html:not([data-lpcv="cv"])` so chapters keep `auto` post-open and render only near the viewport; control keeps today's behavior byte-identical, as do JS-off visitors. Shipped alongside for both arms: per-section `contain-intrinsic-size` re-measured at 1280px and 375px (a `max-width:760px` override block; the old blanket 800px was off by ~1000px on #faq), and the 8 dead selectors from the 2026-05-27 list pruned (.price-compare, #founder-video, #overview, #debate-ai, .topics-edu-pair, .landing-guides-block, .landing-compare-block, #philosophy no longer exist in the document). Deep links through the gate got the founder-scroll treatment in the cv arm: `revealHashTarget` force-renders chapters above the target, lands, then re-lands at 450ms; #waitlist (outside the gate but after every chapter) joins the same path when the gate is open. Measured in isolated Chrome 150 (localhost, analytics blocked, 2 runs per arm + mobile): gate-open to next paint 43-54ms cv vs 124-161ms control; renderer-process RSS in the open-at-top state 267-278MB cv vs 338-353MB control (~70MB, ~20% lighter); anchors settle within 0.5px in cv on desktop and mobile (control parks ~41px past every chapter target, a pre-existing bias from the chapter strip relocating on open, left as-is for experiment purity); scrollspy walks all 13 sections; the #ranked-band sticky rail pins at exactly 72px; FAQ toggles work; identical console noise across arms. The #live-now globe canvas is outside the containment list, so its zero-width fallback sizing is untouched. Watch `ab_impression`/`ab_conversion` for `landing_cv_v1` plus `landing_more_toggle.cv` in GA4; if the cv arm wins, the follow-ups are deleting the scoped override rule and giving the gated `.hero-founder-frame > .hero` (founder scene, still uncontained) its own entry.

- **Round-corpus SEO unblocked from both ends: async rounds get indexable /r/ pages, the /practice publish CTA becomes an experiment** (2026-07-27). sitemap-rounds.xml was serving a valid but empty urlset: `public_rounds` has 0 docs (months of the passive publish card converted nobody) while the async relaunch's completed public rounds lived only at /rounds?r={id}, a client-rendered view whose single canonical points at /rounds, so Google never saw them. Two coupled fixes. (1) **r.mjs falls back to async_rounds**: completed public human-opened rounds render server-side at /r/{id} (turn transcripts with Prop/Opp labels and clocks, ballot + bold speaker points, crowd-vote strip, Article JSON-LD, per-round canonical) and join sitemap-rounds.xml through one shared gate, `indexableAsyncRound()` in lib/async-rounds.mjs. The gate excludes AI-seeded challenges twice over: the 'seed-' id prefix AND prop.uid=='ai', because pre-2026-07-22 sweep passes created AI challenges under plain auto-ids; live Firestore confirmed one such doc that a prefix check alone would have leaked to Google once answered. Hidden, unlisted, incomplete, and transcript-less docs are excluded too; excluded-but-existing ids 302 to /rounds?r={id} so shared links never dead-end. Honesty boundary per the Arena decision: AI inventory is never presented to Google as user rounds. Media stays on /rounds (turns hold Netlify Blobs references); the /r/ page renders transcripts and links "Listen to this round". rounds.html's share button now hands out /r/{id} for indexable rounds so inbound links stack on the canonical URL. At ship time zero async rounds qualify (the only completed round is an AI-opened seed), so this is plumbing that fills as human rounds complete. (2) **practice_publish_cta_v1 A/B on /practice**: 'card' arm keeps the old passive purple card below the fold; 'spotlight' arm renders the same publish action inside the ballot, directly under the verdict and points, with primary styling. Sticky per-browser assignment, ?publishCta=card|spotlight QA force (forced arms excluded from telemetry), impressions counted only when the CTA is 50% visible (the ballot mounts long after experiments.js boots, so a React ref mirrors sendImpression's event shape and sessionStorage dedupe key), clicks ride experiments.js data-ab-test, and round_published carries publish_cta_arm. Both arms signed-in only, since publishing requires auth; the anonymous sign-in card gains one sentence noting that signed-in debaters can publish. Results in Mission Control once each arm passes 20 impressions.

- **Keep the Arena separate from the landing page while it proves itself** (2026-07-27). `/arena` is a standalone live debate lobby, surfaced through the Explore menu without replacing `/`. It combines real public activity where available with clearly labeled seeded broadcast previews when live inventory is quiet. The honesty boundary matters: preview matches, markets, and schedules must never masquerade as current real events. Its visual-authenticity pass uses archived round photography, raw webcam-style stills, signed-in users' own profile photos, and lightweight charts. Generated portrait headshots were rejected because they made the community feel synthetic. Real leaderboard names never receive invented portraits.

- **The tournament front door becomes a spectator lobby** (2026-07-25). `/tournaments` now leads with one job: tell a visitor whether a featured round is live and what will happen when it starts. The first screen pairs a plain waiting-state explanation with a dark watch-room frame where the video, speakers, sides, and official motion will appear. Four selectable sample motions give people something concrete to explore while they wait, and a three-step Waiting, Live, Decision guide explains the full viewing flow. Tournament hosting remains available as a secondary path through one concise pilot card. The director-first Tournament OS architecture still stands behind the product, but the public page no longer asks a new spectator to understand ledgers, state machines, or control-room operations before they can watch.

- **Website experiments get a shared success loop, and investors get an honest vision board** (2026-07-25). The landing hero copy test is now joined by a landing community-CTA test and an investor-thesis test, all using the shared `app/js/experiments.js` primitive. It records one unique-session impression per arm, excludes query-forced QA views, waits until the below-fold community CTA is actually visible before counting exposure, and records unique-session clicks plus raw target clicks. `/api/log-event` dual-writes bounded rows to `experiment_events`; `/api/admin/experiments` rolls them into views, clicks, and CTR, and Mission Control shows a directional lead only after every arm reaches 20 impressions. The `/investors` page remains indexed in the sitemap but is intentionally absent from the global More menu, keeping the public navigation focused on debaters. It deliberately separates Live now, Building, Long term, and Exploratory ideas, and says plainly that the page is a long-term vision board, not a roadmap, forecast, or offer to invest.

- **Debatable becomes the sole public name** (2026-07-25). Aidan explicitly removed the retired brand everywhere public. The sweep deletes it from visible copy, CTA language, accessibility labels, JSON-LD aliases, social metadata, narration labels, extension copy, and native App Store strings. The main timed-round URL moves to `/practice`; the comparison pages move to `/compare/debatable-vs-*`; the differentiator page moves to `/why-debatable`. Old public URLs remain only as 301 redirects so existing links do not break. Compatibility-only infrastructure may retain legacy domain aliases, external service slugs, the existing iOS bundle identifier, and recognition of the first native build's user-agent token. Those are not brand surfaces and must not leak into copy.

- **Profile becomes a training room, with the debate brain as the first move** (2026-07-25). `/profile` now opens as a personal training surface instead of a stack of equally weighted dashboard cards. The identity hero has more depth and energy, and the first interactive block is a live-fact brain card that shows the user's round, format, and training-day signals. Its primary action links directly to `/spectate#brain`, where Identity, Case vault, and Thinking map are built. Daily quests and the nearest milestone remain beside it, while XP, ballots, mastery, achievements, saved rounds, the generated style fingerprint, and account settings keep their existing data and behavior. The zero-round state gets the same brain entry point, so the compounding loop is visible before the first debate.

- **Email/password joins Google account auth** (2026-07-25). The shared sign-in modal now offers first-class email/password registration and sign-in on web, with display-name capture, an 8-character password minimum, verification email, generic password-reset responses, readable Firebase error states, and anonymous-account linking so a guest's local session is not discarded during registration. Google remains a one-tap option, and Apple remains in the native chooser. The topbar, profile, cross-page signup nudge, `/app`, and `/practice` all route into the same chooser. Firebase Identity Platform is configured for password-required email auth; magic links stay retired.

- **Domain cutover: debateai.com → itsdebatable.com** (2026-07-22). Aidan bought **itsdebatable.com** on the GoDaddy aftermarket (whois: registrar GoDaddy, updated 2026-07-18, registration through 2034 — verified BEFORE the sweep, the step both the 2026-05-25 and 2026-06-08 traps skipped) and asked for it to become the primary domain, with debateai.com and the rest redirecting to it. The domain finally matches the "Debatable" brand. **Code sweep (~160 files):** every `debateai.com` reference flipped to `itsdebatable.com` — canonical tags, og:url/twitter, JSON-LD `url`, sitemap.xml + sitemap index, robots.txt, function site origins (create-checkout `siteUrl` default, OpenRouter HTTP-Referer, email template links), /topics + /compare SEO pages. **netlify.toml (both mirrors):** the 2026-07-18 rules that 301'd itsdebatable.com → debateai.com were INVERTED into a canonicalization block at the top of the redirect chain: www/http variants of itsdebatable.com normalize to the https apex; debateai.com, debateit.live (the interim Netlify primary), debatetable.com, and debatethedevil.com all 301 to itsdebatable.com; debateai.com `/api/*` uses **308** (method-preserving) so stale service-worker-cached pages on the old origin can still POST mid-migration; `debateai.com/native` serves IN PLACE (200 rewrite, no redirect) because the iOS binary uploaded to ASC 2026-07-18 hardcodes debateai.com/native with an allowNavigation list that lacks itsdebatable.com — remove that exemption only after a new binary ships (mobile/capacitor.config.ts already points at itsdebatable.com/native). **CORS:** itsdebatable.com + www ADDED to all 16 allowlist files (lib/response.mjs + per-function copies + root mirrors); debateai.com deliberately KEPT for the migration window. **Deliberately NOT touched:** the Chrome extensions (`app/extension/`, `app/float-extension/` — ship through the store separately; their iframe/API calls ride the 301/308), TWA dir (Play-store coupled), `debateai-*` localStorage keys / CSS classes / JS identifiers, `debateai.daily.co` (external Daily.co subdomain), competitor `debateai.org`, sender email addresses (env-driven; links inside emails DID flip). **Infra moves alongside the code:** GoDaddy DNS pointed at Netlify (A @ → 75.2.60.5, www CNAME → debateos1.netlify.app — the previous owner's Dreamhost nameservers had to go, Forwarding stays OFF per the 2026-07-01 outage), Netlify primary flipped www.debateit.live → itsdebatable.com with all legacy domains kept as aliases, SSL provisioned, itsdebatable.com added to Firebase Auth authorized domains (App Check needs nothing — the site key was never filled in). The push was gated on DNS actually resolving first; code never ships pointing at a dead domain. **Open follow-ups:** GSC property for itsdebatable.com + sitemap submission; Stripe webhook endpoint URL update (Stripe doesn't follow redirects; harmless while checkout is BETA_NO_CHARGE-gated); social handles unchanged.

- **Stated mission on the landing: democratize the verdict, the people decide who won** (2026-07-20). Two new landing sections give the site an explicit "what you do" and "why this exists", distilled from Aidan's July 2026 correspondence with an essayist he had pitched by declaring the essay dead. **`#how-it-works`** sits after the live band: four stations (pick a motion, speak on the clock, it argues back, read your ballot) plus the round CTA. Its "Watch the walkthrough" button is rendered hidden behind a `HOWTO_VIDEO` constant and only appears when that holds a URL, because the founder intro video was pulled 2026-05-19 and nothing replaced it. See the standing no-demo-links rule; this is how it gets satisfied without a dead CTA. **`#why-this-exists`** sits after `#live-proof` and carries the argument: judging, not talent, is the bottleneck ($60-90/hr judges, rounds rationed, 23,000+ US schools with no program, so a shortage of verdicts rather than arguers); the essay is alive as a tool but **dying as a receipt** now that AI made polished prose cheap, which is why schools are returning to oral exams; and argument is a spectator sport whose reputation is earned across hundreds of public rounds rather than one tab room's opinion on one weekend. **TRUTH BOUNDARY, and it is load-bearing: audience voting does not exist.** There is no poll or crowd-vote path in `live-round.html` or `spectate.html`, and The Floor's crowd read was switched off the same day (see the floor preview entry). Spectating IS real, instant ballots ARE real, so tenet 03 says "Next comes the vote" in future tense on purpose. Do not promote crowd verdicts as shipped until a vote path exists; when it does, that one clause is the only edit needed. Related copy not used, deliberately: the correspondence claims "we've spent nothing on paid marketing", which contradicts §8 (a ~$660 paid push around Apr 19 2026). Do not put that claim on any surface, investor-facing included.

- **Brand renamed to Debatable, domain unchanged** (2026-07-18). Aidan expanded the same-day app-only rename to the whole brand. Visible legacy naming was swept across titles, metadata, wordmarks, body copy, and email templates. Technical identifiers and structured-data aliases were initially preserved, then superseded by the sole-name sweep on 2026-07-25.

- **iOS app ships under the name "Debatable"** (2026-07-18). The App Store listing name and home-screen label are Debatable. The bundle identifier remains unchanged because it is the registered app identity, not public branding.
- **Reprice Individual $5→$10/year, Team $20→$50/year; free voice cut to 1/2** (2026-06-27). A unit-economics audit (real provider prices) showed the old $5/year Individual tier loses money on any engaged user once billing turns on, and voice (OpenAI Realtime) is ~80% of per-user variable cost. Aidan set the new prices himself: **Individual $10/year, Team $50/year** (BYOK $1/mo, Lifetime $14.99, Free $0 unchanged). Swept across all canonical surfaces (JSON-LD offers, pricing.html, in-app paywall/error strings, landing, compare pages, terms, story, report, geo.mjs INR → ₹838/₹4,190) in one commit, SW `v1284→v1285`. Coupled cost fix: **free voice hard-capped 1 anon / 2 signed-in** (was 3 / 8) — client gate (`voice-debate.html`), the real server enforcement (`realtime-session.mjs` `FREE_VOICE_LIFETIME_LIMIT 8→2`), and the gate copy. Voice is now the paid hook, not a giveaway. Note: prices are display-copy only until billing is switched on — checkout is still gated by `BETA_NO_CHARGE` (defaults true) in `create-checkout.mjs`, and `BETA_PRO_UNLOCK=true` in `index.html` still unlocks pro for everyone. Stripe→Firebase webhook path was audited and is wired end-to-end. The remaining provider-cost and premium-TTS follow-ups are tracked in the profitability and unit-economics strategy documents.
- **Lifetime repriced $14.99→$30, referral-code plan added to /pricing** (2026-07-02). Aidan asked for the Lifetime plan to move to **$30 once** and for pricing-page links to either land on the correct in-app plan or email `aidandavidhollinger@gmail.com`. `/pricing` CTAs now have real fallback hrefs: Free goes to `/app`, Individual/Lifetime go to `/app?upgrade=...`, BYOK and Team beta open mailto. Added a pricing-page referral-code plan: beta behavior is attribution first (`?ref=code` carried through sign-in and admin), post-beta rewards are tested only after paid conversion, and code owners include coaches, captains, organizers, ambassadors, and business champions. Swept current pricing surfaces and INR display for Lifetime `$30` / `₹2,514`.
- **Hero creed line removed from the landing** (2026-06-27). The rotating "voice-of-the-builder" creed under the hero ("AI as antagonist and tool. Not the Messiah, nor an arbitrary enemy.", "Built to challenge you. Not to flatter you.", etc.) was removed at Aidan's direct, repeated request. This **supersedes** the 2026-05-19 / 2026-05-23 entries that protected the creed as a permanent fixture ("the creed stays by design") — those described the stance at the time; the founder has since asked for the line gone. Both render elements were deleted from `app/landing.html`: the `<p class="hi-creed">` in the `.hero-illustrated` arm (the rotating one, driven by the `CREEDS` array + `swapCreed` rotator, which now no-ops on its `if (creed)` guard) and the static `<p class="hero-creed">` in the sign-in arm. The `CREEDS` array and the `.hi-creed` / `.hero-creed` CSS were left in place as inert dead code (no element references them); a later cleanup can strip them. Earlier the same day the same line had also been removed from the footer wordmark where a prior commit had relocated it. Net: no creed/tagline line renders under the hero or in the footer anymore. Do not re-add it without Aidan asking.
- **Earlier brand-name reframe, later superseded** (2026-06-08, corrected 2026-06-25). The product briefly used another name while keeping debateai.com as the owned production domain. The 2026-07-18 Debatable rename and the 2026-07-25 sole-name sweep supersede that public naming system.
- **Removed anonymous trial** (2026-04). The free-bypass was leaking usage and preventing a real paywall signal. Gmail-required is the only clean policy.
- **Removed magic-link auth** (2026-04). Firebase email-link wasn't enabled and "via Gmail" was the stated direction. Two paths = confusion; one is clearer. **REVERSED 2026-08-12, see the emailed-link entry.**
- **Reversed Gmail-required gate to advise-only** (2026-04-20). The full-screen pricing/sign-in gate locked out users whose browser couldn't load Firebase (iOS Safari ITP, Instagram/FB/TikTok in-app browsers, strict ad blockers). Too big a funnel leak to ignore. Now: anyone can enter with the 5-request anon cap; sign-in is advised via the topbar CTA + the post-2-generations nudge modal + feature-specific paywalls.
- **Tab shadows bumped to 30px vertical padding** (2026-04). `overflow-x:auto` on `.nav` clamps overflow-y to auto, clipping the red halo. 30px padding gives the shadow room to breathe.
- **Case Feedback moved from Other → Competitive** (2026-04). Post-round critique is a competitive-prep tool, not a support link.
- **Vocab Quiz added to Competitive** (2026-04). 52-term bank across debate flow / Policy / philosophy / elevated vocab. Multiple choice + flashcard mode, localStorage streak.
- **God mode trait preset** (2026-04). All strengths on, no weaknesses. `buildTraitPrompt` detects the combo and swaps to a dedicated world-champion prompt.
- **Audience pages use shared `/css/audience.css`** (2026-04). Level 1 distinctness is per-page `:root` override blocks for `--accent` and `--accent-glow`.
- **Per-format research allowance** (2026-04). `voice-guidelines.mjs` now has explicit research rules per format. Policy cards, PF citations, LD philosophy lit, parli no-evidence, Congress / MUN conversational cites. Prevents the AI from fabricating `Smith 2022` quotes in formats that don't use them.
- **APDA removed from CURRENT_TOPICS** (2026-04). Impromptu format; no rolling motion exists to hub. Users routed to Motions tab instead.
- **Counter sub-brand for the chrome extension** (2026-05-10). The chrome extension at `app/extension/` ships as **Counter: Oral Exam Trainer (by DebateAI)** — student-first, viva / oral-exam framed. The web app stays debater-first (per the 2026-04-20 commitment); the extension is a focused entry point for the "students" secondary audience listed in §2 (interview prep, admissions essays, thesis defense). This is NOT a contradiction with debate-first: same engine, different front door, sized for the highest-conversion entry context (highlight study material → defend out loud). India is the obvious primary market for this surface (80% of traffic, viva is universal in CBSE/ICSE board exams + IIT/NEET interviews + dissertation defenses). When future cleanup sweeps the surface for stale "DebateAI"-only branding, leave the extension's "Counter" labels alone.
- **Viva format added to FORMATS** (2026-05-10). New `viva` entry in `app/debate-ai.html` and `app/netlify/functions/lib/voice-guidelines.mjs`. Auto-selected when `?ext=1&mode=counter` is present. Two-speech structure (opening defense + cross-examination Q&A), no fabricated citations, plain academic register, examiner asks follow-ups under time pressure. This is the format the Counter extension defaults to.
- **Examiner persona ('Dr. Iyer')** (2026-05-10). New persona in `app/voice-debate.html` PERSONALITIES + `tts.mjs` voice maps. Indian-English measured academic tone via the OpenAI/Realtime `instructions` field; ElevenLabs falls back to its existing American voice (timbre stays American, register matches). Default persona for the Counter extension. Hindi-language vivas use the existing `aiLanguage='hi'` toggle, which already routes through the LLM and TTS pipelines.
- **Counter extension iframe switched to voice-debate.html** (2026-05-10). For oral-exam prep, the voice round (OpenAI Realtime) is the actual drill, not the typed flow. The extension side panel now loads `voice-debate.html?ext=1&mode=counter` instead of `debate-ai.html?ext=1&mode=counter`. The bridge useEffect in voice-debate.html prefills motion + auto-picks the Examiner persona + auto-picks `crossex` mode, landing the user one tap from "Connect."
- **Reverted the splash interstitial at /** (2026-05-15). Five days after adding `/splash.html` as the public face of the site at `/`, reverted it back to serving `/landing.html` at `/`. The splash had ~14 words of body content and zero outbound links (PREP/COMMUNITY removed the same day), so Google was indexing an empty page at the canonical URL `https://debateai.com/` while the 2,700-line marketing page with full SoftwareApplication + FAQPage structured data was buried at `/landing` with no inbound link equity. This is the root cause for the §8 "organic search is near zero" entry — not a content problem, a routing problem. The splash is still reachable at `/splash` for anyone who wants the voice-first intro; it just stops being the URL Google sees first. Paired with this: bumped the SoftwareApplication offers in the JSON-LD to the canonical pricing tiers (Free / BYOK $1 / Individual $5 / Lifetime $14.99 / Team $30), and added a `google-site-verification` meta placeholder so the user can verify ownership in GSC and finally see what queries the site ranks for.
- **Learning loop wired end-to-end** (2026-05-13). Closes §9 #4. Four surgical patches shipped: (1) `captureTurn` lives inside `callAnyAI` + `callClaude` in `app/index.html` so every typed AI turn (case, opp_attack, rebuttal, judge, debate-chat, casual, vision, resolution) lands in the `generations` Firestore collection with the full prompt → output triple, not just the case generator. (2) `voice-debate.html` `stop()` flushes the full turn-by-turn transcript to `/api/log-generation` with `kind:'voice_round'` so the WebRTC voice path stops being a blind spot. (3) New `app/netlify/functions/lib/exemplars.mjs` pulls 1–3 admin-weighted past rounds matching motion + format + side and prepends them as REFERENCE ROUNDS to `body.system`. Wired into all four brain functions (`claude.mjs`, `openai-chat.mjs`, `gemini.mjs`, `grok.mjs`) before `applyVoiceGuidelines`. Client (`callAnyAI` / `callClaude` in `index.html`) now sends `_motion` + `_side` in the body, stripped server-side after read. (4) New `scheduled-distill.mjs` runs nightly at 04:00 UTC over the previous 30 days of top-rated (rating ≥ 4 OR saved) generations per format, sends them to Claude Haiku with a strict "extract patterns" system prompt, writes the result to `learning_distillations/{format}`. `lib/distillations.mjs` reads that doc and appends a LEARNED PATTERNS block to `body.system` on every subsequent generation. Final system-prompt order: `[REFERENCE ROUNDS] + base system + [LEARNED PATTERNS] + [VOICE GUIDELINES]`. Voice rules come last so they win conflicts. Two 1-hour in-memory caches (admin uids, distillations) keep per-request Firestore reads near zero. Anon users can't log (server requires Firebase token), so this only captures signed-in usage — fine, that's the population we want.
- **Free tier bumped 5→15 and pricing surfaces softened** (2026-05-14). The 5-anon / 10-signed-in cap made the product feel paid-by-default; casual visitors hit the paywall before they understood the wedge. New caps: 15 anon, 30 signed-in (15 + 15). Per-pro-feature trial (Sneaky, Opp Attack, Competition Depth, Style Memory) bumped from 1 → 5 in `app/index.html` for the same reason. Pricing surfaces dialed back the same day: (a) `app/js/upgrade-cta.js` now only renders the floating "Upgrade" pill when the user has actually hit the cap, not as a persistent default-state ad on every page for every free user. (b) `app/js/usage-banner.js` hides the top-center usage banner for free users until 75%+ used (was 33%); paid users keep the graduated 50%/75%/90% severity tiers since budget awareness is genuinely useful for them. (c) `/pricing` removed from the `.landing-jumpnav` sticky in-page nav on `app/landing.html` — pricing info still reachable via the on-page FAQ "Pricing & practical stuff" block and the footer, just not surfaced as a top-level nav target. (d) Copy strings everywhere that said "5 free requests" / "all 10 free requests" / "Sign in for 5 more" got updated to either the new numbers or to ambient phrasing ("rounds left," "more on the house") so the per-action counter stops shouting the metering at the user. Updates to: `app/debate-ai.html` (ANON_LIMIT/SIGNED_IN_LIMIT consts + counter + modal copy), `app/index.html` (getAnonLimit, canUseProFeature, paywall modal, sign-up nudge), `app/learn.html` (ANON_BASE_LIMIT + banner copy), `app/high-school.html` (limit-hit copy), `app/counter.html` (hero pre-install + FAQ pricing line), `app/pricing.html` (Free-tier feature bullets), `app/report.html` (Free-tier row in the offers table), `app/landing.html` (JSON-LD FAQ answer + visible FAQ answer + pricing-card Free row), `app/extension/STORE_LISTING.md` (free-tier line). Server-side per-IP throttle in `app/netlify/functions/claude.mjs` (5/min, 40/hour, 150/day on `ANON_LAYERS`) is unchanged — that's an anti-abuse layer, not a paywall signal. Supersedes the 5-request principle in §4.
- **Beta-pricing framing, bento FAQ, villain-line removal** (2026-05-14, same day, third pass). Three more positioning moves:
  - **All tiers reframed as "future pricing — beta is free."** The product is officially in beta now; every pricing surface leads with "Currently free. The numbers below are what plans will look like after beta — share thoughts via feedback." Updates to: `app/pricing.html` (hero eyebrow + h1 + lede + A/B variant, new beta banner above the cards), `app/landing.html` FAQ "Is Debate AI free?" answer and the bigger "How much does it cost?" answer (both now lead with the beta state and link to the feedback form for pricing proposals), `app/debate-ai.html` paywall modal (eyebrow "Beta cap reached" + body explains beta + button now routes to the feedback Google Form, not /pricing), `app/js/upgrade-cta.js` (title "Help shape future pricing"; sub "Beta is free. Pricing proposals welcome."; CTA renamed "Feedback" + routed to the feedback form instead of /pricing). soul.md §4 + §5 + §7 updated: "Free during beta" lifted off the banned-phrases list since the product is now genuinely in beta with explicit future-pricing-for-reference framing. The literal "go to feedback for better pricing proposals" phrasing the user asked for is reused verbatim across surfaces so the message is consistent.
  - **Landing FAQ converted to a bento layout.** Replaced the uniform 3-column grid with a 6-column `grid-auto-flow: dense` bento where each tile is sized by editorial weight: `.faq-item--big` (3col × 2row) for wedge-defining questions (What is Debate AI / How is this different / How much does it cost / Is using this to prep cheating), `.faq-item--wide` (3col × 1row) for substantive secondaries, `.faq-item--circle` (2col × 1row, `aspect-ratio:1/1`, `border-radius:50%`) for short punchy questions ("Which debate formats?" / "Get started in 60 seconds" / "Bring your own API key?" / "Share cases with my team?" / "Who's behind this?"), and the rest as default 1×1 tiles. Open circles transition out of the disc shape into a normal rounded card so the answer panel fits; on mobile (<680px) all tiles collapse to a single column and aspect ratios are killed (a circle in a 1-col stack is awful). The `#faq > .wrap` max-width bumped 1240 → 1480 to give the bento room to breathe. Big-tile copy gets a larger non-uppercase question font so the size hierarchy reads visually, not just structurally.
  - **Villain lines removed sitewide.** The hero "The chatbot agrees with you. We don't." beat on `app/landing.html` was cut along with its CSS rule. The whole `.why-different` section (60+ lines: "Why Debate AI, not ChatGPT — Grounded in real rounds. Not just internet text. ChatGPT doesn't have any of that. It also agrees with you. This doesn't.") got deleted. The landing FAQ "How is this different from just using ChatGPT?" was renamed to "How is this different from a general-purpose AI?" and rewritten to describe what the product does rather than what ChatGPT allegedly fails at. The pricing.html "Does it actually make me better, or is it just another ChatGPT?" FAQ question and "ChatGPT will generate you a persuasive blob" answer got rewritten the same way. The counter.html "Is this just another AI chatbot in a side panel?" question was rewritten as "How is this different from chatting with an AI tutor?" without the villain framing. The topics/index.html "Most 'AI debate' tools give you a generic chatbot" line and the extension/STORE_LISTING.md "Not a chatbot pretending to be a tutor" line both lost the villain phrasing. The index.html "Debate-trained, not generic AI" pill lost its "not generic AI" half. Decision: positive product framing only — sneering at the out-group reads as insecure and contradicts the "varsity debater on the circuit, not philosophy seminar" register the AI itself is supposed to use. Banned-phrase list in soul.md §5 updated.
- **Individual repriced $5/mo → $5/year, Voice "Pro" rebranded to "New", FAQ regridded** (2026-05-14, same day, second pass). Three coupled product-positioning moves:
  - **Individual: $5/mo → $5/year.** The previous $5/mo target tier was being mentally compared to Netflix ($15-22/mo) and Spotify ($11/mo) — same shape (monthly SaaS), so the user's frame was "is this worth a recurring monthly bill?" At $5/year the frame switches to "is this worth a single tournament entry fee?" which is a trivially-yes answer for anyone who debates. Annual billing also kills churn-rate volatility. Updated in: `app/landing.html` (JSON-LD offer description + visible FAQ answer + pricing-rows card), `app/pricing.html` (JSON-LD + title + meta og/twitter + lede + Individual card cadence/button/note + FAQ entry on under-50-requests + Lifetime + Team subtitles), `app/debate-ai.html` (JSON-LD + paywall-modal "signed-in quota used" body), `app/index.html` (callAnyAI + callClaude error strings, pricing-grid in both signed-out and signed-in states, BYOK-cross-provider error, paywall-modal `/month` → `/year` cadence in localizedPrice block), `app/counter.html` (FAQ pricing line), `app/report.html` (header + Individual row + closing margin paragraph), `app/india.html` (rupee/USD mix), `app/us.html` (USD mix), `app/extension/STORE_LISTING.md` (free-tier line), `app/js/upgrade-cta.js` (pill title), `app/netlify/functions/lib/auth.mjs` (paid-feature error message). JSON-LD `price` field kept as "5" (still $5) — only the cadence/recurrence framing changed. BYOK ($1/mo = $12/yr) is now nominally more expensive than Individual ($5/yr), but BYOK serves a different niche: power users who'd rather route everything through their own Anthropic key for cost transparency / privacy / having the receipts. Lifetime ($14.99 once) is now ~3 years of Individual — still a meaningful upsell for users who want to forget about renewing.
  - **Voice rebranded: "Pro" tag → "New" tag.** On `app/landing.html` the hero pill rail and hero CTA rail both labeled Voice with a gold-on-amber `Pro` tag (CSS classes `hero-pill-pro` / `hero-cta-rail-pro` / `hero-pill-tag` / `hero-cta-rail-tag`). That framing put Voice mentally in the "paid feature locked behind a tier" bucket. Renamed CSS classes to `-new` and recolored from gold/amber to brand red (the same red as the primary CTA) so the badge reads as a fresh-feature highlight, not a paywall flag. Paired with the rename, the voice gate in `app/voice-debate.html` bumped its free caps (anon 1 → 3, signed-in 3 → 8) so the label isn't lying — Voice is now genuinely more accessible, not just relabeled. Updated copy on the upgrade gate ("Upgrade to Pro for unlimited voice" → "Keep going with a plan") and the sign-in gate ("Sign in for 3 more rounds" → "Sign in for 8 more voice rounds"). The TTS HD voice provider selector inside `app/debate-ai.html` (ElevenLabs / Inworld vs free OpenAI gpt-4o-mini-tts) keeps its `Pro` tag — that one is a genuine paid premium-voice upsell, not the Voice-round feature itself.
  - **Landing FAQ regridded into 3 columns.** `app/landing.html` `#faq` wrap max-width bumped 780 → 1240 (override on the `!important` rule at line 2636). The inner container converted from a vertical stack to `.faq-grid` with `grid-template-columns:repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr))`; group headings (`.faq-group-head`) span the full row via `grid-column:1 / -1`. Drops to 2 columns under 1024px and 1 column under 680px so mobile reading order stays linear. Visual goal: FAQ now uses the available page width instead of a tall narrow rail nobody scrolled to the bottom of.

- **Team $30/mo → $20/year** (2026-05-14, fifth pass). Extending the annual-pricing framing to Team. At $20/year for 50 seats, the math is ~$0.40/seat/year — cheaper-than-anything for a whole debate club. Updated in: `app/landing.html` (JSON-LD + pricing-rows Team card), `app/pricing.html` (JSON-LD + Team `<article>` card: $30/mo → $20/year, 2,000 → 1,500 requests/mo, 30 → 50 seats, fine print), `app/debate-ai.html` (JSON-LD), `app/index.html` (both pricing-grid blocks, the upgrade-modal `localizedPrice(30→20)` + Team card `/month` → `/year` + "1,500 requests · 50 members" → "1,500 requests/mo · 50 seats", the team-card price string in the in-app paywall row), `app/us.html` (footer pricing), `app/extension/STORE_LISTING.md`, `app/report.html` (Team row + the "ARPU multiplier" line referencing the $30 tier), `app/netlify/functions/lib/geo.mjs` RAZORPAY_INR_PRICES (Individual ₹399/mo → ₹419/year, Lifetime ₹1,499 → ₹1,249, Team ₹2,400/mo → ₹1,675/year), `soul.md` §7 + `AGENTS.md`'s "Pricing is locked" line + the "ask before doing" pricing-tier line. JSON-LD `price` field kept as "20" (still $20) — only the cadence/recurrence framing changed.

- **Two new brains, same day: DeepSeek + Open Lab** (2026-05-15, sixth pass). The stack went 4 brains → 6 brains via two parallel commits that arrived hours apart. First commit (576cb71) added `deepseek` — direct DeepSeek API proxy at `app/netlify/functions/deepseek.mjs` (mirrored to root), pinned to `deepseek-chat`, color `#4D6BFE`, paired with per-task brain routing (BRAIN_TASKS map: case gen → Claude, judging → DeepSeek, rebuttals → Grok, current-events → Gemini), multi-brain panel judging that fans the ballot to DeepSeek + Claude + GPT in parallel with a consensus banner, /exhibition AI-vs-AI orchestrator, and the Sparring Roster v0.1. Second commit (this entry's branch) added `openlab` — OpenRouter-backed proxy at `app/netlify/functions/openlab.mjs` (mirrored to root), default model `nousresearch/hermes-4-405b`, color `#a78bfa`, backend allow-list also covers Hermes 3 (fallback), Mistral Large 2, Qwen3-235B, Llama 4 Maverick/Scout — env override via `OPENLAB_MODEL` / `OPENLAB_ALLOWED_MODELS`. Both are Pro-tier only (`requirePaidPlan`) with identical applyExemplars / applyVoiceGuidelines / applyDistillations plumbing so the learning loop captures their turns the same way as the original four. Both also got their own env var: DeepSeek wants `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, Open Lab wants `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. Pitch separation: DeepSeek is the cheap reasoner — sharp, technical, default for AI-judged ballots so the verdict has no house-style preference. Open Lab is the human-voice pool — Nous Hermes 4 was picked specifically for character-rich prose and low refusal rates ("THBT the means justify the ends" style motions where the big-lab brains get hedge-y). Realtime council in `realtime-session.mjs` deliberately stays at the original four — adding two more parallel calls adds noticeable latency to session start for marginal prep diversity. "4 brains" → "6 brains" sweep across: `app/index.html` (CASE_BRAINS, three paywall/error strings, the "GPT/Gemini/Grok still count" notice now reads "GPT, Gemini, Grok, DeepSeek, and Open Lab still count"), `app/debate-ai.html` (BRAINS, typed-mode preamble, upgrade-popover, "Upgrade for all 6 brains" link, JSON-LD), `app/landing.html` (proof-item card → "6 brains / Claude · GPT · Gemini · Grok · DeepSeek · Open Lab", pricing-rows Individual blurb, JSON-LD, sign-in error fallback), `app/voice-debate.html` (typed-mode-vs-voice explainer), `app/pricing.html` (Individual / Lifetime / Team feature lists), `app/counter.html` (FAQ "Who built this"), `app/report.html` (Individual row), `app/extension/STORE_LISTING.md` (positioning paragraph), `AGENTS.md` ("ask before doing" line), `soul.md` §3 wedge #4 + §7 pricing-table.

- **Devil's Advocate brand sunset** (2026-05-18). The mirror domain `debatethedevil.com` and the dead Netlify deploy URL `https://devilsadvocate1.netlify.app` were removed from every surface that could be cleaned without breaking real product features. Sweep covered: (a) the `contact@devilsadvocateteam.com` team email replaced sitewide with `feedback@debateai.com` (16 surfaces — terms, privacy, schools, pricing, all footers, mailto subjects). (b) CORS allowlists in all 13 Netlify brain/api functions (claude/openai-chat/gemini/grok/deepseek/openlab/tts/argument-lint/docs-agent/billing-portal/realtime-session/lib-response, plus the mirrored root copies for deepseek + openlab) dropped both `debatethedevil.com` and `devilsadvocate1.netlify.app` entries — anyone still hitting the old mirror from a browser now gets a CORS denial. (c) `billing-portal.mjs` final-fallback URL `https://debatethedevil.com` → `https://debateai.com`. (d) `app/extension/manifest.json` host_permission for `https://debatethedevil.com/*` removed (the chrome extension's side panel stops loading on the mirror; debateai.com is the only supported origin). (e) Terms and privacy meta descriptions + visible "covers the web app at debateai.com AND debatethedevil.com" lines collapsed to debateai.com only. (f) `landing.html` and `app/netlify.toml` comments mentioning the old mirror retired to generic descriptions. (g) `soul.md` §8 "Live at debatethedevil.com" → "Live at debateai.com" and AGENTS.md header line trimmed. 23 files, -41 net stale lines. What stays: the `/devils-advocate` route is a legitimate product feature (the single-AI sparring mode, distinct from the multi-format trainer at `/app`), so its filename and admin-report label stay. If the mirror domain `debatethedevil.com` is later retired entirely at the DNS level, no further code action is needed; the codebase no longer references it. **2026-06-14 reversal (email only):** Aidan asked to point contact email back to `contact@devilsadvocateteam.com`, so the sitewide sweep was undone for the address — every user-facing `mailto:` link and the JSON-LD contact `email` field across the static HTML pages + `signup-nudge`/loaders now read `contact@devilsadvocateteam.com` again (the Counter chrome extension's `sidepanel.js` was left on the old address since it ships through the store separately). The domain/CORS/mirror cleanup from this entry STAYS; only the contact inbox flipped back. Don't re-sweep the email to `feedback@debateai.com`.

- **Multilingual end-to-end wiring** (2026-05-18). Audit caught that the i18n infrastructure shipped half-built: landing.html had a working `<select>` picker (Google Translate + `debateos-locale` localStorage); debate-ai.html had its own 14-language `aiLanguage` picker that injected a directive into `buildPrompt()`; **but** index.html (the `/app` surface) had `SUPPORTED_LOCALES`, the I18nContext, `loadLocale()`, and `t()` lookups all working — yet no picker in the topbar (lost in a refactor) and no language directive on any brain call, so signed-in `/app` users could land in Hindi UI but the case generator still replied in English. voice-debate.html had aspirational comments referencing `aiLanguage='hi'` but the field was never sent or read, and the realtime transcription was hard-pinned to `language:'en'` which mis-transcribes non-English audio. Five surgical patches: (1) `app/index.html` adds a `buildLanguageBlock()` helper that reads `debateos-locale` and emits a non-negotiable LANGUAGE directive at the head of the system prompt; wired into both `callAnyAI` and `callClaude` so every typed-mode AI surface (case gen, battle, judge, vision, casual, philosopher) now respects the picked locale. (2) `app/index.html` topbar gets a language-picker pill that overlays an invisible `<select>` on a visible flag-and-code pill matching the theme/style/Donate pill row — same UI pattern the original commit shipped before the refactor dropped it. (3) `app/voice-debate.html` reads `debateos-ai-lang || debateos-locale` and posts it as `aiLanguage` in the `/api/realtime-session` mint body, also mirrors it onto `window.__aiLanguage` so the dc.onopen `session.update` (separate closure) can pin `transcription.language` to the real user language instead of hardcoded `'en'`. (4) `app/netlify/functions/realtime-session.mjs` reads `body.aiLanguage`, maps it through a `REALTIME_LANG_NAMES` allow-list (14 languages mirroring debate-ai.html's `LANGUAGES`), and prepends a `LANGUAGE: Speak the entire round in {name}` directive at the top of the instructions stack so the spoken AI honors the user's language pick. (5) `app/netlify/functions/tts.mjs` Cartesia path had a hardcoded `language: 'en'` (sonic-2 requires the field but accepts any language code) — now threaded through from a new `body.language` field (BCP-47 short codes, regex-validated, default `'en'`). `app/debate-ai.html` TTS call sites (fetchTTS + fetchTTSBlob) now post `language` from `debateos-ai-lang || debateos-locale`. ElevenLabs `eleven_turbo_v2_5` and OpenAI `gpt-4o-mini-tts` already auto-detect from input text so the field is harmless on those paths and load-bearing only on Cartesia. Result: a user picks Hindi on landing → reaches `/app` → UI strings hydrate from `/i18n/hi.json` → AI brain calls (typed + voice) all reply in Hindi → TTS synthesizes Hindi audio across every provider. Pre-existing translation-coverage gap (many hardcoded React-tree strings still in English on `/app`) is a separate concern from this infrastructure wiring; that's a `t()`-call sweep, not a multilingual-system fix.

- **Netlify credit-burn audit: scheduled trims + soft-cap rollback** (2026-05-18, two passes). The user opened a Netlify dashboard showing credits draining unexpectedly fast; analysis flagged 63 functions with the dominant burn sources being the keep-alive cron, the 5-min kickoff-reminder cron, the nightly distillation/fingerprint Haiku passes, and a generous anon-IP day cap that bots could rotate against. **First pass (invocation + cron trims):** (a) Deleted `app/netlify/functions/scheduled-keepalive.mjs` entirely. Was already dropped 5min → 15min on 2026-05-16 and still bleeding (4 runs/hr × 5 invocations ≈ 14.4K/month). The 2-5s cold-start penalty on the first off-peak user is the trade. Brain functions still work without it; the file's own comment documented deletion as the disable path. (b) `scheduled-kickoff-reminder.mjs` 5-min cron → 15-min cron; window widened 5-15 min → 5-25 min so coverage stays gapless and the `reminderSent` flag still dedupes. Saves ~5.7K invocations/month. Reminder now fires 5-25 min before kickoff (median ~15) instead of 5-15 (median ~10). (c) `claude.mjs` `ANON_LAYERS` tightened: hour 40 → 25, day 150 → 60. Same-day second pass dropped further to hour 15, day 30 once the user-facing caps came down. The user-facing soft cap is the friendly paywall; the IP layers exist only to throttle bot abuse rotating on a single IP. (d) `scheduled-user-fingerprint.mjs` nightly cap 60 → 30 users, freshness 7 → 14 days. Style fingerprints describe an argumentative identity and don't drift weekly. Halves nightly Haiku spend (~$0.18 → ~$0.09). Env overrides `FINGERPRINT_MAX_USERS`/`FINGERPRINT_FRESH_DAYS` remain. **Second pass (soft-cap rollback):** the 2026-05-14 bump from 5/10 → 15/30 was a conversion-funnel move, but with the product still in beta nothing converts anyway and the wider funnel doubled drain on real users PLUS doubled bot abuse on the same lever. Reverted to 5 anon / 10 signed-in across `app/debate-ai.html` (ANON_LIMIT + SIGNED_IN_LIMIT consts + JSON-LD + quota-modal copy + counter-banner copy + paywall comment), `app/index.html` (ANON_BASE_LIMIT + getAnonLimit + "sign in for 5 more" copy), `app/learn.html` (ANON_BASE_LIMIT), `app/counter.html` (FAQ), `app/pricing.html` (JSON-LD + cancellation FAQ), `app/report.html` (Free row), `app/landing.html` (JSON-LD FAQ answer + visible pricing-rows Free card), `app/extension/STORE_LISTING.md` (free-tier line). Per-pro-feature trial (Sneaky / Opp Attack / Competition Depth / Style Memory) `canUseProFeature` in `app/index.html` reverted 5 → 1 for the same reason — each premium-feature try is a pro-brain call on an unconverted user. Pricing-surface softening from 2026-05-14 (upgrade-cta.js render conditions, usage-banner 75% threshold, jumpnav Pricing link removal) is **left in place** — the issue was the cap size, not how aggressive the pricing surfaces were. soul.md §4 + §7 rewritten; this entry supersedes the 2026-05-14 "Free tier bumped 5→15" entry above. AGENTS.md "Free tier" line + the "ask before doing" pricing-cap reference updated to match.

- **External-audit sweep: trust gaps, hero rewrite, brand-collision, bigger goals, orange stripped** (2026-05-19, multi-pass). External audit landed with both vibe critique and concrete factual gaps. Vibe stuff (homepage density, code-filename leak on landing, AI-written feel) treated as judgment calls; factual stuff swept against soul.md as canonical truth. **(1) Stale-number sweep.** `app/report.html` "4 AI brains, 16 voices" → 6 across the stat-card (line 209), Section 03 row "Four AI brains" → "Six AI brains" with DeepSeek + Open Lab added to the function list, and the §04 architecture paragraph extended with `deepseek.mjs` + `openlab.mjs`. `app/india.html:380` + `app/us.html:376` "Free = no card, 30 rounds total" → "5 anonymous + 5 on sign-in (10 total)" to match the post-2026-05-18 cap. `app/pricing.html` JSON-LD Individual offer description "four LLM brains" → "six LLM brains". **(2) Privacy-policy honesty.** `app/privacy.html:98` "We do not use Google Analytics" was false — GA4 (`G-0V4R5MY3BT`) is loaded on `index.html`, `profile.html`, `live-round.html`. Rewrote the line to disclose GA4 + Firebase Analytics + Microsoft Clarity (gated behind a project ID, not active by default) and added a Google Analytics 4 row to the sub-processor table at line 107. Linked Google's GA opt-out add-on for users who want out. **(3) Hero rewrite on `app/landing.html`.** Replaced "Debate AI. Rethink argumentation." with the more physical "Debate an AI opponent out loud." + "Pick a motion. Take a side. The AI argues back, takes POIs, interrupts, and writes the judge ballot when the round ends. Built for Asian Parli, WSDC, BP, APDA, PF, LD, Policy, Congress, MUN, plus Quick Clash." (format order leads with Asian Parli/WSDC/BP per the India lean from §2). **2026-05-23 correction:** the "AI as antagonist and tool. Not the Messiah, nor an arbitrary enemy." creed under the hero is **kept** by design (a previous version of this entry claimed it was removed; user reaffirmed 2026-05-23 that it stays). The creed lives at `app/landing.html:4560` as the founder's stance on what kind of AI this is; don't sweep it. Primary CTA "Start debating" → "Start a 3-minute round". Removed the in-hero Google sign-up button — it contradicted the "no sign-in for the first demo" wedge the audit was asking for; sign-in still surfaces via topbar + the post-2-generations nudge modal + feature-specific paywalls (so §4's advise-not-require principle is preserved). Replaced the Google-button slot with a "Watch a sample ballot" secondary CTA scrolling to `#live-proof` (the existing real-screenshot + AI-written-RFD block). The two hidden delegate IIFEs (`#heroGoogleSignup` at lines 4109 + 4774) gracefully no-op when the button is gone — they already had `if (heroBtn)` guards and fall through to `#googleSignupBtn` lower on the page. **(4) Three-answer trust block.** New section between hero and founder-video answering the audit's three "what a new visitor is actually asking" questions: Why is it different · Can I try it now · Can I trust it.
