Three equal cards, eyebrow + 1-2 sentence body each, format list leads with Asian Parli, sign-in framed as "no card, no sign-in for the first round" matching the hero's new wedge, trust answer cites "national APDA champion at UChicago" (per the §10 "no founder name on public site surfaces" memory) + 115 countries + GA disclosure + "pointed at coaches, not at homework shortcuts." **(5) "Tools" section de-jargoned.** `app/landing.html:7372-7376` previously name-dropped `<code>tts.mjs</code>`, `<code>claude.mjs</code>`, `<code>openai-chat.mjs</code>`, `<code>gemini.mjs</code>`, `<code>grok.mjs</code>`, `<code>deepseek.mjs</code>`, `<code>openlab.mjs</code>`, `<code>tts-humanize.mjs</code>`, `<code>voice-guidelines.mjs</code>` on the four feature cards. Audit reading: "debug-copy wandered onto the landing page in socks." Rewrote all four cards to keep the user-value framing (16 personas with 8 strengths/8 weaknesses, 6 brains with distinct argumentative styles, free vs premium voice tiers, format-register switching) without any filename references. The filenames still live on `/report` where investor/PM readers benefit from the architecture detail. **(6) Brand-collision title sweep.** Audit flagged that "Debate AI" alone is too generic and crowded (debateai.org, debateai.net, DebateAI.app, DeepAI's AI Debate, Symbai). Swept `<title>`, `og:title`, `twitter:title` across the 10 first-impression pages to lead with `DebateAI.com` (or qualified phrase): `landing.html` ("DebateAI.com · Voice-first AI debate sparring"), `pricing.html`, `schools.html` ("DebateAI.com for Schools · Voice-first debate practice"), `india.html`, `us.html`, `debate-ai.html`, `voice-debate.html`, `learn.html`, `counter.html`, `index.html`. JSON-LD `name`/`brand.name` fields kept as "Debate AI" — those are structured-data identity, not visible titles. Body copy across hundreds of "Debate AI" mentions left alone for now — title/og/twitter is the surface that decides search-result clickability. **(7) "Goals" section rewritten on `app/landing.html:6237`.** Previous three tactical bullets (Tournament prep, Cross-language practice, Solo practice without a partner) read as "jobs the platform does for the user," not company-scale ambitions. User explicitly asked for bigger goals. Now leads with six year-out ambitions: (i) host a flagship online tournament with AI prelims + human elims, (ii) ambassadors on every major circuit (India/UK/US/SG/PH/KE/AU + Indian school federations), (iii) bridge the language gap so Hindi-medium debaters can drill English-medium WSDC, (iv) bring varsity-grade prep where there is no varsity program, (v) make oral exams the test schools trust again via Counter + viva mode, (vi) build the largest open RFD library in debate from the rated-rounds corpus. Original three tactical jobs demoted into a "The three jobs the platform does today" sub-list underneath so the present-tense product is still surfaced. **(8) "Overstimulated?" self-aware founder-video header on `app/landing.html:5446`.** Audit + user noted the page is dense; the founder-video section now names the density at the top instead of pretending it isn't there. Eyebrow "Introducing Debate AI" → "Overstimulated?" and title "An intro with guidance" → "Same. Here's a calm intro." Reframes the video as the antidote to the surrounding density. **(9) Orange chrome stripped sitewide.** User pointed at the orange-amber CTA gradient and said get rid of it. Decorative amber gradients converted to solid brand red (or red→deep-red where dimension was needed): `app/index.html` `.nav-btn-feature` across base + light + crimson + hover variants (was `linear-gradient(135deg,#f59e0b,#ef4444)`, now solid `#ef4444` with `#dc2626` hover), focus-ring amber RGB → red RGB, `.loading-bar-fill` and inline siblings dropped the `#f59e0b` middle stop, `.judge-pool-avatar` red→amber gradient → red→deep-red, `.output .ai-h3 / .output h3` border-left + background tint `#fbbf24` → `#ef4444`, `.crimson-theme` `.output` `h3` overrides same. `app/landing.html` `.beta-strip` red→amber gradient → solid red. `app/learn.html` hero `#ef4444 → #f97316` → `#ef4444 → #dc2626`. `app/admin.html` `.brand-mark`, `.pill-btn.primary`, `.gate button`, `.bar`, `.pu-row .rank.top` all stripped of `#f97316`/`#f59e0b` stops (bar-warn at line 107 left alone — warn is a functional state). `app/css/ui.css` `.ui-beta-strip` background gradient → solid red, and the big one: `.ui-btn-voice` topbar pill (visible on every page) was gold-amber-red gradient + amber halo + dark text; now solid red + white text + brighter red halo. Pulsing white dot inside still carries the "live, talk right now" signal. `app/high-school.html` "Why Parliamentary?" eyebrow, pricing-card border, upgrade-banner gradient + border, "Upgrade →" link all flipped amber → red. Kept as functional amber/gold (not touched): rate-limit warn banners, error toasts, low-rating star ratings, achievement tier-legendary gold (semantic tier indicator), `bar-warn` admin row, "AI Fallback" spar.html status pill, voice-debate persona avatar tints (per-persona color identity), profile.html lifetime-tier gold chrome (premium tier indicator), format-color associations in `profile.html` (BP=gold, Asian Parli=orange are category colors, not decoration). Brand discipline: amber/gold now reserved for state semantics (warn, achievement, tier); red is the brand-action color across all CTAs.

- **Per-format brain training: 4 parallel multi-hour research agents → 11-commit voice-guidelines synthesis** (2026-05-19, big late-day pass). Closes the long-standing wedge claim in §3 — "Format accuracy. Policy gets tagged-card delivery. LD gets value/criterion. BP gets extensions + whip structure. APDA is impromptu with no fake citations." Until today that was true at the *minimum competence* layer but not at the *2026-current-meta* layer; every generic LLM had the same training data and missed the recent changes (Sofia 2026 WUDC manual, India's WSDC 2025 win, NDT 2026 NEG dominance, the real WPM correction 260-320 not 350-450, the Arctic topic meta, the Sofia POI mandate). **Architecture:** four background `general-purpose` research agents spent 2-3 hours each researching format clusters (Policy + LD; PF + Congress + MUN; BP + Worlds + APDA; Asian Parli + WSDC + India circuit). Each returned a structured markdown findings doc with proposed `FORMAT_VOICES` patches + new `TOPIC_PRIMERS` + real-author libraries + tournament texture + failure modes — all sourced to verified URLs (worlddebating.com, calicotab tabs, openCaselist wiki, NSDA topic announcements, Tabroom paradigms, APDA results database, ISDS, IIMUN). **Synthesized into voice-guidelines.mjs across 11 commits:** (1) BP/WUDC/APDA depth pass — Sofia 2026 manual changes (POIs MANDATORY now, generative-AI banned in 15-min prep, whip flexibility for reactive analysis, closed-round confidentiality, ranked-pairs voting), full Imperial speaker-scale bands (50-100 with explicit decent/strong/exceptional targets), motion-shape playbook (THBT/THW/THR/THS/THO/as-actor/preference), verified 2024-26 champions (Oxford Hanoi 2024, Dartmouth Panama 2025, Sydney Sofia 2026 — CRITICAL correction since most LLM priors have WUDC 2026 as Belgrade not Sofia; Belgrade was the 2022 virtual edition), tight-call full taxonomy + elite speech-position playbook for APDA, recent Nationals winners + TOTY + SOTY through 2025-26. (2) Asian Parli + WSDC depth pass + **two new FORMAT_VOICES keys** — `india_school` and `india_college`, directly targeting the 80% India traffic. india_school covers CBSE/ICSE/IB schools running Asian Parli + WSDC + Frank Anthony Memorial, tier-1 schools (DPS RKP, Vasant Valley, Shri Ram Moulsari, Emerald Heights Indore, Pathways, Cathedral Mumbai, La Martiniere), tournament list (Frank Anthony, India Today Cup, DPS RKP Outspoken, IDL, ISDC, MSDL), Indian-English register guide (KEEP invariant "isn't it" tags, syllable-timed prosody, formal salutations, "kindly note" / "do the needful" / "I would humbly submit" — don't over-correct to flat American TTS), India-context example library covering Supreme Court cases (Navtej / Puttaswamy / Sabarimala / NJC / Article 370 / CAA-NRC / Farm Laws / Agnipath / NEP 2020), South Asia (Bangladesh Hasina ouster 2024, Sri Lanka bankruptcy, Maldives Muizzu pivot, Pakistan Imran Khan / military), region-specific religion frames, non-philosophy-name-drop rule (reach for Ambedkar / Gandhi / Tagore / Sen / Nehru first, not Kant / Rawls / Mill). Hindi-language register section with core debate vocab (मुद्दा / पक्ष / विपक्ष / तर्क / सबूत), formal Hindi parliamentary openings, Lok Sabha-derived rhetorical cadence patterns, the unique-to-Hindi-vaad-vivaad interjector role, periodic SOV Hindi grammar (don't translate-from-English). india_college covers NLSIU / IIT Bombay / SRCC / NMIMS / BITS / NLU Delhi / RVCE / Ramjas / St. Stephen's circuit, both Asian Parli and BP in use, ABP as pipeline tournament. 14 India aliases added to `forFormat` synonym map ("india today cup," "frank anthony," "isdc," "outspoken," "nls debate," "abp," "iit bombay iv," "srcc gambit," "gambit" all route correctly). (3) PF + Congress + MUN depth pass — full PF speech order + times, 1st-vs-2nd speaker asymmetry, issue summary collapse, full 10 weighing mechanisms (not just 4: scope / severity / reversibility / magnitude / probability / timeframe / urgency / prerequisite / link-in / short-circuit), 4-point judge spectrum (lay / flay / flow / tech). Congress: seven speech positions as different jobs, precedence/recency PO logic, parliamentary motion catalog, 2026 docket categorized. MUN: committee types as basically different formats (GA / Crisis / Specialized / JCC), position paper grading specs (BMUN / HMUN formats), 3-ring bloc model, verified UN resolution library (UNSC 1325/2231/1373/1540/1701/2118, UNGA 2758, UN Charter Articles 2(4)/2(7)/51/Chapter VI vs VII, 1951 Refugee Convention, ICCPR/ICESCR, 1968 NPT), India circuit (IIMUN 150K+ schools, HMUN India, DAIMUN). (4) Policy + LD depth pass — actual speaking speed correction (260-320 wpm per The 3NR 2021 study, not the 350-450 software-chart myth), read-a-card SHAPE (tag → cite → body, three beats with the qualifier matters MORE in Policy than any other format), 2025-26 Arctic meta in depth (four AFF tracks Military/Cooperation/Indigenous-Env/Energy-Mining, icebreaker load-bearing factoid US 2 vs Russia 40+, ICE Pact + Trump Greenland uniqueness anchors, K-aff scene Decolonial Fugitivity/Red Deal/Cartography-K, T-shells specific to Arctic), 2025-26 Labor meta (NDT/CEDA: 37% AFF win rate in elims, K dominance, top AFFs Federal Workers / Bankruptcy / Sectoral / Gig / Moon-workers), K-aff vs T-FW pivot, personal narrative K-affs (Binghamton 2025 NDT wedge). LD: trad vs circuit audience-split rule (default trad unless circuit signal), full 11-framework bank with real anchors (Kantian Korsgaard, Util Parfit, Scanlonian, Rawlsian, Virtue Aristotle/MacIntyre, Hobbesian, Pettit, Galtung Structural Violence, Pummer Moderate Deontology, Bostrom/Ord Existential Risk, Mackie Moral Skepticism), circuit theory mechanics, 2025-26 full LD topic cycle. (5) 12 new ELITE MOVE additions to STRATEGY block (FOUR-WALL BREAK / TAX FRAME / META-COLLAPSE / ASYMMETRIC CONCESSION / UNOBVIOUS COUNTERFACTUAL / WEDGE / ENGAGEMENT TEST / ROLE INVERSION / PRINCIPLED-PRACTICAL SPLIT / CRYSTALLIZATION CALLBACK / BACKHANDED EXTENSION / BURDEN-INVERTING POI) with worked examples drawn from real recent finals. (6) **Server-side topic auto-classifier infrastructure** at `app/netlify/functions/lib/voice-guidelines.mjs` — `inferTopicFromText` with regex word-boundary + plural/gerund tolerance + body-extraction across Claude/OpenAI/Gemini shapes, conservative `MIN_TOPIC_SCORE = 4` threshold (two strong matches or one strong + one medium, or four mediums). Critical infrastructure: the entire `TOPIC_PRIMERS` system had shipped weeks ago with `FINANCE_PRIMER` defined, but the client never passed `_voiceTopic` so primers sat dormant. This auto-classifier closes the gap; primers now fire automatically when motion keywords match, no client work required. Each new primer is a two-place edit (primer const + `TOPIC_KEYWORDS` entry). (7-11) **19 TOPIC_PRIMERS total** added across the synthesis (1 pre-existing FINANCE + 5 cross-cutting strategy: ARCTIC / COLLECTIVE_BARGAINING / HEGEMONY / CAPITALISM_K / CRITICAL_PHIL_K; 6 PF monthly cycle: pf_uk_eu / pf_encryption / pf_china_extraction / pf_sports_betting / pf_zoning / pf_war_powers; 3 LD topic cycle: plea_bargaining / rewilding / nuclear_weapons; 4 cross-cutting domain: ai_governance / climate / criminal_justice / immigration). Each primer is ~30-50 lines of grounded domain context — factual anchors, real authors with works, real organizations, framework pairings, contention archetypes, weighing tilts. Test harness at `scripts/test-topic-classifier.mjs`, 53 / 0 fail. (12) JUDGE_RULES in `app/live-round.html` augmented across all 9 existing format keys + **two new keys** (wsdc + mun) with the same depth additions applied from the judging side — Sofia 2026 manual for BP / Worlds, full WUDC speaker scale bands, 2024-26 verified champions as benchmarks, WSDC India 2025 win + conferral adjudication + per-country team styles, Asian Parli definitional-debate adjudication, LD trad-vs-circuit + theory-shell adjudication, PF 1st-vs-2nd asymmetry penalties, Policy WPM-myth correction + K-aff vs T-FW evaluation framework + Arctic-topic-specific T-shells, Congress seven-speech-positions + PO ranking, full MUN committee-type evaluation framework. **Net architectural impact:** voice block grew from ~30K → ~60K chars across `FULL` system prompt. Every brain endpoint (Claude / GPT / Gemini / Grok / DeepSeek / Open Lab) now prepends FULL + format block + topic primer (auto-classified) + exemplars + distillations + user fingerprint before the LLM call. This is what "uniquely better trained than a normal LLM at debate" looks like at the prompt layer. Critical recent-meta corrections embedded that no other LLM has in its priors: WUDC 2026 = Sofia not Belgrade; Sofia 2026 manual changes (POI mandatory now); India won WSDC 2025 9-0 vs Australia; real WPM = 260-320; Trump 2025 Greenland gambit + October 2025 White House Arctic Security Cutters EO + November 2025 ICE Pact ministerial as Arctic-topic uniqueness anchors; NDT 2026 = Emory GS won, K dominance, 37% AFF win rate in elims; APDA 2026 Nationals = William & Mary surprise win, Sophie Rukin top-speaker as freshman. Commits `4ebc1df` → `bad6510`. SW `v455 → v479`. The brain-training thread in HQ ([[DebateAI — HQ]]) flips from "scoping" to "shipped at prompt layer + Aidan still needs to rate generations at /admin/rate to compound through the nightly distill loop."

- **Spar waitlist + private DM marketplace** (2026-05-20). First async community surface (progresses open question §9 #5). The /spar page was synchronous-only: you queued, waited 60s, and either matched a random human or fell through to the AI. A slow queue dead-ended you. New left rail on `app/spar.html` ("Open invites") is a one-tap **waitlist signup**: signed-in users hit "Join the waitlist" (note OPTIONAL — joining alone marks you available; a note just adds color), which writes a doc to the new `waitlist_posts` Firestore collection. Note-less signups render "Open to a round. DM to organize." Once joined, the action slot flips to a green "You're on the waitlist" confirmation + a Leave button (membership derived from the live posts feed via `myWaitlistPost()`, so no duplicate posts). Anyone signed in can browse the list and DM a waitlister privately to organize a debate on their own time. (Note: this shipped first as "post an invite" with a *required* note, then was reframed same-day to the lighter one-tap signup — the marketplace data model is identical, only the join UX got lighter.) DMs live in `dm_threads/{threadId}/messages` with `threadId = sorted([uidA,uidB]).join('_')`; thread docs carry participants + participantInfo + lastMessage + an unread counter map. Inbox button with unread badge (mirrored onto the mobile FAB), real-time `onSnapshot` on both the posts feed and the thread. Posts have a 24h client-side TTL filter (kept in Firestore until the author deletes). Read-only teaser on the signed-out gate (social proof + sign-in nudge); posting + DMing require Google sign-in (same anti-bot posture as the matchmaker). Mobile: rail becomes a slide-in drawer behind a floating FAB. Required Firestore rules (waitlist_posts + dm_threads + messages subcollection) and the composite index (`dm_threads`: participants ARRAY_CONTAINS + lastMessageAt DESC) are documented inline in spar.html above `WL_POST_TTL_MS`; both are console-only actions the rules don't ship from the repo. Same commit: the /spar gate + searching **orb** got a visual pass — was a flat red gradient blob, now a layered sphere (specular-highlight + shadow gradient for 3D, two staggered expanding ring pulses, a conic radar sweep, three orbiting satellite dots at different radii/speeds) with the live world-globe.js canvas pins still drawing on top at z-index 3. The orb reads as a rich animated sphere even if world-globe.js fails to load.

- **Voice-round rating widget moved to the ballot page** (2026-05-24). Closes the §9 #4 open follow-up on session-end rating prompts. The 1-5 star + boring + notes widget had been wired on `app/voice-debate.html` since the learning-loop pass (2026-05-13), but it was dead code in the happy path: `generateRFD` writes a payload to `sessionStorage['debateos-voice-rfd']` and navigates the user to `/voice-rfd` via `window.location.href = '/voice-rfd'`, which destroys the React state holding `lastVoiceRoundId`. `/voice-rfd` had no rating UI of its own. Net effect: every voice round that reached the ballot generated zero labels for the nightly `scheduled-distill.mjs` pass, so the voice path was running the distill loop blind — the "PATTERNS THAT WORK" block compounded only from the typed-mode generations Aidan manually rated at `/admin/rate`. Two surgical patches: (a) `app/voice-debate.html` `generateRFD` payload now carries `generationId: lastVoiceRoundId || null` and `format: mode || ''`, so the destination page knows which Firestore doc to write the rate signal onto. (b) `app/voice-rfd.html` gets a new `.rate-card` section between the ballot blocks and the CTA row, gated on `payload.generationId` AND `firebase.auth().currentUser`. Hidden by default; the auth-check IIFE waits up to 2.5s for `onAuthStateChanged` to settle before giving up (anon visitors never see it). Stars POST `action:'signal', signal:'rate', value:n` to `/api/log-generation` immediately; the boring toggle + notes textarea re-fire the rate signal with the merged meta, matching the existing widget's shape so `log-generation.mjs` needs zero changes. Notes textarea auto-surfaces when rating ≤ 2 OR boring is flagged — debounced 1.2s, plus a blur-flush belt-and-suspenders. Eyebrow swaps to "Rated N/5 — thanks" on first click so the user sees they're not shouting into a void. Amber stars + red boring toggle = state semantics, not chrome (per the 2026-05-19 amber-reserved-for-state rule). GA4 `voice_rfd_rate` event ships alongside each POST for funnel visibility. Commit `7fdefb6`. Net architectural impact: the voice path is no longer the silent partner in the distill loop. Every signed-in voice round that reaches the ballot now has a one-tap path to becoming labeled training data. The `applyDistillations` voice block per format gets stronger with each rated round, not just with each typed round Aidan rates by hand.

- **Distillation freshness on /admin** (2026-05-24, third pass). Closes the last open follow-up under §9 #4 (Learning loop). The nightly `scheduled-distill.mjs` cron writes `learning_distillations/{slug}` docs at 04:00 UTC, but until now the only signal it actually ran was digging through Netlify function logs — easy to miss a regression for weeks. New `app/netlify/functions/admin-distillations.mjs` (+ mirror) reads all 9 format docs in parallel (cheap: 9 Firestore reads/call), returns per-format age + exampleCount + model + 200-char preview + a stale flag (>36h, leaves a 12h grace window for one missed cron before the alarm rings), sorted stale-first within stale and oldest-first within fresh so a regression sorts to the top. Cached 5min via `admin-cache` so dashboard refreshes don't re-read. New "Learning loop · nightly distill" card on `/admin` renders a table with red pills for stale or missing formats, quiet greys for fresh ones, and a meta header showing "N stale · M/9 present" for at-a-glance health. Same admin-only auth pattern as the other admin-* endpoints. Commit `d7f989d`. Net: the nightly heartbeat is now visible on the dashboard you already check daily, not hidden in a log surface you only open after a regression.

- **Light-theme dashboard contrast rebuild** (2026-05-20). The Saved-tab dashboard cards in `app/debate-ai.html` (Saved cases / Round history / How I think as a debater / Practice drill / Record vs. AI) were near-invisible on light theme. Root cause: `[data-lighting="light"]` set `--c-surface: rgba(0,0,0,.025)` — a 2.5% black tint on the `#f5f5f0` cream bg, i.e. invisible cards — plus `--c-dim` at .28 alpha for dates (~2:1 contrast) and hardcoded `rgba(255,255,255,.02)` inner-row backgrounds in the JSX (white-on-cream, also invisible). The light-theme surface vars were authored as dark-on-cream tints copied from the dark theme's white-on-black logic, which only works at much higher alphas. Fix flips surface direction on light theme: surfaces are now BRIGHTER than the bg (solid `#ffffff` / `#fbfaf5`) with real borders + box-shadow elevation, dim/subtle/faint text alphas bumped to clear WCAG AA, and the hardcoded row tints swapped to `var(--c-surface2)` so they theme. New `.dash-card` class adds the elevation shadow + hover lift; `.dash-row` adds a slide-right hover (full-white fill on light). WIN/LOSS/TIE pills moved off inline colors to `.dash-pill--win/--loss/--tie` classes with theme-split palettes (light: deep red/green text on light tints; dark: light red/green on near-black tints). `.cta-ghost` light-theme override bumped from `rgba(0,0,0,.4)` text to `.72` + a faint white fill so "Open library" / "Open all" / "Analyze" read as real buttons. Dark theme untouched — only the `[data-lighting="light"]` block + the new shared classes changed. SW bumped to v508.

- **Brand renamed: DebateAI → Debatable** (2026-05-25). User acquired `debatable.com` and a few related domains. Visible brand surfaced across the site is now "Debatable" (titles, og/twitter, hero, topbar, footer, CTAs, JSON-LD `name` / `alternateName`, manifest `name`, body copy). What was deliberately NOT touched in this sweep: (a) the production domain `debateai.com` — still live in canonical URLs, og:url, sitemap entries, JSON-LD `url`, CORS allowlists, and mailto addresses. DNS migration is a separate move; flipping the domain in code before DNS would break canonical resolution + CORS overnight. (b) The Counter sub-brand at `app/extension/` — per the 2026-05-10 decision, the chrome extension ships as "Counter: Oral Exam Trainer (by Debatable)" and its own brand identity stays. The sweep updated the parenthetical attribution (was "by DebateAI") but left every other "Counter" string alone. (c) Historical decision-log entries in soul.md / AGENTS.md — those are timestamped events that describe what was true at the time; rewriting them would falsify the record. (d) Filenames like `debate-ai.html`, `debate-ai-tools.html`, `ai-debate-practice.html` — these are routes the rest of the codebase + Google's index reference; renaming them is a slug migration with redirects, not a brand rename. Net: 107 files touched, ~1230 visible mentions flipped, single commit on `chore/rename-to-debatable`. Hardcoded "Debate AI" / "DebateAI" → "Debatable" across both word forms via sed; the keyword meta-tag now shows `Debatable, Debatable, debateai.com` (harmless duplicate from the two source forms collapsing) — Google deprecated meta keywords years ago, low priority to deduplicate. Open follow-ups for the user, in rough priority order: (i) decide when to flip DNS on `debatable.com` and run a domain-migration sweep covering canonical URLs / og:url / sitemap / JSON-LD `url` / CORS allowlists / mailto; (ii) set up `feedback@debatable.com` and re-do the email sweep; (iii) update social handles (`tiktok.com/@debateai`, `instagram.com/debateai`, `twitter.com/debateai`, `linkedin.com/company/debateai`, `youtube.com/@debateai` are still old-brand in the `sameAs` JSON-LD); (iv) art/icon updates if "Debatable" wants a different logomark; (v) GSC: add `debatable.com` as a property once DNS resolves and submit the new sitemap.

---

- **Interim three-part naming system adopted, then superseded** (2026-05-26). The product briefly used one name in titles, “Debatable” as a personality word, and a two-word CTA slogan after discovering that `debatable.com` had not been acquired. That system is historical only. The 2026-07-25 decision makes **Debatable** the sole public brand at `itsdebatable.com`; old aliases and slogans must not return to visible copy, metadata, accessibility text, narration, or internal authoring guidance.

- **Expansive styles: Karl Popper + the Career trio (Courtroom / Negotiation / Pitch Defense)** (2026-06-10). Shipped the day before the Entrepreneur First meeting to make the "one engine, several markets" pitch visible in the product itself. Until now the format picker was 11 keys, all competitive prep except Viva; the professional vertical (lawyers / sales / founders) existed only as landing copy. Four new end-to-end formats: (1) **Karl Popper** ('popper') — the IDEA-circuit 3v3 prepared-resolution format dominant in Eastern Europe, the Balkans, the Caucasus, and Central Asia; 8 segments including two cross-ex periods, criterion-based judging, open citations (prepared topics mean real research is expected). Strengthens the global-circuit story next to Asian Parli / WSDC / BP. (2) **Courtroom** ('courtroom') — opening statements, cross-examination exchanges, closing arguments; AI plays opposing counsel; voice block encodes openings-preview-closings-argue, leading-question discipline, burden language, candor toward the tribunal, and a hard no-fabricated-case-law rule. (3) **Negotiation** ('negotiation') — anchor / counter-anchor, two objection rounds, concession trading, final offer, accept-or-walk; voice block encodes interests-over-positions, trade-never-give, walk-away realism. (4) **Pitch Defense** ('pitch') — 3-minute pitch then three investor pushes (market / moat / traction) and a close; voice block encodes the partner-meeting register: one push per turn, follow-the-dodge, vanity-metric allergy, reward candor with harder questions. The founder can literally rehearse an investor meeting on the product before walking into one. Client (`app/practice.html`): 4 FORMATS entries, 4 SEAT_LAYOUTS, both format-picker arrays extended to 15 keys, new blue 'Career' pill badge (`entry: 'career'`; blue = info, amber stays reserved for state per 2026-05-19), simple-mode gets a fifth "Work & Career" practice card routing to Pitch Defense. Server (`app/netlify/functions/lib/voice-guidelines.mjs`): 4 new FORMAT_VOICES blocks (14 → 18 keys) + forFormat synonyms ('karl popper', 'mock trial', 'moot court', 'sales', 'investor pitch', 'salary negotiation', etc.). Root `netlify/functions/lib/` mirror no longer exists on main, so the app copy is the single edit target. Stale "10 formats / ten formats" copy swept to 15 across debate-it (og + JSON-LD), story, future, online-debate-camp, debate-an-ai, uwc, landing infoRow, and both compare pages. Voice-debate drill MODES deliberately untouched — that surface's mode wiring is separate plumbing and the typed surface was the right scope for one pass. Live-round JUDGE_RULES also untouched (human-vs-human surface, classic formats only for now).

- **Contact email → founder Gmail sitewide + mailto chooser** (2026-07-04, Aidan's explicit call). REVERSES the 2026-06-14 email reversal: all user-facing contact points now read aidandavidhollinger@gmail.com; contact@devilsadvocateteam.com is retired everywhere in the repo (last refs were ambassadors.html + benchmark.html; most of the site had already been swept in earlier July sessions). The Google Workspace mailbox behind devilsadvocateteam.com is being cancelled (owner action), so any stale reference would BOUNCE; do not reintroduce it, and do not re-sweep back. Note: this knowingly supersedes the no-founder-name-on-site rule for the email address itself (his email contains his name); Aidan chose this. Also shipped: a mailto chooser in js/topbar.js — clicking any mailto link on the ~60 topbar pages now opens a small picker (Open in Gmail / Open in Outlook / Use my mail app / Copy address) instead of dumping visitors into whatever desktop client the OS defaults to; pages without topbar.js keep plain mailto.

- **Lifecycle email system unified: shared lib, real unsubscribe, deliverability, motion rotation** (2026-07-05). The five Resend senders (scheduled-wau-digest, scheduled-winback, send-first-round-email, notify-accepted, notify-dm-accept) each hand-rolled their own esc(), HTML shell, footer, and Resend fetch, with three different FROM defaults (aidandavidhollinger@gmail.com in three places, onboarding@resend.dev in two), no plain-text parts, no actionable unsubscribe ("Reply to opt out" was a manual flow), and inconsistent opt-out checks. Now: (1) new `lib/email.mjs` owns esc/toText/brandHeader/renderFooter/isOptedOut/unsubToken/verifyUnsubToken/unsubUrl/sendEmail; from resolves arg > EMAIL_FROM > 'Aidan at the retired brand <aidan@debateai.com>', per-function env overrides still honored; every send now carries a text/plain alternative. (2) Real unsubscribe: new `email-unsub.mjs` at /api/email-unsub, HMAC-SHA256-gated (EMAIL_UNSUB_SECRET, set on debateos1 2026-07-05), GET renders a branded confirmation page with undo + unsubscribe-from-all links, POST is RFC 8058 one-click; bulk sends (digest/winback/onboarding) carry List-Unsubscribe + List-Unsubscribe-Post headers, which Gmail/Yahoo bulk-sender rules require and which surfaces Gmail's native unsubscribe chip. Streams: digest -> wauDigestOptOut, winback -> winbackOptOut, all/onboarding -> emailOptOut (new global kill switch respected by every sender; winback keeps its legacy wauDigestOptOut||winbackOptOut behavior). Transactional sends (notify-accepted, notify-dm-accept) are exempt from bulk headers by design. (3) Digest motion bug fixed: MOTD_BY_FORMAT keys never matched the real generations format slugs (wsdc vs worlds, quickclash vs quick/clash/1v1 aliases), so every user got the same static fallback motion forever; keys realigned with a lowercase alias map and the fallback now rotates weekly via dailyMotionFor(run date) from lib/daily-motion-bank.mjs. (4) Escaping fixes riding along: previously-raw firstName in the digest H1 and firstName/side/format in the first-round email now esc'd; an em-dash in the notify-accepted headline removed. Root netlify/functions mirror confirmed VESTIGIAL for these files (no lib/ dir, imports could never resolve; app/netlify/functions is the deployed dir per both netlify.toml) but the two existing root copies were text-synced per the mirror convention. Env note: RESEND_API_KEY lives in the Netlify production context only, which is why `netlify env:list` does not show it; EMAIL_UNSUB_SECRET was added in the all context. No client files touched, so no SW bump.

- **Open Spar Night: fixed weekly live hour** (2026-07-15). The /spar liquidity problem is arrival-time spread, not traffic volume: on a small base, visitors almost never overlap inside the 60s matchmaking window, so the queue reads dead even in weeks with enough total traffic for ten rounds. Fix = concentrate demand at one fixed hour: **Wednesdays 8:00 PM ET, 90 minutes, first event 2026-07-22.** (The Des Moines cluster, the only organic liquidity to date, was already a cohort on a weekly schedule; this formalizes the mechanic, chess.com-arena style.) Shipped: `app/js/spar-night.js`, a deterministic client-side countdown (DST-safe America/New_York math, no server dependency) mounted on the landing #live-now band and the /spar waitlist rail via `data-spar-night` slots; flips to a green LIVE state during 8:00-9:30 PM ET with a "Join the queue" CTA, pre-event CTA is a recurring Google Calendar add (deliberately not a fake RSVP), GA4 `spar_night_seen` / `spar_night_click` events, `?sparnight=off` QA escape. Email: new `scheduled-spar-night.mjs` cron (Wed 13:00 UTC) emails every opted-in profile the morning of the event; dry-run unless `SPAR_NIGHT_ENABLED=1` (winback posture), only acts when the next event starts <24h out (so it stays quiet until event week), stamps `sparNightSentAt`; new `sparnight` unsub stream through lib/email.mjs + email-unsub.mjs; scheduled-winback (Wed 16:00 UTC) skips anyone spar-night-emailed the same day, preserving the one-email-per-day rule. Honesty guardrail: the live card says "Spar Night is on," never claims a full queue. Founders + first ambassadors seed the first events. ACTION: set `SPAR_NIGHT_ENABLED=1` in Netlify env before Jul 22, or the first event's email is a dry run.

- **Spar style picker + judge-paradigm notes with opponent consent** (2026-06-12). /spar's format was URL-param-only (default Quick Clash) and the AI judge ran one fixed lens for everyone. Now the searching screen carries a "Round setup" card: 8 style chips (Quick Clash / APDA / BP / Worlds / Asian Parli / LD / PF / Policy — the live-round-supported set; the Career trio stays on the AI surface) plus an optional 240-char "Judge instructions" note ("Weigh probability before magnitude. Punish drops hard."). Style switch = clean requeue (fresh doc, fresh 60s clock, URL + localStorage rewritten); the note rides the queue doc and persists across sessions. **Consent is the load-bearing piece:** a note never reaches the judge without both debaters' eyes on it. When either side of a candidate pair filed one, `spar-pair.mjs` lands the pair in a two-phase `status:'consent'` state instead of matching — both clients render an accept/pass card showing the opponent's exact note (20s auto-pass; a side with nothing to review is auto-consented; the proposer waits with a withdraw button + a silent 30s dead-peer net). Accept-by-both flips to 'matched' and collapses the notes into a name-attributed `pairedParadigm`; pass reverts both docs to 'waiting' with mutual `skipUids` (no re-propose loop, joinedAt refreshed so nobody gets stale-skipped for time spent reading) and a "{name} passed on the proposal" line on the other side. Background "Spar live" pairs (js/notifications.js has no consent surface) complete instantly and drop the notes rather than ride unconsented. Reaper sweeps stranded consent docs on the same (status, joinedAt) index; no Firestore rules changes (consent flips are all admin-SDK writes; the note lives on the user's own doc). On /live-round the agreed paradigm arrives by URL, renders under the format chips ("Judge paradigm · agreed by both"), and spreads into BOTH ballot builders as an AGREED JUDGE PARADIGM block layered on top of the adjudication core: it may shift emphasis (weighing order, speed tolerance, drop severity), it may NOT override deciding on the flow, and any instruction that names a winner or dictates scores is explicitly dead on arrival — the consent gate plus that guard is the prompt-injection defense. Anon /spar visitors get the same setup card (choices persist locally and apply on their first signed-in queue); switchFormat routes them through joinQueueAnon so the queue-less searching UI never dead-ends. **Hardening pass, same day** (after walking every join/crash/blip permutation as hostile QA): (a) ghost detection — tab close no longer deletes queue docs (the 0188953 queue-follows-you change), so proposing to a dead tab is routine; timer-fired declines now carry `auto:true` and when the peer never acted on a 25s+ proposal the server CANCELS the ghost (`cancelReason:'consent_ghost'`, which the client treats as a system cancel and auto-requeues if it was actually alive) instead of reverting it to 'waiting' with a fresh joinedAt that dressed a corpse up as the youngest doc in the queue. (b) Consent clicks got their own throttle lane — they previously shared the 600ms pair-poll throttle, so an Accept landing within 600ms of a poll POST got eaten by a 429 and the card sat dead; any failed consent POST now restores a usable card from the last snapshot. (c) The AI-fallback clock yields while a proposal is in flight (15s grace, keeps ticking rather than dying silently at 0:00) and triggerAIFallback hard-guards on inConsent. (d) Phase-1 proposals re-stamp joinedAt so the reaper sweeps a dead pair together, never one half. (e) The "{name} passed" notice holds 7s against tryMatch repaints; a tab-title nudge fires while a 20s decision is pending.

- **Lifetime tier removed from all pricing displays** (2026-07-03). The public pricing surface is Free, BYOK, Individual, and Team. Existing backend lifetime entitlements remain valid for accounts that already hold one, but the plan no longer appears in user-facing pricing, upsells, comparison pages, or legal copy.

- **Live voice upgraded to gpt-realtime-2.1 + reasoning effort** (2026-07-09). OpenAI shipped `gpt-realtime-2.1` and `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini` on 2026-07-06 (~25% lower p95 voice latency via better caching, steadier interruption handling, better alphanumeric/silence handling, and a new configurable `reasoning: { effort }` on the session — `minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`, default `low`). Endpoints and body shape are UNCHANGED from `gpt-realtime`, so this was a drop-in. All three Realtime minters now put `gpt-realtime-2.1` at the front of the model try-list with `gpt-realtime` / `gpt-realtime-2` / `gpt-4o-realtime-preview` as fallbacks: `realtime-session.mjs` (the /voice-debate opponent + /newvoice, which is the landing's primary "Adaptive voice" CTA), `coach-session.mjs`, and `room-judge-session.mjs`. Reasoning effort is attached in the GA mint body ONLY when the negotiated model matches `^gpt-realtime-2\.1` (older models 400 on the field, which would drop us to the legacy path); the server also returns `reasoningEffort` so the client mirrors it in its post-connect `session.update` (guarded — never sent to a model that lacks it, since a bad field 400s the whole update and would drop turn_detection, the mid-speech-interruption feature). Default effort is `low` to keep the realtime path snappy; the main opponent nudges to `medium` on the top smartness tiers (World Champ / Council), and everything is tunable without redeploy via `OPENAI_REALTIME_REASONING_EFFORT` (plus `OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL=gpt-realtime-2.1-mini` to trade depth for the cheaper $10/$20 vs $32/$64 audio pricing). Training-log `model` labels on the voice paths now record the actually-negotiated model instead of the hardcoded `gpt-realtime`. Verified: all three functions pass `node --check`, both inline-React and plain-JS client blocks parse. AGENTS.md's "OpenAI Realtime API reference" section + file map updated with the 2.1 models, the reasoning-effort field, and the new env var. SW cache auto-bumped by the pre-commit hook (client files touched: voice-debate.html, newvoice.html, practice.html).

- **Learning loop wired into the live voice opponent + newvoice turn-taking tuned** (2026-07-09). Two things, same push. (1) **Loop into voice.** The nightly learning loop (admin-weighted REFERENCE ROUNDS from `exemplars.mjs` + the per-format LEARNED PATTERNS distillation from `distillations.mjs`) fed the typed brains since 2026-05-13 but NOT the realtime voice path — so the live opponent captured every round into `generations` yet never read any back, and didn't compound. Now `realtime-session.mjs` pulls both, in parallel with the AI council (so session start pays max(), not sum), gated on feature `'opponent'` (the closest match in both helpers' allowlists — `'bot'` isn't in either) and keyed on `loopFormat = mode` so read ↔ capture stay symmetric. **Split by stack:** the full training stack (/voice-debate) gets BOTH exemplars + distillations; the deliberately-lean clash stack (/newvoice, `mode==='clash'`) gets distillations ONLY — full reference rounds would bloat the lean prompt and drag the model toward tournament-speak, which clash mode is built to avoid. Distillations are a compact pattern block, safe on both. Both helpers return '' gracefully until a doc exists, and work for anon callers, so the voice opponent now compounds from rated rounds the same way the typed side does. (2) **newvoice turn-taking**, per Aidan looking at a live round: response felt laggy and the AI interrupted too easily. newvoice runs a custom turn engine (`create_response:false` + `interrupt_response:false`, replies driven by a silence timer + spoken-handoff phrases, barge-in gated by a sustained-loudness meter). Dropped `TURN_SILENCE_MS` 3000 → 1500 (the AI now answers ~1.5s after you go quiet instead of 3s; handoff phrases still answer instantly), and decoupled the two mic thresholds that were conflated into one `BARGE_LEVEL`: a low `SPEECH_LEVEL` (0.12) keeps the user's turn alive / holds the AI off, while a higher `BARGE_LEVEL` (0.22) is what it now takes to cut the AI off mid-answer — so a cough or "mm" no longer steals the floor, only clearly-louder sustained speech does. voice-debate.html's separate server_vad path (silence_duration_ms via `vadSilenceMs(mode)`, interrupt_response:true) was left as-is; this tuning is newvoice-specific. Verified: `realtime-session.mjs` passes `node --check`, newvoice's inline script parses. SW cache auto-bumped (client file touched: newvoice.html).

- **Leaderboard seeds reintroduced — as Firestore docs this time** (2026-07-18, Aidan's call, REVERSES the 2026-05-25 "real entries only" decision). With the landing's new ranked band surfacing the leaderboard on the scroll, Aidan asked to fill the near-empty board (1 real entry) with believable seed data so the surface reads alive. 48 docs written directly to `leaderboard_entries` (ids `seed_v2_01..48`, fields `seed:true, seedV:2`) via the Firestore REST API — NOT the old client-side `community-seed.js` merge, which was removed 2026-05-25 and stays removed. Doc shape mirrors real entries (uid / displayName first-name+last-initial / score as 30-scale speaker points 26.3-29.5 / won / kind live-or-voice / format + formatName / completedAt recency-skewed minutes-to-3-weeks), so both /leaderboard (whose `seed:true` render path with synthetic W/L + streak texture survived the 2026-05-25 removal) and the landing band's /api/leaderboard-top display them with zero code changes. Name mix is global per the 2026-05-21 reframe (India well represented, not dominant). Live-kind seeds only use the 8 spar-supported formats. Real entries outrank seeds naturally as ranked rounds land (a real 29.6 tops the board). **Cleanup path when real activity outpaces the bank: one query — `leaderboard_entries.where('seed','==',true)` — deletes the whole pool; the seeding script is idempotent (fixed doc ids) at scratchpad `seed-leaderboard.mjs`, re-runnable if scores need refreshing.** Landing-band `fmtScore` switched to one decimal to match the speaker-points scale. The seeds are unlabeled on the public surface (Aidan's explicit call: believable, not previewed) — the 2026-05-25 honesty framing is superseded for the board itself; the empty-state pitch remains in code for the no-data fallback.

- **Page narration: the site can explain itself out loud** (2026-07-22). Any content page can now be listened to instead of read, and the narration keeps playing while the visitor navigates the rest of the site. A pill sits bottom-left on 41 content pages; press it and a calm ElevenLabs voice explains what the page is, what you can do there, and where to go next. **It is an explainer, not a screen reader.** Reading the markup aloud would have voiced nav labels, table cells, and CTA fragments; instead Claude writes a ~170-word spoken script per page and that is what gets voiced. Full transcript is one tap away in the player, which is also the accessibility answer for anyone who wants the text. **Architecture: pre-generated, not live.** `/api/tts` is capped 60/min and 100/day per IP and one page is far more than one call, so a live path would have cut listeners off after roughly five pages AND billed ElevenLabs for every anonymous visitor, which is precisely the drain shape the 2026-05-18 credit-burn audit was about. Instead `scripts/generate-narration.mjs` builds `app/audio/narration/<slug>.mp3` + a manifest once per page per rewrite; serving is then free and unmetered forever. 41 pages, 64 kbps mono-grade speech, ~17 MB total (the ElevenLabs 128 kbps default would have put 36 MB of binaries in git permanently for no audible gain). **Continuity across navigation** is the actual feature and the hard part: the site is a multi-document app, so `<audio>` dies on every page load. The player writes `{slug, time, playing}` to sessionStorage on every tick and resumes on the next document, with the mp3 already in the SW cache, so the listener hears a seam rather than a restart. Honest limitation: browsers gate autoplay per document, so the resume can be refused; when it is, the pill shows a one-tap Resume instead of failing silently. Chrome generally stops refusing once the visitor has played media on the origin a few times, Safari tends to keep asking. **The generator lints its own output before spending on audio**, and this earned its keep immediately: the first pass produced "no card is required" on /landing and /pricing (friction-selling, banned) and "lock in these rates before beta ends" on /pricing, which was a **fabricated promise** — no such commitment exists anywhere on the page. Both were caught pre-synthesis and regenerated. The lint blocks em-dashes, banned phrases, invented urgency or scarcity, and friction-selling. Do not remove it: a bad line here ships as an MP3, which is far harder to notice than a bad line of text. Loaded via `topbar.js` so one edit covers all 63 topbar pages; the app surfaces (a round in progress, profile, leaderboard, messages) are deliberately excluded because narration there would talk over the product instead of explaining it. **Known maintenance edge: nothing watches for copy drift.** Editing a narrated page does not regenerate its narration, so the spoken version silently ages. Re-run the builder when a page changes meaningfully, and bump `CACHE_NAME` in both `sw.js` files after, because narration is served cache-first.

- **Page narration extended to 62 pages, plus three fixes to what already shipped** (2026-07-22, same day as the original). Added 21 pages: product and integration surfaces (/judge, /team, /early, /twitch-plugin, /zoom-plugin, /counter, /oral-exam-prep, /benchmark, /atlas), lobbies and listings (/live, /spar, /debate-chat, /leaderboard, /livedebates, /float, /floor), and the search-entry pages (/ai-debate-practice, /debate-ai-tools, /debate-case-generator, /debate-topic-generator, /prediction-market-debate). Pages with no topbar carry their own `<script defer src="/js/read-aloud.js">`, since `topbar.js` is what loads the player everywhere else. **Three things the first pass got wrong, all caught by testing this batch rather than by a user:** (1) **Narration followed listeners into live rounds.** Playback deliberately survives navigation, which meant someone who pressed play on /spar and then got matched would have had a narrator talking over their actual debate. New `SILENT_ROUTES` in read-aloud.js stops playback and clears the resume state on the live-round surfaces (/live-round, /voice-debate, /newvoice, /room-judge, /casual-room, /voice-rfd, /practice) and on pages that make their own sound (/coach, /exhibition, /spectate). Lobbies that hand off to a round are still narrated; the target is what's silenced. Any new audio-playing page must be added to that list. (2) **Thin pages produced invented narration.** Pages that render their body with JS scrape as a skeleton, and the model filled the gap instead of failing: /spar extracted **72 characters** and shipped a narration announcing the feature was "still loading, check back soon"; /high-school extracted 192 and invented a national champion "who coaches high schoolers"; /scale is a meta-refresh redirect stub that got narrated at all. Fix is a `MIN_PROSE` gate at 700 characters plus an optional per-page `hint` carrying a human-verified description that outranks the scrape (/spar and /high-school have one); /scale and /predict were dropped. (3) **Three live pages carried a stale brand name** ("the retired brand" on /research, /benchmark, /high-school) because the prompt never stated the brand. Brand is now a named `BRAND` constant, prompted and linted, so the next rename is one edit and the audio cannot silently disagree with the site. Lint also gained loading-state, empty-state, spelled-out-URL ("chrome colon slash slash extensions" on /counter) and preface rules; auditing the existing bank against the new rules found 7 pages needing regeneration, 3 of them already live. **Standing lesson: changing a lint rule does not re-check what already shipped** — audit the manifest after any rule or brand change. **A seventh problem surfaced only after deploying: six of the newly added pages were legacy SEO URLs that now 301 elsewhere (/ai-debate-practice, /debate-ai-tools, /debate-case-generator and /debate-topic-generator to /practice or /topics, /prediction-market-debate to /landing, /debate-chat to /spar), so their narration was unreachable the moment it shipped.** Removed, and the builder gained `--check-routes`, which hits production and reports any route that redirects away or 404s. Run it before adding pages. Final count 56 pages, ~24 MB. Deliberately still excluded: per-user pages (/profile, /messages, /communication-profile), /changelog (turns over faster than anyone will rebuild it), internal tooling, and **legal text (/terms, /privacy) — a spoken summary of legal wording invites a gap between what someone heard and what the page says, so those stay text-only on purpose.**

- **Live-round left pane rebuilt video-first, plus a hidden between-speech floor judge** (2026-07-22). Two coupled changes on /live-round, per outside advice that the surface read clanky and buried the actual video call. (1) **Video is now the hero of the left pane.** The old pane stacked a large decorative "room stage" panel ABOVE the video: kicker + title + explainer sub-line + green "Live room" pill + two 52px seat cards + a dashed audience row, roughly 200px of copy-heavy chrome before the call started, duplicating the roster cards already in the right panel. All of it is gone. The Daily call shell now takes the full pane height, and the only chrome left is a slim **stage rail UNDER the video**: two compact nameplates (initials avatar, name, side, red speaking pulse — same `stagePro*`/`stageCon*` ids so `updateRoomStage` needed no rewiring) flanking a center **AI-judge chip** (⚖ AI Judge, gold, with a slow-pulsing "Listening in" status that flips to "Writing the ballot" in the ballot phase). The chip is the visible expression of "two humans debate, the AI listens in" — the judge reads as a quiet third party in the room rather than a feature buried in the ballot screen. Pane padding 18→12px. The rail hides <1000px (mobile keeps the fixed-height video tile + panel roster, unchanged), and stays hidden in the docked-mini and pop-out modes which have their own judge affordances. (2) **Floor judge, built but NOT public.** A real-time third-party judge that comments BETWEEN speeches — sideline color in a card that slides up from the judge chip over the bottom of the video. Hard rules in the prompt: 2-3 sentences max, never calls a winner, never gives scores, names the strongest thing the speaker just did + the biggest hanging thread + what the next speaker must answer, format lens from the first JUDGE_RULES lines. v1 rides the existing transcript pipeline: on `endSpeech` (skipped/empty speeches excluded, spectators never generate) the acting debater's client sends the round-so-far to `/api/claude` (sonnet, 220 tokens), renders the remark, and syncs it through a `floorRemark` field on the round doc so the opponent and spectators render it too. Remarks auto-clear the moment the next speech goes live (both the local `startSpeechTimer` and the off-side `applyRemoteTimer` mirror path). **No UI toggle exists anywhere; OFF by default.** Enable per client with `?judge=live` on the URL or `localStorage['lr-floor-judge']='1'` — both debaters need the flag to both see it, which is the point while it's unannounced. Analytics: `live_round_floor_remark`. **v2 upgrade path (planned, not built):** mint a listen-only OpenAI Realtime transcription session from `/api/realtime-session` and feed it the room audio so the judge hears the actual speeches instead of the typed/mic transcript and can react with sub-second latency — same render surface, same round-doc sync, only the input changes; the plan is written into the module comment in live-round.html. When this goes public it should launch as an instrumented A/B per the landing-experiments norm, with a per-round toggle both debaters consent to (same consent shape as the /spar judge-paradigm handshake, 2026-06-12).

- **Selectable judge styles, paradigms, and brains on /judge** (2026-07-25). The standalone listen-or-paste judge previously hardcoded Claude Sonnet and mentioned paradigms only in a non-functional explainer below the FAQ. It now separates three choices: (1) judge style, the audience lens (match format / lay / flow / technical / communication); (2) decision paradigm (automatic / tabula rasa / policymaker / stock issues / games player / truth seeker / hypothesis tester / communication / custom); and (3) AI brain (Claude / GPT / Gemini / Grok / DeepSeek / Open Lab). Defaults remain one-tap and beginner-safe: Match the format + Choose for me + Claude. The selected style and paradigm are injected as bounded judge instructions, custom text cannot name a winner or dictate points, and the ballot labels the exact combination used. New indexable `/judge-paradigms` guide explains the choices in plain language, links public Tabroom examples as methodology, and deep-links each preset into `/judge`. The six brains now share the same server-side `ADJUDICATION_CORE`: new `applyAdjudicationForFeature` in `lib/adjudication.mjs` gives non-Claude proxies the judging block Claude already injected through its cached-prefix assembly. Alternative brains remain signed-in-plan features and return a clear sign-in/plan error; Claude is the public default.

- **Audience cameras: verified spectators can be seen, never heard** (2026-08-10). Debaters could see a watcher COUNT and nothing else, so a live round felt like speaking into a void even with people in it. Spectators can now opt into appearing on camera in the same Daily room, and the whole design is built around the fact that inviting strangers' cameras into a room with minors in it is the part that can go badly. **The mic is not a setting, it is an impossibility.** `/api/audience-cam` mints a Daily meeting token with `permissions.canSend:['video']`; the refusal to publish audio happens at Daily's media server, so a hostile client running patched JavaScript still cannot open a mic. A UI toggle would have been a promise; this is a property. **Five gates before a token is issued:** (1) NAMED account only, because Firebase anonymous accounts are free and unlimited to mint (see the 2026-07-28 rate-limit entry) and an anonymous camera is an unaccountable camera, so `sign_in_provider != 'anonymous'` is required; (2) Cloudflare Turnstile, which **fails CLOSED**: with `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` / `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` unset the entire feature is unavailable and the client never renders the camera button, because shipping a stranger-camera feature with its human check quietly skipped is the one degradation that is not acceptable here (a configured-but-unreachable Cloudflare is a different case and fails OPEN, since the other four gates hold); (3) `video_bans`, using the SAME salted-uid-and-IP derivation as create-daily-room, so a strike earned as a debater blocks the camera here too; (4) a rate limit of 12/hour and 40/day per uid through the shared `lib/rate-limit.mjs`; (5) a cap of 4 concurrent cameras per round, counted server-side from fresh `camAt` heartbeats on the watchers subcollection (the same 75s liveness window the watch count uses, so it needs no new index and no new collection). On top of the token, the camera runs the SAME on-device `nsfw-guard.js` watchdog debater cameras run, with the identical two-consecutive-samples trip and scores-only reporting. **The debaters hold a kill switch.** `allowAudienceCams` on the round doc, flipped from a third option in the existing visibility menu, defaults on; false makes the server refuse new tokens AND drops live audience cameras client-side through the round snapshot every client already holds. Participant-only by the existing `live_rounds` update rule, so no rules change was needed; `camAt` rides the watcher's own doc, which the existing watchers rule already scopes to `watcherUid == request.auth.uid`. **CPU was a first-class constraint, not an afterthought,** because four extra faces land on the same machines already running a Daily call, an avatar canvas at 24fps, and a TF.js classifier every 1.6s. Audience cameras publish at 320x240/12fps (texture, not broadcast), and every audience tile is pinned to the lowest simulcast layer via `updateReceiveSettings` on BOTH the debater and audience frames, matched by the `Audience · ` user_name prefix the token stamps. Daily's prebuilt layout promotes the active speaker and an audience token can never send audio, so a downgraded audience tile can never be the thing anyone is trying to look at. **Bug found while sizing this, and it was live:** `max_participants` was 8 with a comment calling it a flood guard for debaters, but every SPECTATOR joins the same Daily room receive-only, so the ceiling was really two debaters plus six watchers and the seventh person to open any live round got a full room. Raised to 24; flooding is handled where it belongs (unlisted rounds, the admission gate, and this function's own cap of 4). Because create-daily-room is create-or-fetch, existing rooms keep the old ceiling until they expire, within 24h. Same pass: `/floor`'s server poll and `/arena`'s three demo-stage tickers now skip hidden tabs, matching what every other timer on those pages already did. **Ops: audience cameras are OFF on arrival and stay off until four env vars exist** (`DAILY_API_KEY`, `DAILY_DOMAIN`, `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`). Setting the two Turnstile keys is what switches the feature on, with no redeploy. **Not built:** audience-camera moderation from the debaters' side beyond the kill switch (no per-face eject yet, the round-wide switch is the blunt instrument for now).

- **Anonymous analytics keyed by device, not by browser tab; visitor counter un-padded and un-broken** (2026-08-10). Nothing in the signed-out path had a stable identity, which made the anon half of every dashboard a count of TABS. `log-event.mjs` minted its synthetic uid as `anon:` + the per-tab sessionStorage id, so a returning visitor, a second tab, and a reload after a crash were three different "anonymous users" — and anon retention was therefore unmeasurable by construction, for the population that dominates traffic while retention is the stated #1 problem (§8). The durable id already existed (`localStorage._da_aid`, minted by track.js and sent as `metadata.anon_id` for the funnel-join work); the server simply never keyed on it. Now it does, falling back to the session id when localStorage is blocked (Safari ITP, in-app browsers) so those visitors degrade to the old per-tab behavior instead of vanishing, with `deviceKeyed` on the row so a reader can tell a real device from a fallback. **DISCONTINUITY, deliberate: unique anon counts step DOWN from this date.** That is the correction, not a regression; do not compare a window that spans it. /admin relabels the funnel from "uids" to "subjects" and defines a subject as one account OR one anon device, with the date noted in the help panel. **Two things about `/api/visitor-tick` found while verifying, both older than this change.** (1) Its cumulative `count` was padded with a hardcoded `BASELINE = 7074` from the ad-spike era, floored so the number could never read below it. That is a claims-discipline problem on a public surface, so the pad is now subtracted back out on read; the stored doc is left intact as the record of what it used to claim. Un-padded, the true all-time figure is **7**. (2) The whole function had been DEAD for months: per-day counters were addressed as `metrics/daily/{YYYY-MM-DD}`, three segments, which Firestore reads as a collection path and rejects as a document, so `db.doc()` threw on construction and every read and write went to the catch. No daily doc was ever written, `count30d` was always 0, and `public-join-history`'s `totalVisits` was structurally pinned at 0 — which is why /community always rendered its static `data-baseline` fallback. `public-join-history.mjs` already carried a TODO diagnosing this precisely ("has been silently throwing for months") and proposing the flatten; taking that fix, `metrics_daily/{YYYY-MM-DD}`. Nothing to migrate, since the old path never held data. Caught by probing the deployed endpoint rather than by reading it: it answered `source: baseline-fallback`, which only happens in the catch. **A tick now also requires a `deviceId` to increment**, because the "already counted" marker lives in the same localStorage a blocking browser refuses, so those visitors were re-ticking on every page load; they are now undercounted instead, which is the honest direction to be wrong in for a number we might quote. **Standing lesson: a metric that renders a plausible number is not evidence that it works.** Both of these shipped a believable figure to a public page for months — one padded, one frozen — and neither surfaced as an error anywhere.

- **A round room does not open until both debaters prove they are there** (2026-08-11). Measured across all 411 live rounds ever created: only ~85 had both people actually present, 215 had exactly one, 111 had neither. 390 never completed a single speech, and median room lifetime was 1.5 minutes. Debater names are written at MATCH time, not on arrival, so a round document looked complete while one chair had been empty the whole time, and every downstream number inherited the lie. **The round itself was never the problem: past the first speech, ~43% of rounds finish (9 of 21).** We were matching people against nobody, and the person who DID show up sat alone and left. That is the retention story, and it is not a top-of-funnel problem. **Two causes, both fixed.** (1) The consent gate from 2026-06-12 only fired when someone filed a judge-paradigm note or the two sides proposed different formats; every other pair flipped straight to `matched` with no evidence anyone was at a keyboard. It now fires for EVERY foreground pair, and both `consents` start false. The old rule auto-yessed the side with nothing to review, which is exactly the side that could be an empty chair: a debater who filed no note was consented into a room without ever proving presence. (2) **This supersedes the 2026-06-15 "B" auto-accept tuning.** That change made the deciding card auto-JOIN at zero when the tab was visible, so a present-but-slow user wouldn't lose a rare match. Its own comment named the risk exactly right ("accepting there would strand the opponent alone") and then trusted `visibilityState` to rule it out. It does not: a foregrounded tab nobody is sitting at is `visible`, so for 15 seconds an empty chair could accept a match. Timeouts now always auto-PASS, which routes into the ghost path already built in the 2026-06-12 hardening (server cancels the silent side, requeues the live one on a clean doc). Trading a few extra matches for rooms that open onto nobody is a bad trade at this liquidity, because the person we lose is the one who showed up. A pure presence check gets its own card ("X is ready" / "I'm here, start the round") rather than the review card, which is worded for weighing something; notes and cross-format pairs keep their review cards, and background "Spar live" pairs still skip the gate because `js/notifications.js` has no card to render. **Deliberately NOT built, because it already existed:** the in-room dead-opponent rescue. `live-round.html` has carried a 28s grace, a "Looking for your debater" strip, and a no-show prompt (Message them / Find another / Leave / Keep waiting) for a while. It worked fine. It was simply being handed rooms that never had two people in them. **Lesson: a record written at intent time is not evidence of the thing happening.** Both debater names, the room id, the motion and the format were all populated and correct on 411 rounds; not one of those fields meant anyone had arrived.

- **The nightly learning loop has never once run, and the claim came off the pitch** (2026-08-11). `learning_distillations` holds zero documents, and not because the cron is broken. `scheduled-distill.mjs` requires `MIN_EXAMPLES = 3` per format drawn from generations rated ≥4 or saved. Live counts: 153 generations captured (85 in the last 30 days, still writing today), **0 rated ≥4, 2 saved, 1 carrying any rating at all.** With three labels ever created across eighteen formats, every format returns `skipped` every night, permanently. `NIGHTLY_PAUSED` is not set; the job runs and finds nothing. The newest rating signal is 2026-05-23, one day BEFORE the /voice-rfd rating widget shipped, so that widget has produced zero labels in its entire life. So §3 wedge #5's "the AI on motion X today is meaningfully different from the AI on motion X last month" has never been true. Capture works; the learn-back half has never produced an artifact. `report.html` (investor-facing) and `landing-classic.html` no longer assert it: they now say the loop is built to sharpen from rated rounds and state plainly that no learned-pattern block has shipped into a prompt to date. **Do not restore the flat claim until `learning_distillations` actually holds documents.** Sequencing note, and it is the reason not to chase this directly: ratings are downstream of FINISHED rounds, and rounds were not finishing (see the ready-check entry above). Fix arrival, then completion, and labels become possible; going hunting for labels first would be treating a symptom.

- **Free entry finished: the surfaces caught up with the decision, and the metering bug behind it** (2026-08-11). Paid tournament entry was retired on 2026-08-10 in `entry-checkout.mjs`, but only the endpoint learned about it, so for a day the site sold a $20 door that answered `410 ENTRY_IS_FREE`. **What was still live:** `/tournaments` rendered "Paid entry / $20 / Pay in with card" because the paid door was hidden only by a JS branch that ran when `entryFeeCents` was 0, and the doc still carried 2000; the same page's FAQ and JSON-LD already said "There is no entry fee and no card" beside it; and `/tournament-rules`, the published rules of an $850 cash contest, still said free entry was "Not eligible for cash prizes" and that an entry is prize-eligible "if it paid in or was granted a fee waiver". That last one is the one that mattered: it told a free entrant who wins first place that the title is theirs and the $500 goes to someone else, which is the opposite of the decision, on the document a dispute would be argued from. **The fix that generalizes: the paid door was DELETED from the markup, not hidden, and no copy on either page reads `entryFeeCents` at all.** Hiding on a data value is precisely what let stale data resurrect a payment promise; with one entry model there is nothing for stale data to flip. Rules rewritten so eligibility turns on placement and age and nothing else, section 6 became "Cost" (no fees, so nothing to refund), and the liability clause no longer caps at an entry fee nobody pays. Verified by serving the pages the REAL live doc with its fee still at 2000: no pay button, no `$20`, one door. Then the doc itself was corrected (`entryFeeCents` 2000 → 0, description rewritten off the "$20 pay-in makes your entry prize-eligible" line that `/tournament` renders as its lede); `paidEntries` was 0 at the time, so no money was stranded. **Same session, the bug that started it:** `/practice` metered one free credit per `callAI` rather than per ROUND, and a Quick Clash round makes ~19 calls, so at `ANON_LIMIT = 1` a guest could never finish their first round. The second call threw the quota error, which rendered mid-debate as "AI couldn't respond. Guest preview used. Sign in..." and then auto-advanced through empty speeches into a ballot judging nothing. Now a round charges once at `beginRoundCredit()` in `goToSpeeches` and everything inside it runs free through the ballot; out of credit shows the CTA before the round starts instead of breaking it halfway. **Do not "fix" future quota complaints by raising the caps** (1 anon / 10 signed-in) — those numbers are correct as rounds and were set deliberately on 2026-05-18. Two riders found the same way: anonymous Firebase accounts were being read as signed-in on `/practice` (`js/notifications.js` mints one for the background Spar pill on every page), which gave guests the signed-in cap, suppressed every sign-in nudge, saved rounds under a uid they can never sign back into, and sent anonymous tokens to the account endpoints; and the prep-timer auto-advance effect had an empty body, so prep counted to 0:00 and sat there looking frozen. **Standing lesson from both halves: a surface that renders a plausible screen is not evidence the rule behind it is right.** The $20 button looked like a working checkout, and the round looked like it was running, right up to the point where each contradicted a decision that had already been made.

- **Spectator search: /watch was 140 crawlable words, and a replay had no URL of its own** (2026-08-12). Aidan asked to rank for "watch people debate". The audit found the technical base already good (thoughtful robots.txt, dynamic sitemaps with honest lastmod, ~6,000 static words on the homepage) and the failure concentrated in exactly the surface built for that query. **Two problems, one per layer.**
  - **/watch promised video and delivered a spinner.** 140 static words wrapped around a grid filled by JS from a corpus that is EMPTY: 0 recordings, 0 clips, 1 published round sitewide. A crawler that skipped the fetch saw a page advertising replays and rendering "Loading replays…", which is the shape Google's helpful-content system demotes, and a human who searched for a debate to watch hit a dead end. Now ~750 words of plain HTML (three ways to watch, how to get into a live round, what a round actually is, 7 questions with FAQPage markup verbatim against the visible copy) that carries the page whether or not the corpus has anything in it, and an empty state that routes to /live and /spar and says WHY it is empty (recording is opt in).
  - **The competitive read is the reason this was worth doing, and it inverted the obvious plan.** theChatStage ranks for the same query with roughly 400 words of plain copy and our exact model (matched opponent, anonymous spectators). They are not winning on a video library, so this is winnable on content rather than on inventory. Debatable was already ranking for spectator long-tail, but via the HOMEPAGE, because /watch was too thin to be the answer.
  - **A recording had no address.** Sharing one handed out `/watch?r={id}`, a query string on a page whose canonical is bare `/watch`, so the equity from every share piled onto the hub and not one replay existed as a crawlable URL. New server-rendered `/w/{id}` per published recording, own canonical, VideoObject. **The thing that made the markup possible: `/w/{id}/play`,** a stable path that mints a fresh Daily link per request and 302s to it. The signed URL behind it expires within the hour and could never be published as `contentUrl`; the address in front of it never moves. Playback needs no JavaScript as a result, since `<video src>` follows the 302 itself, Range requests included. `sitemap-recordings.xml` joins the index and falls back to /watch while empty, since a zero-entry urlset reads as a broken sitemap in Search Console. Replay cards became anchors (crawlable path, cmd-click opens the standalone page, plain click still plays inline) and Copy link hands out `/w/{id}`.
  - **What was deliberately NOT emitted, and it is the same discipline as the /reviews entry above.** No VideoObject or ItemList on /watch itself: there is no video there, and marking up media a visitor cannot find is what earns a manual action rather than a rich result. It belongs on a per-recording page, which is why one now exists.
  - **The thumbnail gap, and why YouTube closes it.** `/w/{id}` shipped valid VideoObject that was NOT rich-result eligible, because Google wants a thumbnail representing the video and the fallback is the brand OG card; nothing in the pipeline produces a still and there is no image library here to make one. A `youtubeId` on the recording doc (set from a yt button on the /admin row, accepting any pasted YouTube URL form) swaps in YouTube's real per-video still, adds `embedUrl`, and renders the nocookie player so watch time lands on the channel instead of being split from it. `contentUrl` still points at `/w/{id}/play`, because the Daily mp4 is the authoritative full round. With no id the behavior is byte-for-byte the old one, which is the state of every recording today. The id is validated against `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{11}$` **at render, not only at write**, because it is interpolated into an iframe src and into structured data.
  - **Honest limit, stated so nobody re-litigates it later:** this makes /watch competitive for "watch debates online" style queries. It will not beat Open to Debate or the Jubilee results for the raw head term, because that half of the SERP is a video carousel and that is YouTube's game, not a page's. The remaining lever is inventory: recordings have to exist, and that needs debaters to opt in, not code.

- **Watch hub shipped: owner livestream, round replays, clips at /watch** (2026-08-10). Ports the WS2 streaming work forward from a branch that had fallen ~1150 commits behind main; a re-apply against the current brand and architecture, not a merge. **Shipped:** five Netlify functions (`stream-control` admin start/stop of a Daily owner_only_broadcast room; `stream-status` public read, CDN-cached 15s, one doc read; `recordings` public replay index + short-lived playback links; `recordings-admin` sync-from-Daily + publish toggles, auto-publishes finished recordings over 45s; `clips` signed-in timestamped windows into published recordings, 3s-10min, 30/user/day), the `/watch` page (live band, replay grid, clip maker, deep links `/watch?r=` and `/watch?clip=`), `/replays` as an alias, /admin cards to actually go live and manage recordings, a landing live band that injects ABOVE the hero only while a stream is on (off air the page is byte-for-byte untouched, so hero A/B arms and the See-more gate never see it), and a "Watch streams and replays" action in the stream-it section, which is /watch's first in-content inbound link. `/watch` joined the sitemap (daily, 0.84; /replays deliberately not listed, same document) and read-aloud's SILENT_ROUTES (it plays video). **Deliberately NOT ported:** Phase 5's live-round viewer points — main rebuilt spectating with admission gates and presence-folded counting since the branch diverged, so audience scoring needs designing against that model, not patching over it. **Salvage audit of the rest of the stale branch:** most of it had already been re-done on main by parallel sessions; the auth-modal Google-only simplification and gmail FROM defaults were REJECTED as regressions (main has first-class email/password since 2026-07-25, and a gmail From on Resend silently 403s per the sender-domain rule); `token_balances` / `tournament_contacts` Firestore rules were left behind because nothing on main consumes those collections. **Two riders:** `/reviews` lost its Review/reviewBody JSON-LD (self-serving review markup is barred by Google's structured-data policy and risks a sitewide markup-trust discount; the visible testimonials stay, an inline comment guards against re-adding), and `firebase.json` gained the `storage` block so `firebase deploy` actually ships `app/storage.rules`, which had been undeployable since Storage was enabled 2026-07-22. **Ops note:** going live requires `DAILY_API_KEY`; recordings require a Daily plan with cloud recording (stream-control retries room creation without recording so the stream itself survives a plan without it).

- **App Check hard-enforced, and the anonymous per-IP ceiling raised behind it** (2026-08-18). Load-readiness pass ahead of an expected traffic spike. `APP_CHECK_REQUIRED` was `false` in the Netlify prod env, so the ~804KB of reCAPTCHA Enterprise shipped to every visitor was decorative: a plain `curl` with no token got a 200 from `/api/claude`. That left the anon per-IP counter in `claude.mjs` (5/min, 15/hour, 30/day) as the only thing between a script and the Anthropic bill, which is why the 2026-05-18 audit had to set it so tight. But an IP is not a person: at ~5 requests per free round that was **three anonymous visitors per address per hour**, so the fourth person on a school, campus, or CGNAT mobile carrier was told they had hit a "free-trial cap" they had never used. `realtime-session.mjs` hit the identical wall on 2026-07-28 and fixed it by moving NAMED users to per-UID metering; the anonymous lane has no such escape hatch, so the ceiling itself had to move. **Sequence, which mattered:** wire every calling surface first, verify tokens mint, then flip. **Two findings made that non-optional.** (1) `tts.mjs` and `realtime-session.mjs` call `checkAppCheck` at the top of the handler, *before* identifying the caller, so they need a token from signed-in users too — and `live-round.html` called both (plus transcribe and claude) with no App Check wired at all. Flipping against that tree would have killed every live round and all voice. `coach.html`, `live.html` and `high-school.html` were in the same state (high-school had no Firebase on the page whatsoever), and `pricing.html` loaded `app-check.js` without ever loading the App Check SDK, so its wiring was inert. (2) **The SDK injection race:** six client modules (`track`, `usage-banner`, `upgrade-cta`, `topbar`, `notifications`, `auth-modal`) each lazily inject `firebase-app-compat` when they find no firebase on the page. Whichever injection lands last owns `window.firebase` and takes the `appCheck` component with it, so a static `app-check-compat` tag registered against an earlier namespace simply vanishes. Measured on `/high-school`: two `app-compat` tags raced and `firebase.appCheck` came out undefined while every script tag looked correct. `app-check.js` now loads the App Check SDK itself at activation time, after the injectors settle. **Perf rider:** `activate()` is what pulls the 804KB bundle, so activation moved to first intent (pointer/key/touch) or the first gated fetch.
