A non-gated route attaches a token only if App Check is already up and never triggers activation, so public polls like `/api/watch-live` cannot drag it back onto page load. **New numbers:** `claude.mjs` 5/min → 30, 15/hour → 200, 30/day → 800; `realtime-session` anon 6/hour → 15, 10/day → 40 (voice rises less; it costs more per call, and named accounts keep the per-UID lane). Signed-in text callers are metered per uid and never touch these. **Verified on prod:** tokenless `curl` → 401 `APP_CHECK_MISSING`; landing, practice, voice-debate, high-school, coach, live-round and live all mint a valid 957-char token and pass every gated endpoint; 12 rapid anonymous calls that would have died at 6 now all return 200. **Tuning note:** there is no `UPSTASH_*` in the prod env, so both limiters run the in-memory fallback — counters are per function instance, making the effective ceiling both higher than the numbers suggest and unpredictable, since a cold instance starts from zero. Reverting enforcement is one env var plus a redeploy.

- **Homepage weight: animation governor, and a Firestore listener per visitor removed** (2026-08-18, same pass). The landing ran ~120 infinite CSS animations; unlike rAF, keyframes keep painting in a backgrounded tab and for sections nobody has scrolled to. New `app/js/anim-governor.js` (landing + index + practice) pauses animation on a hidden document and on off-screen sections, un-pausing 300px early so entrances still play; `animation-play-state` resumes in place, so nothing restarts. Separately, the "N debates live now" band opened a `live_rounds` `onSnapshot` on **every** visitor, lazy-loading firestore-compat and holding an open realtime listener per person just to print a count — a few hundred concurrent visitors is a few hundred listeners fanning out on every write. It now reads `/api/watch-live` off its 12s shared server cache under the same ~100s liveness rule, and Firestore loads only when someone clicks Join. Three blocks on the page had also each been polling that endpoint on their own timer; they now share one in-flight promise and a 45s window, which incidentally stops the hero count and the band from disagreeing. Landing heap 33.5MB → 21.4MB.

- **The Open: prize entry $20 → $5, and $5 becomes the real door** (2026-08-19, later pass). The $20 prize entry had earned nothing: eight days after entries opened, `paidEntries` was 0 against a field of 7. The fix earlier the same day widened the automatic founding comp from Friday Aug 22 out to entries close on Aug 29, on the correct reasoning that a $20 wall with "email the organizer and ask" as the only way past it is not a wall a stranger climbs during a ten-day bracket-fill push. But that argument was about $20, not about paying at all, and it left the event with a free door for everyone and no revenue path. **$5 is a door a stranger actually walks through**, so the blanket comp had nothing left to protect and the headline it contradicted became true. Aidan's call, given three options: $5 real door, comp preserved for accounts that already held it. **The comp is stopped, not retracted** — `FOUNDING_CUTOFF_MS` moves Aug 29 → Aug 19 end-of-day EDT, so the seven entries already on the field, the six existing `compedEntries`, and everyone the announcement email reached keep full prize eligibility at no cost; only accounts created after today meet the $5 door, and the fee is still waived on request for anyone who asks. The pot is untouched at $850 ($500/$250/$100): prizes are funded by the organizer, not raised from entry fees, so the ladder stands whether one person pays or fifty. **Stripe needed no work** — `sk_live_`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `ENTRY_PAYMENTS_LIVE=true` were already set in the Netlify env, and `entry-checkout.mjs` / the `tournament_entry` webhook branch / `tournaments/{tid}/payments/{uid}` were all already built. This was a price change, not an integration. `entryFeeCents` 2000 → 500 written directly to the live doc, which surfaced a real gap: the fee was designed as data rather than code precisely so pricing is not a deploy, but nothing could **write** it, so `tournament-admin`'s `update` action gained a site-admin-only `entryFeeCents` (clamped 0–50000; 0 turns the paid door off) and the price is now changeable without a raw Firestore edit. **Copy swept to $5** across `tournament-rules.html` (the published contract for a cash prize, and the one that actually matters), `tournaments.html` (both JSON-LD offers, the hero kicker, the logistics card, the enter head and the two FAQ answers), `get-paid-to-debate.html` including its FAQPage JSON-LD, and the three announcement emails. The /tournaments paid door now leads with "Pay in · $5" as a visible primary button instead of hiding it behind a failed comp claim, with the claim path demoted to the secondary action; the 2026-08-10 lesson that a door must never be hidden by a JS branch on a data value still holds, and both doors still render unconditionally. **Promo, per Aidan, on the landing and sitewide:** a parallel `js/open-promo.js` strip was built for the first screen and then **deleted before shipping** — `origin/main` landed `.fs-open-card` in the same slot mid-branch (504e63bf), so the shipped card was updated to $5 rather than competing with it. The surviving change is the sitewide `topbar.js` ribbon, which said "Free to enter" on every page and was made false by the price cut; it now leads "Win $500 for $5" and keeps everything past the hook in the `.ui-open-strip-tail` span that hides under 560px, because `body.has-beta-strip` reserves a fixed 32px while the bar becomes 48px the moment it wraps, clipping the wordmark beneath it. That clipping is pre-existing and unfixed: the 32px is load-bearing for four other rules in `ui.css`, so resizing it is its own chrome pass. **Riders:** `admin-open-reminder` is a deadline email again (free entry genuinely ends tonight), and its own `CUTOFF_PASSED` guard makes it unsendable from tomorrow, which is correct rather than a regression.

- **The first screen becomes one composition, and the headline comes back** (2026-08-19, latest pass, commit `697c87a5`). Aidan supplied a positioning brief off a 12:25 screenshot (`Codex/2026-08-19/revie/outputs/CLAUDE_SIMPLE_UI_POSITIONING_INSTRUCTIONS.md`): the hero had good parts arranged as five separate floating objects. **This pass deliberately reverses several decisions made earlier the same day, which is the point of it, not an accident** — a future agent reading those entries should read this one first. **The visible `<h1>` is restored.** It had been cut to screen-reader-only that morning on the reasoning that the two CTAs plus the globe could carry the first screen; what it actually produced was a column opening on a button row with nothing anchoring it, a centered CTA axis fighting a left-ragged everything-else, and a background constellation promoted to full opacity to supply the texture the headline used to. All three of those come back out together. **One grid:** `min(100% - 64px, 1400px)` centered, `minmax(0,5fr) minmax(0,7fr)`, copy capped 560 and board capped 760, so surplus width on a wide monitor lands outside the composition rather than pushing the two columns toward opposite edges. Headline, supporting line, actions, walkthrough and the live row now share one left edge. **The CTAs are buttons again** (54px tall, 14px radius, content-sized, 12px apart) rather than the 23px-padded full pills the headline's absence justified; the "two equal doors, colour alone says which leads" rule from that morning survives on height, while Debate takes back sole ownership of large-red — verified by measuring painted backgrounds in the first viewport, not by eye. The walkthrough drops to a quiet text link and loses its pinging red ring, which was a third pulsing red object competing for the same glance. The globe stops being a third focal point: 150px with the count beside it as one component instead of 330px with a caption floating loose underneath. The example board keeps its content and loses its nesting — pager moved from a header to a quiet footer so the card reads people, motion, audience result, verdict, then index; one radius, one shadow, 28px padding, name labels as compact overlays rather than capsules. **The constellation stays at full strength** (that half of the morning's change was about the whole page, not the hero) but stops crossing type at contrast, via a `var(--bg)` scrim on the copy column that tracks all five themes rather than a global dimming.

  - **The in-hero Debatable Open card came off, which updates the "$20 → $5" entry above.** That entry records `.fs-open-card` as the surviving promo in this slot. It is gone. The sequence in one afternoon: the card was added because / had no path to the event (274 arrivals, 18 reaching /tournaments), repriced to $5, then had its fine print moved to /tournaments — and at that point the card and the sitewide strip stated identical facts (same event, same date, same $500 for $5, same link to the same page). Two promotions of one event on one screen with nothing unique between them is the duplication the brief was written to remove. **The sitewide `topbar.js` ribbon is now the single path from / to the Open**, and it retires on the same schedule the card would have.

  - **The strip's wrap-clipping is FIXED, which corrects the "pre-existing and unfixed" line in the entry above.** That entry is right that `body.has-beta-strip`'s 32px is load-bearing for four other rules in `ui.css` and that resizing it is its own chrome pass. So the 32px was not touched. Instead the strip is pinned to one line (`white-space:nowrap; overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis`) with size steps at 560px and 380px, so it can never become the 48px bar that drew over the wordmark. This composes with, rather than replaces, the `.ui-open-strip-tail` hide under 560px: the tail keeps the message short, the nowrap guarantees the height whatever the copy later becomes. Clipping the tail is the lesser failure, since the strip is a link to /tournaments rather than the event page itself. Same pass took the strip's 32px out of the first screen's `100svh` (`body.has-beta-strip .fscreen`), which had always ended exactly that far below the fold; the class is removed on dismiss, so the full height returns with the space.

  - **Not done, deliberately: the brief's navigation section.** It asks for one control height (40-44px), text nav that reads as text rather than pills, square equal icon controls, and a profile control that does not pair a truncated name with a separate label. The topbar is shared chrome across 63 pages in `js/topbar.js`, and its signed-in state cannot be reproduced on a local static server without Firebase auth, so it was left rather than restructured against a screenshot that could not be verified. It is its own pass.

  - **Process note worth keeping, because it will recur.** Five commits landed on this same hero from other sessions while this one was in a worktree, so the branch rebased four times and two of its own conclusions were overturned by facts that arrived mid-flight. One of those commits (`503b4ec6`, "open up the vertical rhythm in the hero's left stack") sized nodes this pass restructured; its intent (generous left-stack gaps with a short-viewport wind-back) is carried forward and its literal values are superseded. When the landing is under this much concurrent edit, re-read `origin/main` before resolving anything, and prefer taking the newer *reasoning* over the newer *diff*.

- **The walkthrough is a link, not a chapter** (2026-08-25, Aidan). The large poster card and its standalone `#live-now` chapter came off the landing. The same three-minute video remains available through a quiet text trigger in the link row at the end of `#live-proof`, directly above the round CTAs. Its lightbox, poster and video asset stay unchanged. The live-room status pill that used the removed chapter as its injection point now lands with those round CTAs instead, so removing the card does not remove the live signal. The tour navigation and one-pager gate no longer advertise or target the retired chapter.

- **Spar gets a player-build match profile** (2026-08-27, Aidan; expanded the same day). The political-stranger idea becomes a seven-pick editorial arcade flow before `/spar` writes a queue doc: debate background, self-chosen political family, one hot-take headline, whether the person wants random topics or subjects they care about, matching rule, whether they want to be asked about public clips after the round, and optional writing help from their own AI chat. The whole questionnaire has an explicit Skip action, and both political questions have their own skip choice. The full-screen build uses a distinct paper-and-ink Match Desk with large serif questions, restrained italics, oxblood/blue sides and a meaningful print-style diagram on every step; the masthead carries the real current queue count, and the waiting screen keeps the result as a compact loadout card with a Change button. The AI-writing option only builds a local clipboard prompt that asks three follow-up questions before drafting a short first-person summary; it calls no provider from Debatable, uploads no summary, and tells the model not to invent or intensify beliefs. Three matching rules are real: First available preserves queue order; Closest rating reads the public Glicko ladder and minimizes rating distance; Viewpoint clash prioritizes another person who also explicitly asked to be challenged, then the in-room motion draft puts them on opposite sides. **Privacy limit:** the political family and hot take never leave localStorage. The queue receives only `matchMode`, a neutral preference, because `matchmaking_queue` is readable by every signed-in client and is not an acceptable place to publish political opinions. Clip intent also stays local and is not recording consent. The existing all-party in-round recording choice remains the only path to capture or publish a replay.

- **The Debatable Open publishes twenty motions and locks its competitive roster at the start** (2026-08-27, Aidan; supersedes the entry-open portions of the 2026-08-18 Open decisions above). Every tournament-day motion comes from the twenty-motion pool on `/tournaments`. Each one-on-one room draws three from that pool, both sides secretly strike one, and the existing seeded resolution handles an overlapping strike: one side calls the motion from the two survivors and the other calls its side. The draft profile is written by the pairing server into `round_drafts/{room}`; the browser cannot substitute a pool or change the counts. Starting the day now means two things in the same `registration` to `running` transition: the participant queue opens and the roster locks. People already registered may arrive later during prelims, but a new account cannot enter after that transition. Reading the event, draw, tab and room remains public. Joining a tournament room does not trust a client-supplied role: pairing also writes `room_admissions/{room}`, Daily creates the room as private, assigned roster UIDs receive participant tokens, and everyone else may receive only a public, receive-only viewer token. Missing admission fails closed for tournament-shaped rooms. This deliberately reverses the earlier drop-in promise that registration and running remain open together; the founder's explicit requirement is now a private competitive event that is publicly spectatorable.

- **Live video becomes a Google signup door, with people around it** (2026-08-27, Aidan: "require google sign in to use the video feature", "say 12 live", and "dont allow 1 round anonymous"). This supersedes the 2026-08-24 one-guest-round reopening for `/spar`. The client allowance and server default are both zero, `matchmaking_queue` accepts new docs only from an active `google.com` token, `spar-pair` repeats that check before it can open a human room, and the background "Spar live" pill admits the same provider. Email and password remain valid account paths elsewhere; the restriction is the human video feature. The signed-out card has one action, Google, and the email, Discord, anonymous-mask, and AI escape paths are absent from that card so the conversion ask is not competing with itself. Ten surrounding frames make the screen feel occupied: the existing 18+ consented still bank, face46-49 and face51-54, plus the founder-supplied Sydney and Sofia screenshots, which he confirmed are fake the same day. The two fictional tiles belong to this atmosphere layer, not the ROUNDS board or any real round record. The gate says **12 live now** by founder call even when the raw queue is lower; that fixed display is confined to the signed-out conversion surface, while matchmaking, presence, and watch surfaces keep their real counts.

---

*Last updated: 2026-08-27 (latest passes: The Debatable Open locks its roster and publishes its motion pool; /spar live video requires Google up front with the founder-called 12-live signup display)*
